1	"""``drain-cycle`` CLI entry point.
2	
3	Zero-arg invocation drains the current Linear cycle. Each issue's target
4	repo is resolved from a ``repo:<name>`` label against
5	``~/.drain-cycle/repos.yml``; the operator runs ``drain-cycle`` from
6	anywhere, not from inside a target repo. The ``grade`` subcommand reads
7	the run logs and prints a health read.
8	
9	Secrets load before any module reads ``os.environ``, first hit wins:
10	shell-exported vars → ``~/.drain-cycle/.env`` → the drain-cycle repo
11	root ``.env`` (dev-checkout fallback, absent once installed as a uv
12	tool). ``load_dotenv`` defaults to ``override=False``, so an
13	already-set var always beats a later source and the shell always wins.
14	
15	``repos.yml`` and the optional ``limits.yml`` are validated eagerly at
16	startup so a broken config halts exit 1 on stderr before any Linear
17	traffic or run-log file is written — there is no cycle yet to log
18	against. ``limits.yml`` is optional (its absence yields the baked-in
19	guardrail defaults); a present-but-malformed one still halts.
20	"""
21	from __future__ import annotations
22	
23	import sys
24	from pathlib import Path
25	
26	from dotenv import load_dotenv
27	
28	from . import grade, grade_draft, limits, orchestrator, repos, telemetry
29	
30	_REPO_ENV = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent / ".env"
31	
32	
33	def _load_secrets() -> None:
34	    """Populate ``os.environ`` from the first ``.env`` that defines each key.
35	
36	    ``~/.drain-cycle/.env`` sits beside ``repos.yml`` and the run logs,
37	    so an installed tool finds its secret there. The repo-root ``.env``
38	    is a dev-checkout fallback only — once installed as a uv tool the
39	    package lives in an isolated env where that path has no ``.env``.
40	    ``$HOME`` is resolved per call so tests can redirect it.
41	    """
42	    load_dotenv(Path.home() / ".drain-cycle" / ".env")
43	    load_dotenv(_REPO_ENV)
44	
45	
46	_USAGE = (
47	    "usage: drain-cycle [--watch|-w] [--no-stack]  drain the current Linear cycle\n"
48	    "       drain-cycle grade                       print health read from run logs\n"
49	    "       drain-cycle grade-draft <issue>         write per-ticket grade draft\n"
50	    "       drain-cycle status                      show status of the active run\n"
51	    "       drain-cycle --help\n"
52	    "\n"
53	    "options:\n"
54	    "  --watch, -w      open a tmux split-pane per issue running the live\n"
55	    "                   claude session (requires running inside tmux)\n"
56	    "  --no-stack       push to main instead of stacking PRs (default: stack)"
57	)
58	
59	_WATCH_FLAGS = frozenset(["--watch", "-w"])
60	_NO_STACK_FLAGS = frozenset(["--no-stack"])
61	
62	
63	def main() -> None:
64	    import os
65	
66	    _load_secrets()
67	    # Telemetry reads its key from the environment just populated above; a
68	    # no-op when HONEYCOMB_API_KEY is unset, so an unconfigured run is
69	    # unchanged. Registers its own atexit flush, hence no teardown here.
70	    telemetry.setup()
71	    argv = sys.argv[1:]
72	
73	    # Drain invocation: zero or more flags, no subcommand.
74	    # Currently --watch/-w and --no-stack are recognised.
75	    remaining = [a for a in argv if a not in _WATCH_FLAGS and a not in _NO_STACK_FLAGS]
76	    watch = any(a in _WATCH_FLAGS for a in argv)
77	    no_stack = any(a in _NO_STACK_FLAGS for a in argv)
78	
79	    if not remaining:
80	        if watch and not os.environ.get("TMUX"):
81	            print(
82	                "drain-cycle: --watch ignored: $TMUX not set — run inside tmux to "
83	                "watch the live session; falling back to normal execution",
84	                file=sys.stderr,
85	            )
86	        try:
87	            loaded_repos = repos.load()
88	            loaded_limits = limits.load()
89	        except (repos.RepoConfigError, limits.LimitsConfigError) as exc:
90	            print(f"drain-cycle: {exc}", file=sys.stderr)
91	            sys.exit(1)
92	        sys.exit(orchestrator.run(loaded_repos, loaded_limits, watch=watch, no_stack=no_stack))
93	
94	    if remaining == ["grade"] and not watch:
95	        sys.exit(grade.run(grade.default_runs_dir()))
96	    if (
97	        len(remaining) == 2
98	        and remaining[0] == "grade-draft"
99	        and not watch
100	    ):
101	        sys.exit(grade_draft.run(remaining[1]))
102	    if remaining == ["status"] and not watch:
103	        from . import status
104	        sys.exit(status.run())
105	    if remaining == ["_stop-guard"] and not watch:
106	        from . import stop_guard
107	        sys.exit(stop_guard.run(sys.stdin, sys.stdout))
108	    if argv in (["-h"], ["--help"]):
109	        print(_USAGE)
110	        sys.exit(0)
111	    print(f"drain-cycle: unknown invocation: {' '.join(argv)}", file=sys.stderr)
112	    print(_USAGE, file=sys.stderr)
113	    sys.exit(2)
114	
```

1	"""Per-issue worker-model resolution.
2	
3	Workers default to a cost-efficient model. A spawned ``claude -p`` session
4	inherits whatever model the operator has globally pinned, which can be the
5	most expensive one — so unattended cycles default to Sonnet here and let an
6	individual issue opt up (or down) via a ``model:<alias>`` Linear label,
7	mirroring the ``repo:<name>`` mechanism in ``repos.py``.
8	
9	Resolution is deliberately lenient and never raises: a worker always has a
10	safe model to run, so any ambiguity falls back to the default rather than
11	halting an unattended cycle. The model actually used is recorded in the run
12	log, so a mis-labelled issue surfaces after the fact instead of stalling.
13	
14	* No ``model:`` label → ``DEFAULT_MODEL``.
15	* Exactly one label → its value, mapped through ``_ALIASES`` if known,
16	  otherwise passed through verbatim (``claude --model`` validates it).
17	* More than one label → ``DEFAULT_MODEL`` (ambiguous intent; the cheap,
18	  safe default wins).
19	"""
20	from __future__ import annotations
21	
22	from typing import Any
23	
24	_MODEL_LABEL_PREFIX = "model:"
25	
26	DEFAULT_MODEL = "claude-sonnet-4-6"
27	
28	_ALIASES = {
29	    "sonnet": "claude-sonnet-4-6",
30	    "opus": "claude-opus-4-7",
31	    "haiku": "claude-haiku-4-5-20251001",
32	}
33	
34	
35	def resolve(issue: dict[str, Any]) -> str:
36	    """Return the model the worker for ``issue`` should run on."""
37	    values = [
38	        label.removeprefix(_MODEL_LABEL_PREFIX)
39	        for label in issue.get("labels", [])
40	        if label.startswith(_MODEL_LABEL_PREFIX)
41	    ]
42	    if len(values) != 1:
43	        return DEFAULT_MODEL
44	    requested = values[0]
45	    return _ALIASES.get(requested, requested)
46

1	"""Per-issue execution-flow resolution.
2	
3	The ``verify`` Linear label switches a drain from the default single-worker
4	flow to the verify pipeline (Task Shaper → Implementer → Outcome Verifier →
5	PR Preparer → PR Responder). Absence of the label keeps the default flow.
6	"""
7	from __future__ import annotations
8	
9	from typing import Any
10	
11	VERIFY = "verify"
12	"""Flow name for the multi-role verify pipeline."""
13	
14	_VERIFY_LABEL = "verify"
15	
16	
17	def resolve(issue: dict[str, Any]) -> str | None:
18	    """Return ``'verify'`` if the issue carries the verify label, else ``None``."""
19	    if _VERIFY_LABEL in issue.get("labels", []):
20	        return VERIFY
21	    return None
22

```
1	"""Drain a cycle by iterating over its sorted Todo/Backlog issues.
2	
3	Halt-on-not-Done, the orchestrator-owned Todo→In-Progress transition, the
4	run-log artefact, and the inspectable-halt UX all live here. The halt-message
5	helper ``_halt_message`` is the single source of truth for the operator-facing
6	halt string — emitted both on stderr and into the run-log entry's
7	``halt_reason`` field.
8	"""
9	from __future__ import annotations
10	
11	import fcntl
12	import json
13	import os
14	import select
15	import shlex
16	import shutil
17	import subprocess
18	import sys
19	import tempfile
20	from datetime import datetime, timezone
21	from pathlib import Path
22	from typing import Callable, TextIO
23	
24	from opentelemetry.trace import Span
25	
26	from . import console, flow, grade_draft, handoff, linear, model, progress, prompt, runlog, stop_guard, telemetry, worker, worktree
27	from .limits import Limits, check_cycle
28	from .linear import DependencyCycleError
29	from .repos import RepoResolutionError, Repos
30	
31	_DONE_STATE_TYPE = "completed"
32	_IN_PROGRESS_STATE_NAME = "In Progress"
33	_CLAUDE_CMD = ["claude", "-p", "--dangerously-skip-permissions"]
34	_DEBUG_ENV_VAR = "DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG"
35	"""Opt-in switch for per-issue ``--debug-file`` capture. Any non-empty value
36	turns it on; the worker then writes each session's startup diagnostics
37	(settings sources, plugins, MCP servers, hooks) beside the run log. Off by
38	default — the diagnostic exists for one-shot investigation, not steady state.
39	See ``docs/design-decisions.md`` §10."""
40	_UNRESOLVED_WORKTREE_DISPLAY = "<unresolved>"
41	"""Worktree-path placeholder for the pre-spawn resolution-halt path.
42	No path has been chosen yet — the issue couldn't be mapped to a target
43	repo — so the run-log entry and stderr halt line carry this marker
44	rather than a misleading fake path."""
45	
46	
47	def _now_iso() -> str:
48	    return datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat()
49	
50	
51	def _emit_summary(log: runlog.RunLog, *, total: int, halted_on: str | None) -> None:
52	    done = sum(1 for e in log.entries if e.get("final_linear_state") == "Done")
53	    next_steps = [] if halted_on is None else [
54	        "inspect the run log for halt context",
55	        "fix and re-run drain-cycle to resume",
56	    ]
57	    console.completion_summary(
58	        issues_done=done,
59	        issues_total=total,
60	        halted_on=halted_on,
61	        cost_usd=log.cycle_cost_usd() or None,
62	        tokens=log.cycle_tokens_cumulative(),
63	        elapsed_seconds=log.cycle_duration_seconds(),
64	        run_log_path=str(log.path),
65	        next_steps=next_steps,
66	    )
67	
68	
69	_WATCH_FIFO_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 10.0
70	"""How long ``_open_fifo_stream`` waits for the pane's ``claude | tee`` to
71	produce its first bytes before giving up and falling back to a normal
72	subprocess spawn. The pane's ``claude`` emits its init event within seconds;
73	this bound only guards a pane that never started (tmux accepted the
74	split-window but the command died), so the drain never wedges on a dead FIFO."""
75	
76	
77	def _watch_formatter_stage() -> str:
78	    """The pane-visible pipe stage that renders stream-json human-readable.
79	
80	    ``<python> -u -m drain_cycle.watch_format`` reads the operator's copy and
81	    writes formatted activity; ``-u`` keeps it unbuffered so the pane updates
82	    live. ``sys.executable`` is the interpreter drain-cycle itself runs under,
83	    so for the editable uv-tool install it is the venv that has ``drain_cycle``
84	    importable.
85	
86	    The whole thing is a brace group with ``|| cat`` so the FIFO branch stays
87	    truly independent of the formatter. A bare ``tee <fifo> | formatter`` is
88	    *not* independent: if the formatter can't launch or dies, ``tee`` takes
89	    SIGPIPE on its stdout and exits, closing the FIFO write end and truncating
90	    drain-cycle's parse. The brace-group subshell holds the pipe's read end
91	    open across the formatter→``cat`` transition, so ``tee`` never sees a
92	    readerless pipe — worst case the pane falls back to raw JSON via ``cat``.
93	    """
94	    py = shlex.quote(sys.executable)
95	    return f"{{ {py} -u -m drain_cycle.watch_format || cat; }}"
96	
97	
98	def _open_watch_pane(argv: list[str], cwd: Path) -> tuple[str, Path] | None:
99	    """Open a tmux split-pane running ``argv`` piped through ``tee`` into a FIFO.
100	
101	    The pane *is* the ``claude`` session: ``argv | tee <fifo> | <formatter>``
102	    splits the stream — the FIFO branch carries byte-for-byte stream-json to
103	    drain-cycle's reader, while the pane copy flows through the formatter (see
104	    ``_watch_formatter_stage``) so the operator sees human-readable activity
105	    rather than raw JSON. ``exec ${SHELL}`` after the pipeline keeps the pane
106	    alive for scrollback once ``claude`` exits (``tee`` still closes the FIFO
107	    write end, so the reader reaches EOF).
108	
109	    ``split-window -P -F "#{pane_id}"`` prints the new pane's ID directly so we
110	    capture the session pane, not the operator's active pane; ``-c`` runs it in
111	    the issue's worktree. Returns ``(pane_id, fifo_path)``, or ``None`` if the
112	    FIFO or pane could not be created — every failure (tmux not on PATH,
113	    non-zero exit, any OS error) is swallowed so a broken tmux environment
114	    falls back to a normal spawn rather than crashing the drain.
115	    """
116	    try:
117	        fifo_path = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="drain-watch-")) / "stream.fifo"
118	        os.mkfifo(fifo_path)
119	    except OSError:
120	        return None
121	    pipeline = (
122	        " ".join(shlex.quote(a) for a in argv)
123	        + f" | tee {shlex.quote(str(fifo_path))}"
124	        + f" | {_watch_formatter_stage()}"
125	        + "; exec ${SHELL:-/bin/sh}"
126	    )
127	    try:
128	        result = subprocess.run(
129	            [
130	                "tmux", "split-window", "-d",
131	                "-P", "-F", "#{pane_id}",
132	                "-c", str(cwd),
133	                pipeline,
134	            ],
135	            check=True,
136	            capture_output=True,
137	            text=True,
138	        )
139	        pane_id = result.stdout.strip()
140	    except Exception:
141	        _cleanup_fifo(fifo_path)
142	        return None
143	    if not pane_id:
144	        _cleanup_fifo(fifo_path)
145	        return None
146	    return pane_id, fifo_path
147	
148	
149	def _open_fifo_stream(fifo_path: Path, timeout: float) -> TextIO | None:
150	    """Open ``fifo_path`` for reading, waiting up to ``timeout`` for a writer.
151	
152	    The FIFO is opened non-blocking so a pane that never started can't wedge
153	    the drain; ``select`` then waits for the first bytes (``tee`` writing the
154	    pane's first stream-json line). Once readable, ``O_NONBLOCK`` is cleared so
155	    the reader thread's line iteration blocks normally until EOF. Returns a
156	    line-buffered text stream, or ``None`` if no writer/data appeared in time
157	    (the caller then tears down the pane and falls back to a normal spawn).
158	    """
159	    try:
160	        fd = os.open(fifo_path, os.O_RDONLY | os.O_NONBLOCK)
161	    except OSError:
162	        return None
163	    readable, _, _ = select.select([fd], [], [], timeout)
164	    if not readable:
165	        os.close(fd)
166	        return None
167	    flags = fcntl.fcntl(fd, fcntl.F_GETFL)
168	    fcntl.fcntl(fd, fcntl.F_SETFL, flags & ~os.O_NONBLOCK)
169	    try:
170	        return os.fdopen(fd, "r", buffering=1)
171	    except OSError:
172	        os.close(fd)
173	        return None
174	
175	
176	def _cleanup_fifo(fifo_path: Path) -> None:
177	    """Remove the FIFO and its temp dir; swallows all errors."""
178	    shutil.rmtree(fifo_path.parent, ignore_errors=True)
179	
180	
181	def _close_watch_pane(pane_id: str) -> None:
182	    """Kill a tmux pane by ID; swallows all errors."""
183	    try:
184	        subprocess.run(
185	            ["tmux", "kill-pane", "-t", pane_id],
186	            check=False,
187	            capture_output=True,
188	        )
189	    except Exception:
190	        pass
191	
192	
193	def _halt_message(identifier: str, state_name: str, worktree_path: Path) -> str:
194	    """Single source of truth for the halt UX.
195	
196	    The same string lands on stderr (the operator's grep anchor) and in
197	    the run-log entry's ``halt_reason`` field — so kill-condition tooling
198	    reads the same human-readable explanation the operator saw at halt
199	    time.
200	    """
201	    return f"Halt: {identifier} (final state: {state_name}) at {worktree_path}"
202	
203	
204	def _resume_attempts(cycle_id: str, identifier: str) -> int:
205	    """Count prior halted attempts for ``identifier`` across this cycle's run logs.
206	
207	    Globs ``~/.drain-cycle/runs/<cycle_id>-*.json`` and tallies entries
208	    whose ``issue_identifier`` matches and whose ``final_linear_state``
209	    is not ``"Done"`` — the same shape every halt path writes. The
210	    orchestrator compares this against ``limits.max_resume_attempts``
211	    before spawning, so a perma-stuck issue stops consuming attempts
212	    once its budget is spent.
213	
214	    Unreadable, unparseable, or shape-corrupted log files are skipped
215	    rather than failed: a partial-write file from a SIGKILL'd earlier
216	    run, or a file whose ``entries`` is missing/null/non-list, must
217	    not pin the operator out of running the cycle. The shape checks
218	    are isinstance-guarded so a JSON file that parses but doesn't
219	    match :class:`RunLog`'s on-disk schema is treated as opaque rather
220	    than crashing the helper.
221	    """
222	    count = 0
223	    for path in runlog.runs_dir().glob(f"{cycle_id}-*.json"):
224	        try:
225	            payload = json.loads(path.read_text())
226	        except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError):
227	            continue
228	        if not isinstance(payload, dict):
229	            continue
230	        entries = payload.get("entries")
231	        if not isinstance(entries, list):
232	            continue
233	        for entry in entries:
234	            if not isinstance(entry, dict):
235	                continue
236	            if (
237	                entry.get("issue_identifier") == identifier
238	                and entry.get("final_linear_state") != "Done"
239	            ):
240	                count += 1
241	    return count
242	
243	
244	def _revert_to_pre_halt_state(
245	    issue_id: str, *, target_state_name: str, pre_revert_state_name: str
246	) -> tuple[str, str | None]:
247	    """Restore Linear state on halt; return ``(state_to_report, error_msg)``.
248	
249	    The orchestrator transitions issues Todo→In Progress before spawning the
250	    agent. When the run halts, that In-Progress flag leaves the issue outside
251	    ``_PENDING_STATE_TYPES`` so a re-run silently skips it. This helper
252	    reverses the transition and re-fetches to confirm.
253	
254	    On revert success, returns the refreshed state name and ``None``.
255	    On revert failure, returns ``pre_revert_state_name`` (the state the
256	    issue is actually still in, so the operator can find it) plus the
257	    exception message — non-fatal. A failed refresh after a
258	    successful revert falls back to ``target_state_name``, since we trust
259	    the mutation landed even if the read-back didn't.
260	    """
261	    try:
262	        linear.set_state(issue_id, target_state_name)
263	    except Exception as exc:
264	        return pre_revert_state_name, str(exc)
265	    try:
266	        refreshed = linear.get_issue(issue_id)
267	    except Exception:
268	        return target_state_name, None
269	    return refreshed["state"]["name"], None
270	
271	
272	def _worker_log_fields(result: worker.WorkerResult) -> dict[str, object]:
273	    """Map a ``WorkerResult`` onto the run-log entry's usage fields.
274	
275	    Shared by all three worker-backed ``append_entry`` calls (timeout
276	    halt, Done, not-Done halt) so the recorded usage shape can't drift
277	    between branches.
278	    """
279	    return {
280	        "duration_seconds": result.duration_seconds,
281	        "model": result.model,
282	        "usage": result.usage,
283	        "cost_usd": result.cost_usd,
284	        "num_turns": result.num_turns,
285	        "session_id": result.session_id,
286	        "is_error": result.is_error,
287	    }
288	
289	
290	def _debug_enabled() -> bool:
291	    """Whether per-issue ``--debug-file`` capture is switched on.
292	
293	    Read from the environment (``os.environ`` already carries any
294	    ``~/.drain-cycle/.env`` value loaded at CLI startup), so the operator
295	    turns it on for one investigative run with ``DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG=1
296	    drain-cycle`` and leaves it off otherwise.
297	    """
298	    return bool(os.environ.get(_DEBUG_ENV_VAR))
299	
300	
301	def run(
302	    repos: Repos,
303	    limits: Limits | None = None,
304	    *,
305	    watch: bool = False,
306	    no_stack: bool = False,
307	) -> int:
308	    """Drain the current cycle inside the ``drain.cycle`` root span.
309	
310	    The span wrapper is thin so the body keeps its shape; per-issue work nests
311	    under it via ``_drain_one_issue``'s ``drain.issue`` spans, and the Linear,
312	    worktree, and worker spans nest under those — yielding one trace per drain.
313	    """
314	    if limits is None:
315	        limits = Limits()
316	    with telemetry.tracer.start_as_current_span("drain.cycle") as cycle_span:
317	        return _run(repos, limits, cycle_span, watch=watch, no_stack=no_stack)
318	
319	
320	def _run(
321	    repos: Repos,
322	    limits: Limits,
323	    cycle_span: Span,
324	    *,
325	    watch: bool = False,
326	    no_stack: bool = False,
327	) -> int:
328	    debug = _debug_enabled()
329	    cycle_id = linear.current_cycle_id()
330	    cycle_span.set_attribute("drain.cycle_id", cycle_id)
331	    log = runlog.RunLog(cycle_id=cycle_id)
332	    try:
333	        plan = linear.pending_issues(cycle_id)
334	    except DependencyCycleError as exc:
335	        halt_reason = f"Halt: {exc}"
336	        log.set_cycle_halt(halt_reason)
337	        telemetry.mark_error(cycle_span, "err-dependency-cycle", halt_reason)
338	        console.halt(halt_reason)
339	        _emit_summary(log, total=0, halted_on=halt_reason)
340	        return 1
341	
342	    cycle_span.set_attribute("drain.issues_planned", len(plan.order))
343	    cycle_span.set_attribute("drain.issues_deferred", len(plan.deferred))
344	
345	    if not plan.order and not plan.deferred:
346	        cycle_span.set_attribute("drain.outcome", "nothing-to-do")
347	        console.orch(f"Cycle {cycle_id} has no Todo/Backlog issues — nothing to do.")
348	        return 0
349	
350	    console.startup_plan(
351	        cycle_id,
352	        [(i["identifier"], i["title"], model.resolve(i)) for i in plan.order],
353	    )
354	
355	    for deferred in plan.deferred:
356	        issue = deferred["issue"]
357	        blocker_id = deferred["blocker_identifier"]
358	        blocker_state = deferred["blocker_state_type"]
359	        console.orch(
360	            f"deferred {issue['identifier']} — blocked by {blocker_id} ({blocker_state})"
361	        )
362	
363	    if not plan.order:
364	        cycle_span.set_attribute("drain.outcome", "all-deferred")
365	        _emit_summary(log, total=0, halted_on=None)
366	        return 0
367	
368	    in_tmux = bool(os.environ.get("TMUX"))
369	    current_pane_id: str | None = None
370	
371	    # Per-repo base-branch baton for cross-issue stacking: maps a repo name
372	    # to the branch of the last issue that submitted PRs there, so the next
373	    # same-repo issue branches its worktree off that branch (a true Graphite
374	    # stack) instead of off ``main``. Mutated in place by each drained issue.
375	    repo_baton: dict[str, str] = {}
376	
377	    total = len(plan.order)
378	    for index, issue in enumerate(plan.order):
379	        # Kill the pane from the previous issue before opening one for this issue.
380	        if current_pane_id is not None:
381	            _close_watch_pane(current_pane_id)
382	            current_pane_id = None
383	
384	        halt_code, current_pane_id = _drain_one_issue(
385	            issue,
386	            index=index,
387	            total=total,
388	            repos=repos,
389	            limits=limits,
390	            log=log,
391	            cycle_id=cycle_id,
392	            debug=debug,
393	            watch=watch,
394	            in_tmux=in_tmux,
395	            no_stack=no_stack,
396	            repo_baton=repo_baton,
397	        )
398	        if halt_code is not None:
399	            _emit_summary(log, total=total, halted_on=issue["identifier"])
400	            return halt_code  # type: ignore[return-value]
401	
402	        # Cycle-wide circuit breaker: every issue may stay under its own
403	        # per-issue caps while their sum drains the quota. Check the
404	        # running totals (which now include the Done issue just finished)
405	        # before spawning the next one; on breach, stop the cycle.
406	        cycle_breach = check_cycle(
407	            limits,
408	            tokens=log.cycle_tokens_cumulative(),
409	            cost_usd=log.cycle_cost_usd(),
410	            seconds=log.cycle_duration_seconds(),
411	        )
412	        if cycle_breach is not None:
413	            halt_reason = f"Halt: {cycle_breach.describe()}"
414	            log.set_cycle_halt(halt_reason)
415	            telemetry.mark_error(cycle_span, "err-cycle-breach", halt_reason)
416	            console.halt(halt_reason)
417	            _emit_summary(log, total=total, halted_on=halt_reason)
418	            return 1
419	
420	    # Final pane is left open for scrollback (intentionally not killed here).
421	    cycle_span.set_attribute("drain.outcome", "drained")
422	    _emit_summary(log, total=total, halted_on=None)
423	    return 0
424	
425	
426	def _drain_one_issue(
427	    issue: dict,
428	    *,
429	    index: int,
430	    total: int,
431	    repos: Repos,
432	    limits: Limits,
433	    log: runlog.RunLog,
434	    cycle_id: str,
435	    debug: bool,
436	    watch: bool = False,
437	    in_tmux: bool = False,
438	    no_stack: bool = False,
439	    repo_baton: dict[str, str] | None = None,
440	) -> tuple[int | None, str | None]:
441	    """Drain a single issue end to end inside a ``drain.issue`` span.
442	
443	    Returns ``(halt_code, pane_id)`` where ``halt_code`` is ``None`` when the
444	    issue reached Done (the caller then runs the cycle-wide circuit breaker and
445	    proceeds to the next issue), or an exit code when the run must halt.
446	    ``pane_id`` is the tmux pane ID opened for this issue (or ``None``).
447	    Each halt path is also marked on the span with a static ``exception.slug``.
448	    """
449	    identifier = issue["identifier"]
450	    with telemetry.tracer.start_as_current_span("drain.issue") as issue_span:
451	        issue_span.set_attribute("issue.identifier", identifier)
452	        issue_span.set_attribute("issue.title", issue["title"])
453	        issue_span.set_attribute("issue.index", index + 1)
454	        issue_span.set_attribute("issue.total", total)
455	        console.worker_event(identifier, f"picked: {issue['title']}")
456	
457	        flow_name = flow.resolve(issue)
458	        if flow_name is not None:
459	            issue_span.set_attribute("issue.flow", flow_name)
460	            issue_span.set_attribute("issue.responder_run_count", 0)
461	
462	        started_at = _now_iso()
463	        is_verify = prompt.is_verify_flow(issue)
464	        issue_span.set_attribute("issue.verify_flow", is_verify)
465	        try:
466	            target_repo = repos.resolve(issue)
467	        except RepoResolutionError as exc:
468	            # Pre-spawn resolution halt: no Linear state was moved, so no
469	            # revert is attempted. The worktree path is the ``<unresolved>``
470	            # placeholder since no repo was chosen.
471	            state_name = issue["state"]["name"]
472	            halt_reason = (
473	                f"{_halt_message(identifier, state_name, Path(_UNRESOLVED_WORKTREE_DISPLAY))}"
474	                f" — {exc}"
475	            )
476	            log.append_entry(
477	                issue_identifier=identifier,
478	                started_at=started_at,
479	                finished_at=_now_iso(),
480	                exit_code=-1,
481	                final_linear_state=state_name,
482	                REDACTED,
483	                halt_reason=halt_reason,
484	                flow=flow_name,
485	                shape_task_invoked=is_verify,
486	            )
487	            issue_span.set_attribute("issue.final_linear_state", state_name)
488	            telemetry.mark_error(issue_span, "err-repo-resolution", halt_reason)
489	            console.halt(halt_reason)
490	            return 1, None
491	
492	        issue_span.set_attribute("issue.repo", target_repo.name)
493	
494	        # Resume-attempt cap fires BEFORE any worktree manipulation or
495	        # Linear state change: a no-spawn refusal must leave the issue
496	        # exactly where it was so a future re-run (after the operator
497	        # raises the cap, clears prior runs, or finishes the work by
498	        # hand) can pick up cleanly. The cap is a *policy* knob, not a
499	        # runtime guardrail — no Breach is raised; see limits.py.
500	        #
501	        # ``max_resume_attempts=N`` allows up to N resumes after the
502	        # initial attempt (one fresh + N resumes = N+1 total halts
503	        # before refusal), matching the convention of ``max_retries`` in
504	        # the stdlib's urllib3/requests world. The cap fires once
505	        # ``prior_halts`` (count of non-Done entries for this issue in
506	        # the cycle's run logs, written by *prior* runs since a halt
507	        # exits this run) exceeds N.
508	        if limits.max_resume_attempts is not None:
509	            prior_halts = _resume_attempts(cycle_id, identifier)
510	            if prior_halts > limits.max_resume_attempts:
511	                planned_path = target_repo / worktree.WORKTREE_DIR / identifier
512	                state_name = issue["state"]["name"]
513	                halt_reason = (
514	                    f"{_halt_message(identifier, state_name, planned_path)}"
515	                    f" — resume-attempt cap reached "
516	                    f"(all {limits.max_resume_attempts} resumes used); "
517	                    "raise max_resume_attempts, clear prior runs, or finish by hand"
518	                )
519	                log.append_entry(
520	                    issue_identifier=identifier,
521	                    started_at=started_at,
522	                    finished_at=_now_iso(),
523	                    exit_code=-1,
524	                    final_linear_state=state_name,
525	                    worktree_path=str(planned_path),
526	                    halt_reason=halt_reason,
527	                    flow=flow_name,
528	                    shape_task_invoked=is_verify,
529	                )
530	                issue_span.set_attribute("issue.final_linear_state", state_name)
531	                issue_span.set_attribute("issue.resumed", True)
532	                telemetry.mark_error(issue_span, "err-resume-cap", halt_reason)
533	                console.halt(halt_reason)
534	                return 1, None
535	
536	        stack = target_repo.name not in repos.push_to_main_repos and not no_stack
537	        baton = repo_baton if repo_baton is not None else {}
538	        # Chain onto the prior same-repo issue's branch only in stack mode;
539	        # push-mode issues always branch off ``main`` (they land on main, so
540	        # there is no stack to extend).
541	        base = baton.get(target_repo.name, worktree.BASE_BRANCH) if stack else worktree.BASE_BRANCH
542	        try:
543	            handle = worktree.ensure(target_repo, identifier, base)
544	            worktree_path = handle.path
545	            # A reused worktree keeps the branch it was originally forked from;
546	            # the in-memory baton is empty across runs, so recover the true
547	            # base from the worktree itself rather than the recomputed value.
548	            if handle.resumed:
549	                base = worktree.read_base(worktree_path)
550	            issue_span.set_attribute("issue.base_branch", base)
551	            # A worktree checks out only tracked files, so gitignored
552	            # project config (.claude/, .mcp.json) is absent. Symlink it in
553	            # so the worker loads the same settings/hooks/agents/skills/MCP
554	            # as an interactive session at the repo root.
555	            worktree.link_project_config(
556	                target_repo, worktree_path, repos.worktree_config_paths
557	            )
558	            # Orchestrator owns the Todo→In Progress half so the lifecycle
559	            # doesn't depend on the spawned agent's compliance. The agent
560	            # still owns the …→Done half via Linear MCP — see prompt.py tail.
561	            linear.set_state(issue["id"], _IN_PROGRESS_STATE_NAME)
562	        except Exception as exc:
563	            # Convert any pre-spawn failure into a recorded halt rather than
564	            # a traceback: write a run-log entry with the planned worktree
565	            # path, print the halt message, exit non-zero. Subsequent issues
566	            # are not attempted — same contract as a spawn-time halt.
567	            planned_path = target_repo / worktree.WORKTREE_DIR / identifier
568	            state_name = issue["state"]["name"]
569	            halt_reason = (
570	                f"{_halt_message(identifier, state_name, planned_path)}"
571	                f" — setup failed: {exc}"
572	            )
573	            log.append_entry(
574	                issue_identifier=identifier,
575	                started_at=started_at,
576	                finished_at=_now_iso(),
577	                exit_code=-1,
578	                final_linear_state=state_name,
579	                worktree_path=str(planned_path),
580	                halt_reason=halt_reason,
581	                flow=flow_name,
582	                shape_task_invoked=is_verify,
583	            )
584	            issue_span.set_attribute("issue.final_linear_state", state_name)
585	            telemetry.mark_error(issue_span, "err-setup-failed", halt_reason)
586	            console.halt(halt_reason)
587	            return 1, None
588	
589	        # Plant the stop-guard marker so the worker's Stop hook fires only
590	        # for drain sessions and knows which completion sequence to enforce.
591	        # Replaces any prior marker so a resumed worktree starts fresh.
592	        stop_guard.write_marker(worktree_path, mode="stack" if stack else "push")
593	
594	        agent_prompt = prompt.build(
595	            issue, worktree_path, resumed=handle.resumed, stack=stack, base=base
596	        )
597	        issue_span.set_attribute("issue.resumed", handle.resumed)
598	        worker_model = model.resolve(issue)
599	        issue_span.set_attribute("issue.model", worker_model)
600	
601	        debug_file = log.debug_path(identifier) if debug else None
602	        if debug_file is not None:
603	            console.worker_event(identifier, f"debug capture → {debug_file}")
604	
605	        # Watch mode: run claude inside a tmux pane (so the operator sees the
606	        # live session) and read its stream-json off a FIFO instead of a
607	        # subprocess pipe. If the pane or FIFO can't be brought up, fall back
608	        # to a normal subprocess spawn — the pane is a convenience, not a
609	        # requirement. ``pane_id`` is returned to the caller for lifecycle
610	        # management; ``external_stream``/``kill_fn`` steer the worker onto the
611	        # external path.
612	        pane_id: str | None = None
613	        fifo_path: Path | None = None
614	        external_stream: TextIO | None = None
615	        kill_fn: Callable[[], None] | None = None
616	        if watch and in_tmux:
617	            argv = worker.build_argv(
618	                _CLAUDE_CMD,
619	                model=worker_model,
620	                prompt=agent_prompt,
621	                cost_limit_usd=limits.per_issue_cost_usd,
622	                debug_file=debug_file,
623	            )
624	            opened = _open_watch_pane(argv, worktree_path)
625	            if opened is not None:
626	                pane_id, fifo_path = opened
627	                external_stream = _open_fifo_stream(
628	                    fifo_path, _WATCH_FIFO_TIMEOUT_SECONDS
629	                )
630	                if external_stream is None:
631	                    # Pane accepted but produced no output in time — tear it
632	                    # down so we don't double-spawn, then fall back.
633	                    _close_watch_pane(pane_id)
634	                    _cleanup_fifo(fifo_path)
635	                    pane_id = None
636	                    fifo_path = None
637	                else:
638	                    def kill_fn() -> None:
639	                        _close_watch_pane(pane_id)
640	
641	        marker: dict = {
642	            "pid": os.getpid(),
643	            "cycle_id": cycle_id,
644	            "run_log_path": str(log.path),
645	            "issue": {
646	                "identifier": identifier,
647	                "title": issue["title"],
648	                "repo": target_repo.name,
649	                "worktree_path": str(worktree_path),
650	            },
651	            "model": worker_model,
652	            "started_at": started_at,
653	            "index": index + 1,
654	            "total": total,
655	            "progress": {},
656	        }
657	        progress.write(marker)
658	
659	        def _make_on_progress(m: dict, ident: str):
660	            def _cb(
661	                turns: int,
662	                cumulative_tokens: int,
663	                peak_context_tokens: int,
664	                cost_usd: float | None,
665	                elapsed_seconds: float,
666	            ) -> None:
667	                m["progress"] = {
668	                    "turns": turns,
669	                    "cumulative_tokens": cumulative_tokens,
670	                    "peak_context_tokens": peak_context_tokens,
671	                    "cost_usd": cost_usd,
672	                    "elapsed_seconds": elapsed_seconds,
673	                    "last_event_at": _now_iso(),
674	                }
675	                progress.write(m)
676	                console.worker_event(
677	                    ident,
678	                    f"turn {turns} · {progress.fmt_tokens(cumulative_tokens)} tok"
679	                    f" (peak {progress.fmt_tokens(peak_context_tokens)})"
680	                    f" · {progress.fmt_elapsed(elapsed_seconds)}",
681	                )
682	            return _cb
683	
684	        # Verify-flow verdict and responder data — populated by M2+ roles.
685	        outcome_verdict: dict | None = None
686	        prep_verdict: dict | None = None
687	        responder_runs: list[dict] = []
688	
689	        try:
690	            result = worker.run_issue(
691	                claude_cmd=_CLAUDE_CMD,
692	                model=worker_model,
693	                prompt=agent_prompt,
694	                cwd=worktree_path,
695	                token_limit=limits.per_issue_tokens,
696	                time_limit_seconds=limits.per_issue_seconds,
697	                cost_limit_usd=limits.per_issue_cost_usd,
698	                debug_file=debug_file,
699	                external_stream=external_stream,
700	                kill_fn=kill_fn,
701	                on_progress=_make_on_progress(marker, identifier),
702	                passthrough=console.AgentSink(),
703	            )
704	        finally:
705	            progress.clear()
706	            if fifo_path is not None:
707	                _cleanup_fifo(fifo_path)
708	        finished_at = _now_iso()
709	
710	        if result.breach is not None:
711	            # The worker crossed a per-issue cap (tokens or time) and was
712	            # process-group killed (grandchildren reaped). Same revert + halt
713	            # contract as a not-Done exit, with the recorded usage of the
714	            # killed session and the breached cap named in the halt reason.
715	            original_state_name = issue["state"]["name"]
716	            effective_state, revert_error = _revert_to_pre_halt_state(
717	                issue["id"],
718	                target_state_name=original_state_name,
719	                pre_revert_state_name=_IN_PROGRESS_STATE_NAME,
720	            )
721	            halt_reason = (
722	                f"{_halt_message(identifier, effective_state, worktree_path)}"
723	                f" — {result.breach.describe()}"
724	            )
725	            if revert_error is not None:
726	                halt_reason += (
727	                    f"; revert to {original_state_name!r} failed: {revert_error}"
728	                )
729	            log.append_entry(
730	                issue_identifier=identifier,
731	                started_at=started_at,
732	                finished_at=finished_at,
733	                exit_code=result.exit_code,
734	                final_linear_state=effective_state,
735	                worktree_path=str(worktree_path),
736	                halt_reason=halt_reason,
737	                flow=flow_name,
738	                outcome_verdict=outcome_verdict,
739	                prep_verdict=prep_verdict,
740	                responder_runs=responder_runs,
741	                shape_task_invoked=is_verify,
742	                **_worker_log_fields(result),
743	            )
744	            issue_span.set_attribute("issue.exit_code", result.exit_code)
745	            issue_span.set_attribute("issue.final_linear_state", effective_state)
746	            if outcome_verdict is not None:
747	                issue_span.set_attribute("issue.outcome_verdict", outcome_verdict["result"])
748	            if prep_verdict is not None:
749	                issue_span.set_attribute("issue.prep_verdict", prep_verdict["result"])
750	            if flow_name is not None:
751	                issue_span.set_attribute("issue.responder_run_count", len(responder_runs))
752	            telemetry.mark_error(issue_span, "err-per-issue-breach", halt_reason)
753	            console.halt(halt_reason)
754	            return 1, pane_id
755	
756	        refreshed = linear.get_issue(issue["id"])
757	        post_spawn_state = refreshed["state"]["name"]
758	        is_done = refreshed["state"]["type"] == _DONE_STATE_TYPE
759	        issue_span.set_attribute("issue.exit_code", result.exit_code)
760	        issue_span.set_attribute("issue.is_done", is_done)
761	
762	        if is_done:
763	            # Stack-mode confirmation gate, read before teardown removes the
764	            # worktree. A Done issue in stack mode must have left a non-empty
765	            # ``pr_urls`` — that is the orchestrator's proof submission ran.
766	            # If it's missing, the worker marked Done without opening a PR:
767	            # revert + halt, preserve the worktree for inspection, and do NOT
768	            # extend the baton — the next same-repo issue must not stack onto a
769	            # branch that was never pushed. Push-mode issues have no stack to
770	            # extend and no handoff, so they bypass the gate entirely.
771	            submitted = handoff.read(worktree_path) if stack else None
772	            if stack and submitted is None:
773	                original_state_name = issue["state"]["name"]
774	                effective_state, revert_error = _revert_to_pre_halt_state(
775	                    issue["id"],
776	                    target_state_name=original_state_name,
777	                    pre_revert_state_name=post_spawn_state,
778	                )
779	                halt_reason = (
780	                    f"{_halt_message(identifier, effective_state, worktree_path)}"
781	                    " — marked Done but .drain-handoff.json has no submitted "
782	                    "pr_urls; stack chain halted"
783	                )
784	                if revert_error is not None:
785	                    halt_reason += (
786	                        f" — revert to {original_state_name!r} failed: {revert_error}"
787	                    )
788	                log.append_entry(
789	                    issue_identifier=identifier,
790	                    started_at=started_at,
791	                    finished_at=finished_at,
792	                    exit_code=result.exit_code,
793	                    final_linear_state=effective_state,
794	                    worktree_path=str(worktree_path),
795	                    halt_reason=halt_reason,
796	                    flow=flow_name,
797	                    outcome_verdict=outcome_verdict,
798	                    prep_verdict=prep_verdict,
799	                    responder_runs=responder_runs,
800	                    shape_task_invoked=is_verify,
801	                    **_worker_log_fields(result),
802	                )
803	                issue_span.set_attribute("issue.final_linear_state", effective_state)
804	                telemetry.mark_error(issue_span, "err-stack-no-prs", halt_reason)
805	                console.halt(halt_reason)
806	                return 1, pane_id
807	
808	            if submitted is not None:
809	                # Hand the baton to the next same-repo issue and record the
810	                # submitted PRs as the orchestrator's confirmation line.
811	                baton[target_repo.name] = identifier
812	                issue_span.set_attribute("issue.submitted_pr_count", len(submitted.pr_urls))
813	                console.worker_event(
814	                    identifier,
815	                    "submitted "
816	                    + ", ".join(pr.url for pr in submitted.pr_urls),
817	                )
818	
819	            issue_span.set_attribute("issue.final_linear_state", post_spawn_state)
820	
821	            remove_error: str | None = None
822	            try:
823	                worktree.merge_entire_sessions(worktree_path, target_repo)
824	                worktree.remove(target_repo, worktree_path)
825	            except RuntimeError as exc:
826	                remove_error = str(exc)
827	                issue_span.set_attribute("worktree.remove_error", remove_error)
828	                console.orch(f"{identifier}: worktree teardown failed: {exc}")
829	            # Append unconditionally for every attempted issue.
830	            log.append_entry(
831	                issue_identifier=identifier,
832	                started_at=started_at,
833	                finished_at=finished_at,
834	                exit_code=result.exit_code,
835	                final_linear_state=post_spawn_state,
836	                worktree_path=str(worktree_path),
837	                halt_reason=remove_error,
838	                flow=flow_name,
839	                outcome_verdict=outcome_verdict,
840	                prep_verdict=prep_verdict,
841	                responder_runs=responder_runs,
842	                shape_task_invoked=is_verify,
843	                **_worker_log_fields(result),
844	            )
845	            if outcome_verdict is not None:
846	                issue_span.set_attribute("issue.outcome_verdict", outcome_verdict["result"])
847	            if prep_verdict is not None:
848	                issue_span.set_attribute("issue.prep_verdict", prep_verdict["result"])
849	            if flow_name is not None:
850	                issue_span.set_attribute("issue.responder_run_count", len(responder_runs))
851	            try:
852	                draft_path = grade_draft.write_draft_from_entry(
853	                    identifier, log.entries[-1]
854	                )
855	                console.worker_event(identifier, f"grade draft → {draft_path}")
856	            except Exception as exc:
857	                console.orch(
858	                    f"{identifier}: grade-draft write failed (non-fatal): {exc}"
859	                )
860	            if remove_error is None:
861	                console.worker_event(identifier, "done; worktree removed")
862	            return None, pane_id
863	
864	        # Not-Done halt: revert to the pre-halt state so a re-run picks
865	        # this issue back up instead of silently skipping it.
866	        original_state_name = issue["state"]["name"]
867	        effective_state, revert_error = _revert_to_pre_halt_state(
868	            issue["id"],
869	            target_state_name=original_state_name,
870	            pre_revert_state_name=post_spawn_state,
871	        )
872	        halt_reason = _halt_message(identifier, effective_state, worktree_path)
873	        tripped = stop_guard.read_tripped(worktree_path)
874	        if tripped is not None:
875	            halt_reason += f" — {stop_guard.TRIPPED_HALT_REASON}: {tripped}"
876	        if revert_error is not None:
877	            halt_reason += (
878	                f" — revert to {original_state_name!r} failed: {revert_error}"
879	            )
880	        # halt_reason carries the same string also printed to stderr below
881	        # so the on-disk and terminal surfaces cannot drift.
882	        log.append_entry(
883	            issue_identifier=identifier,
884	            started_at=started_at,
885	            finished_at=finished_at,
886	            exit_code=result.exit_code,
887	            final_linear_state=effective_state,
888	            worktree_path=str(worktree_path),
889	            halt_reason=halt_reason,
890	            flow=flow_name,

892	            prep_verdict=prep_verdict,
893	            responder_runs=responder_runs,
894	            shape_task_invoked=is_verify,
895	            **_worker_log_fields(result),
896	        )
897	        issue_span.set_attribute("issue.final_linear_state", effective_state)
898	        if outcome_verdict is not None:
899	            issue_span.set_attribute("issue.outcome_verdict", outcome_verdict["result"])
900	        if prep_verdict is not None:

902	        if flow_name is not None:

1	"""Spawn a streaming ``claude -p`` worker for one issue and record its usage.
2	
3	The orchestrator owns the cycle loop and the Linear lifecycle; this module
4	owns one session: how it is launched, how its token usage is parsed off the
5	wire, and how it is force-terminated when it overruns the per-issue time cap.
6	Normally the session is a subprocess this module spawns. In watch mode the
7	orchestrator instead runs ``claude`` in a tmux pane and hands us an open
8	stream of the same stream-json (see ``external_stream`` on ``run_issue``);
9	the parser and breach monitor are identical, only the launch and kill
10	differ.
11	
12	**Why stream-json.** A bare ``claude -p`` prints only the final assistant
13	text; the run log then records exit code and wall-clock and nothing about
14	spend. ``--output-format stream-json`` (requires ``--verbose``) emits one
15	JSON event per line — per-turn ``assistant`` messages plus a terminal
16	``result`` — from which we reconstruct exactly what the session cost.
17	
18	**Token accounting.** Each ``assistant`` event carries ``message.usage``,
19	but the same assistant message is emitted *once per content block*
20	(thinking, text, tool_use) — every copy repeats the identical usage under
21	the identical ``message.id``. Summing per event therefore double- (or
22	triple-) counts a turn; we key by ``message.id`` so each turn's usage is
23	counted exactly once. ``cumulative`` is the sum across turns of all four
24	token components — the real billed-token figure, dominated in long
25	tool-use loops by cache reads re-paid every turn. ``peak_context`` is the
26	largest single-turn context (input + cache_read + cache_create), i.e. how
27	close the session came to the context-window ceiling.
28	
29	The terminal ``result`` event is authoritative for ``cost_usd``
30	(``total_cost_usd``), ``num_turns``, ``session_id`` and ``is_error``. Note
31	``result.usage`` is only the *final* turn's snapshot, not the session
32	total, so it is deliberately not used for the token figures — those come
33	from the accumulated per-turn stream, which is also what survives a kill
34	before any ``result`` arrives.
35	
36	**Guardrails — belt and suspenders.** ``per_issue_cost_usd`` is handed to
37	``claude`` as ``--max-budget-usd`` so the session self-terminates on cost
38	(the native belt). The token and time caps are the orchestrator's
39	suspenders: a poll loop watches the live cumulative-token tally and the
40	elapsed wall-clock, and SIGKILLs the session the moment either is crossed.
41	Any cap passed as ``None`` is not enforced; with both token and time caps
42	off the worker simply waits for the session to exit on its own. A crossed
43	cap is reported back as a ``Breach`` so the orchestrator can name it in the
44	halt entry. See ``limits.py`` for the thresholds and their precedence.
45	
46	**Process-group kill.** The worker is launched with
47	``start_new_session=True`` so it leads its own process group. On a breach
48	the whole group is SIGKILLed via ``os.killpg``, reaping grandchildren —
49	MCP servers, sub-agents — that a kill of the direct child alone would
50	orphan to keep burning tokens. SIGKILL (not a SIGTERM grace) is used
51	deliberately: a session being force-terminated past a guardrail has no
52	clean-shutdown work worth waiting for, and SIGKILL is the only signal a
53	misbehaving grandchild cannot ignore. Killing the whole group also closes
54	the stdout pipe those grandchildren inherited, which is what lets the
55	reader thread reach EOF instead of blocking forever.
56	"""

75	_STREAM_FLAGS = ["--verbose", "--output-format", "stream-json"]
76	"""Flags that switch ``claude -p`` into line-delimited JSON event mode.
77	``--verbose`` is required: without it ``stream-json`` emits only the
78	terminal ``result`` event, dropping the per-turn ``assistant`` usage we
79	accumulate."""
80	
81	_READER_JOIN_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 5.0
82	"""Bound on waiting for the stdout-reader thread to drain to EOF after the
83	process exits. The group SIGKILL closes every inherited write-end of the
84	pipe, so EOF normally arrives at once; the bound only guards the
85	pathological case of a group member that somehow escaped the kill, so the
86	worker returns with the usage it has rather than hanging."""
87	
88	_POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS = 1.0

90	elapsed wall-clock against the per-issue caps. A worry-free overshoot of at
91	most one interval before the kill lands; one second is responsive enough for
92	a session measured in minutes and cheap enough to poll."""
93	
94	_TOKEN_FIELDS = (

96	    "output_tokens",
97	    "cache_creation_input_tokens",

103	class WorkerResult:
104	    """Outcome of one spawned session, recorded into the run-log entry.

106	    ``exit_code`` is the process return code (negative when killed by a
107	    signal, e.g. ``-9`` on a guardrail SIGKILL). ``breach`` is the branch
108	    selector the orchestrator reads: ``None`` when the session ran to its
109	    own exit, otherwise the per-issue token or time cap that was crossed,
110	    carrying the cap and the value observed at kill time. ``usage`` carries
111	    the four token components plus ``cumulative`` and ``peak_context``.
112	    ``cost_usd`` / ``num_turns`` / ``session_id`` / ``is_error`` come from
113	    the terminal ``result`` event and are ``None`` when the session was
114	    killed before one arrived.
115	    """
116	
117	    exit_code: int
118	    breach: Breach | None
119	    duration_seconds: float
120	    model: str

124	    session_id: str | None

127	
128	def build_argv(
129	    claude_cmd: list[str],
130	    *,
131	    model: str,
132	    prompt: str,
133	    cost_limit_usd: float | None,
134	    debug_file: Path | None,
135	) -> list[str]:
136	    """Assemble the full ``claude -p`` argv for one session.
137	
138	    ``claude_cmd`` is the base argv (``["claude", "-p",
139	    "--dangerously-skip-permissions"]``); the streaming flags, ``--model``,
140	    ``--max-budget-usd`` (when ``cost_limit_usd`` is set), ``--debug-file``
141	    (when set), and the prompt are appended. The prompt is the trailing
142	    positional, so it must follow the value-taking ``--model`` /
143	    ``--max-budget-usd`` / ``--debug-file`` options, not sit between an option
144	    and its value.
145	
146	    Both spawn paths share this: the worker's own ``subprocess.Popen`` and the
147	    orchestrator's watch pane (which runs this argv under ``tee`` so the same
148	    bytes reach the operator's terminal and drain-cycle's reader).
149	    """
150	    argv = [*claude_cmd, *_STREAM_FLAGS, "--model", model]
151	    if cost_limit_usd is not None:
152	        argv += ["--max-budget-usd", f"{cost_limit_usd:g}"]
153	    if debug_file is not None:
154	        argv += ["--debug-file", str(debug_file)]
155	    argv.append(prompt)
156	    return argv
157	
158	
159	def run_issue(
160	    *,
161	    claude_cmd: list[str],
162	    model: str,
163	    prompt: str,
164	    cwd: Path,
165	    token_limit: int | None,
166	    time_limit_seconds: float | None,
167	    cost_limit_usd: float | None,
168	    passthrough: TextIO | None = None,
169	    debug_file: Path | None = None,
170	    poll_interval_seconds: float = _POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS,
171	    on_progress: Callable[[int, int, int, float | None, float], None] | None = None,
172	    external_stream: TextIO | None = None,
173	    kill_fn: Callable[[], None] | None = None,
174	) -> WorkerResult:
175	    """Run one streaming ``claude -p`` session and return its usage.
176	
177	    Two execution paths share the same usage parser, breach monitor, and
178	    result shape:
179	
180	    * **Spawned (default).** ``external_stream is None``: the worker builds
181	      the argv (see :func:`build_argv`) and launches ``claude`` in its own
182	      process group, reading the merged stdout/stderr pipe. On a token/time
183	      breach the whole group is SIGKILLed.
184	    * **External (watch mode).** ``external_stream`` is an already-open stream
185	      of the session's stream-json — drain-cycle reads it while the *same*
186	      bytes scroll in a tmux pane that owns the ``claude`` process. No
187	      subprocess is spawned here; on a breach ``kill_fn`` is called to stop
188	      the pane's process instead of a process-group kill, and ``exit_code`` is
189	      ``0`` (the pane's ``claude`` return code isn't observable from here — the
190	      breach and the result event's ``is_error`` carry the real outcome).
191	
192	    If a token/time cap is crossed the returned ``WorkerResult.breach`` names
193	    it; a limit passed as ``None`` is not enforced. ``cost_limit_usd`` is
194	    enforced by ``claude`` itself, not here.
195	
196	    Non-JSON lines on the stream (claude warnings, hook stderr) are written to
197	    ``passthrough`` (default ``sys.stderr``) so the operator still sees
198	    diagnostics; recognised JSON events are consumed by the usage parser.
199	
200	    ``debug_file``, when set, is passed to ``claude`` as ``--debug-file`` so
201	    the session writes its startup diagnostics — which settings sources,
202	    plugins, MCP servers, and hooks initialised — to that path. Opt-in only;
203	    ``None`` (the default) passes no flag. See ``docs/design-decisions.md``
204	    §10. (Ignored on the external path, where the orchestrator already baked
205	    it into the pane's argv.)
206	
207	    ``on_progress``, when set, is called from the reader thread after each
208	    new assistant turn with ``(turns, cumulative_tokens, peak_context_tokens,
209	    cost_usd, elapsed_seconds)``. Callers use this for live progress display
210	    and the active-run marker update.
211	    """
212	    with telemetry.tracer.start_as_current_span("drain.worker.session") as span:
213	        span.set_attribute("worker.model", model)
214	        sink = passthrough if passthrough is not None else sys.stderr
215	        accumulator = _UsageAccumulator()
216	        started = time.monotonic()
217	
218	        proc: subprocess.Popen[str] | None = None
219	        if external_stream is not None:
220	            stream: TextIO | None = external_stream
221	        else:
222	            argv = build_argv(
223	                claude_cmd,
224	                model=model,
225	                prompt=prompt,
226	                cost_limit_usd=cost_limit_usd,
227	                debug_file=debug_file,
228	            )
229	            proc = subprocess.Popen(
230	                argv,
231	                cwd=str(cwd),
232	                stdout=subprocess.PIPE,
233	                stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
234	                text=True,
235	                bufsize=1,
236	                start_new_session=True,
237	            )
238	            stream = proc.stdout
239	
240	        # Wrap on_progress to inject elapsed_seconds computed from the start time.
241	        _progress_cb: Callable[[int, int, int, float | None], None] | None = None
242	        if on_progress is not None:
243	            def _progress_cb(turns: int, cumulative: int, peak: int, cost_usd: float | None) -> None:
244	                elapsed = time.monotonic() - started
245	                on_progress(turns, cumulative, peak, cost_usd, elapsed)
246	
247	        reader = threading.Thread(
248	            target=_drain_stream,
249	            args=(stream, accumulator, sink, _progress_cb),
250	            daemon=True,
251	        )
252	        reader.start()
253	
254	        # The monitor needs to know when the session has finished. With a
255	        # spawned process that's ``proc.wait``; on the external path there is
256	        # no process, so completion is the reader reaching EOF on the stream.
257	        if proc is not None:
258	            def _wait(timeout: float | None) -> bool:
259	                try:
260	                    proc.wait(timeout=timeout)
261	                    return True
262	                except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
263	                    return False
264	        else:
265	            def _wait(timeout: float | None) -> bool:
266	                reader.join(timeout=timeout)
267	                return not reader.is_alive()
268	
269	        breach = _monitor(
270	            _wait,
271	            accumulator,
272	            started=started,
273	            token_limit=token_limit,
274	            time_limit_seconds=time_limit_seconds,
275	            poll_interval_seconds=poll_interval_seconds,
276	        )
277	        if breach is not None:
278	            if proc is not None:
279	                _kill_process_group(proc)
280	                proc.wait()
281	            elif kill_fn is not None:
282	                kill_fn()
283	        reader.join(timeout=_READER_JOIN_TIMEOUT_SECONDS)
284	        duration = time.monotonic() - started
285	
286	        cost_usd, num_turns, session_id, is_error = accumulator.summary()
287	        result = WorkerResult(
288	            exit_code=proc.returncode if proc is not None else 0,
289	            breach=breach,
290	            duration_seconds=duration,
291	            model=model,
292	            usage=accumulator.usage(),
293	            cost_usd=cost_usd,
294	            num_turns=num_turns,
295	            session_id=session_id,
296	            is_error=is_error,
297	        )
298	        _annotate_worker_span(span, result)
299	        return result

301	
302	def _annotate_worker_span(span: Span, result: WorkerResult) -> None:
303	    """Record a finished session's usage onto its span.
304	
305	    Token totals, cost, turn count, and the session id become attributes so a
306	    drain's spend is queryable in Honeycomb (HEATMAP cost, GROUP BY model). A
307	    crossed cap is marked as an error with the breach's scope/metric/limit/
308	    observed broken out and a static slug — so a guardrail kill is filterable
309	    and ties back to the worker's monitor loop. ``is_error`` is the claude
310	    session's own terminal flag, distinct from a breach kill.
311	    """
312	    usage = result.usage
313	    span.set_attribute("worker.exit_code", result.exit_code)
314	    span.set_attribute("worker.duration_seconds", result.duration_seconds)
315	    span.set_attribute("worker.tokens.cumulative", usage.get("cumulative", 0))
316	    span.set_attribute("worker.tokens.peak_context", usage.get("peak_context", 0))
317	    if result.num_turns is not None:
318	        span.set_attribute("worker.num_turns", result.num_turns)
319	    if result.cost_usd is not None:
320	        span.set_attribute("worker.cost_usd", result.cost_usd)
321	    if result.session_id is not None:
322	        span.set_attribute("worker.session_id", result.session_id)
323	    if result.is_error is not None:
324	        span.set_attribute("worker.is_error", result.is_error)
325	    if result.breach is not None:
326	        breach = result.breach
327	        span.set_attribute("worker.breach.scope", breach.scope)
328	        span.set_attribute("worker.breach.metric", breach.metric)
329	        span.set_attribute("worker.breach.limit", breach.limit)
330	        span.set_attribute("worker.breach.observed", breach.observed)
331	        telemetry.mark_error(span, "err-worker-breach", breach.describe())
332	
333	
334	def _monitor(
335	    wait: Callable[[float | None], bool],
336	    accumulator: _UsageAccumulator,
337	    *,
338	    started: float,
339	    token_limit: int | None,
340	    time_limit_seconds: float | None,
341	    poll_interval_seconds: float,
342	) -> Breach | None:
343	    """Wait for the session, returning the per-issue cap it breaches (if any).
344	
345	    ``wait(timeout)`` blocks up to ``timeout`` seconds (``None`` = until
346	    completion) and returns ``True`` once the session has finished — a thin
347	    seam over ``proc.wait`` (spawned path) or the reader thread reaching EOF
348	    (external path). With both caps off there is nothing to watch for, so we
349	    block until completion. Otherwise we wake every ``poll_interval_seconds``
350	    to compare the live cumulative-token tally and elapsed wall-clock against
351	    the caps; the first crossed cap is returned for the caller to kill on.
352	    Returns ``None`` when the session finishes on its own first.
353	    """
354	    if token_limit is None and time_limit_seconds is None:
355	        wait(None)
356	        return None
357	    while True:
358	        if wait(poll_interval_seconds):
359	            return None
360	        if token_limit is not None:
361	            observed = accumulator.cumulative()
362	            if observed >= token_limit:
363	                return Breach("per-issue", "token", token_limit, observed)
364	        if time_limit_seconds is not None:
365	            elapsed = time.monotonic() - started
366	            if elapsed >= time_limit_seconds:
367	                return Breach("per-issue", "time", time_limit_seconds, elapsed)
368	
369	
370	def _kill_process_group(proc: subprocess.Popen[str]) -> None:
371	    """SIGKILL the worker's whole process group, reaping grandchildren.
372	
373	    No-ops if the leader has already exited (its pid — and thus the group
374	    id — is gone). SIGKILL is sent before the caller reaps the leader, so
375	    the group still exists and its id cannot have been reused.
376	    """
377	    try:
378	        pgid = os.getpgid(proc.pid)
379	    except ProcessLookupError:
380	        return
381	    try:
382	        os.killpg(pgid, signal.SIGKILL)
383	    except ProcessLookupError:
384	        pass
385	
386	
387	def _drain_stream(
388	    stream: TextIO | None,
389	    accumulator: _UsageAccumulator,
390	    sink: TextIO,
391	    on_progress: Callable[[int, int, int, float | None], None] | None = None,
392	) -> None:
393	    """Read the session's stream-json output line by line until EOF.
394	
395	    Recognised JSON object events feed the usage accumulator; everything
396	    else (non-JSON diagnostics, JSON that isn't an object) is echoed to
397	    ``sink`` so the operator keeps the visibility a captured pipe would
398	    otherwise hide. ``on_progress`` is called once per new turn (deduplicated
399	    by message id) with a live usage snapshot. Reaching EOF (and closing the
400	    stream) is also how the external-path monitor learns the session ended.
401	    """
402	    if stream is None:
403	        return
404	    last_message_id: str | None = None
405	    try:
406	        for line in stream:
407	            line = line.rstrip("\n")
408	            if not line:
409	                continue
410	            try:
411	                event = json.loads(line)
412	            except json.JSONDecodeError:
413	                _echo(sink, line)
414	                continue
415	            if isinstance(event, dict):
416	                accumulator.feed(event)
417	                if event.get("type") == "assistant":
418	                    mid = (event.get("message") or {}).get("id")
419	                    if on_progress is not None and mid != last_message_id:
420	                        last_message_id = mid
421	                        snap = accumulator.live_snapshot()
422	                        on_progress(*snap)
423	            else:
424	                _echo(sink, line)
425	    finally:
426	        try:
427	            stream.close()
428	        except OSError:
429	            pass
430	
431	
432	def _echo(sink: TextIO, line: str) -> None:
433	    try:
434	        print(line, file=sink)
435	    except (OSError, ValueError):
436	        pass
437	
438	
439	def _coerce_int(value: Any) -> int:
440	    return value if isinstance(value, int) else 0
441	
442	
443	class _UsageAccumulator:
444	    """Reconstruct session usage from the streamed events.
445	
446	    Per-turn usage is keyed by ``message.id`` so a turn emitted once per
447	    content block is counted once; the last copy wins (later events carry
448	    the more complete snapshot). The ``result`` event overwrites the
449	    session-summary fields each time it is seen.
450	
451	    All mutation happens on the reader thread and all reads happen on the
452	    main thread after it is joined — but the join is bounded, so a group
453	    member that escaped the kill (e.g. via ``setsid``) could keep the
454	    reader alive past the join. The lock makes the concurrent case safe
455	    rather than a ``dictionary changed size during iteration`` crash.
456	    """
457	
458	    def __init__(self) -> None:
459	        self._lock = threading.Lock()
460	        self._turns: dict[str, dict[str, int]] = {}
461	        self._anon = 0
462	        self.cost_usd: float | None = None
463	        self.num_turns: int | None = None
464	        self.session_id: str | None = None
465	        self.is_error: bool | None = None
466	
467	    def feed(self, event: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
468	        event_type = event.get("type")
469	        if event_type == "assistant":
470	            self._feed_assistant(event)
471	        elif event_type == "result":
472	            self._feed_result(event)
473	
474	    def _feed_assistant(self, event: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
475	        message = event.get("message")
476	        if not isinstance(message, dict):
477	            return
478	        usage = message.get("usage")
479	        if not isinstance(usage, dict):
480	            return
481	        message_id = message.get("id")
482	        turn = {field: _coerce_int(usage.get(field)) for field in _TOKEN_FIELDS}
483	        with self._lock:
484	            if isinstance(message_id, str):
485	                key = message_id
486	            else:
487	                key = f"_anon-{self._anon}"
488	                self._anon += 1
489	            self._turns[key] = turn
490	
491	    def _feed_result(self, event: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
492	        with self._lock:
493	            self.cost_usd = event.get("total_cost_usd")
494	            self.num_turns = event.get("num_turns")
495	            self.session_id = event.get("session_id")
496	            self.is_error = event.get("is_error")
497	
498	    def cumulative(self) -> int:
499	        """Live billed-token total across turns — the per-issue token cap is
500	        compared against this on every poll, so it reads under the lock and
501	        stays cheap (no peak-context / per-component breakdown)."""
502	        with self._lock:
503	            turns = list(self._turns.values())
504	        return sum(turn[field] for turn in turns for field in _TOKEN_FIELDS)
505	
506	    def live_snapshot(self) -> tuple[int, int, int, float | None]:
507	        """Current ``(turns, cumulative_tokens, peak_context_tokens, cost_usd)``.
508	
509	        Called from the reader thread after each new assistant turn. Reads
510	        under the lock so the main-thread monitor cannot see a torn state.
511	        """
512	        with self._lock:
513	            turn_count = len(self._turns)
514	            turns_list = list(self._turns.values())
515	            cost = self.cost_usd
516	        cumulative = sum(t[f] for t in turns_list for f in _TOKEN_FIELDS)
517	        peak = max(
518	            (
519	                t["input_tokens"]
520	                + t["cache_read_input_tokens"]
521	                + t["cache_creation_input_tokens"]
522	                for t in turns_list
523	            ),
524	            default=0,
525	        )
526	        return turn_count, cumulative, peak, cost
527	
528	    def usage(self) -> dict[str, int]:
529	        totals = {field: 0 for field in _TOKEN_FIELDS}
530	        peak_context = 0
531	        with self._lock:
532	            turns = list(self._turns.values())
533	        for turn in turns:
534	            for field in _TOKEN_FIELDS:
535	                totals[field] += turn[field]
536	            context = (
537	                turn["input_tokens"]
538	                + turn["cache_read_input_tokens"]
539	                + turn["cache_creation_input_tokens"]
540	            )
541	            peak_context = max(peak_context, context)
542	        return {
543	            **totals,
544	            "cumulative": sum(totals.values()),
545	            "peak_context": peak_context,
546	        }
547	
548	    def summary(self) -> tuple[float | None, int | None, str | None, bool | None]:
549	        """Read the result-event fields under the lock, as a snapshot."""
550	        with self._lock:
551	            return self.cost_usd, self.num_turns, self.session_id, self.is_error
552
