1	"""Minimal Linear GraphQL client.
2	
3	Reads ``LINEAR_API_KEY`` from the environment. The CLI entrypoint
4	(``cli.main``) loads ``.env`` from the drain-cycle repo root before this
5	module is exercised; a shell-exported value still takes precedence.
6	Single-team scope: hardcoded to the ``Personal`` team per README §1.
7	"""
8	from __future__ import annotations
9	
10	import os
11	from dataclasses import dataclass
12	from typing import Any
13	
14	import httpx
15	
16	from . import telemetry
17	
18	_DEFAULT_GRAPHQL_URL = "https://api.linear.app/graphql"
19	_TEAM_NAME = "Personal"
20	_PENDING_STATE_TYPES = ["backlog", "unstarted"]
21	_RESOLVED_STATE_TYPES = {"completed", "canceled"}
22	
23	
24	@dataclass(frozen=True)
25	class ExecutionPlan:
26	    """Result of ``_plan``: runnable issues in topo order + deferred issues."""
27	    order: list[dict[str, Any]]   # issues to spawn, in execution order
28	    deferred: list[dict[str, Any]]  # each {"issue", "blocker_identifier", "blocker_state_type"}
29	
30	
31	class DependencyCycleError(RuntimeError):
32	    """Raised when the blocks graph among pending issues contains a cycle."""
33	    def __init__(self, identifiers: list[str]) -> None:
34	        self.identifiers = list(identifiers)
35	        super().__init__(f"Dependency cycle among: {', '.join(identifiers)}")
36	
37	
38	def _post(
39	    query: str,
40	    variables: dict[str, Any] | None = None,
41	    *,
42	    operation: str = "graphql",
43	) -> dict[str, Any]:
44	    """Execute one GraphQL request. ``operation`` names the span and the
45	    ``linear.operation`` attribute so the five distinct calls (which all POST
46	    to the same endpoint) stay separable in Honeycomb; the httpx transport is
47	    auto-instrumented, so the raw HTTP POST appears as a child span."""
48	    with telemetry.tracer.start_as_current_span(f"linear.{operation}") as span:
49	        span.set_attribute("linear.operation", operation)
50	        key = os.environ.get("LINEAR_API_KEY")
51	        if not key:
52	            telemetry.mark_error(span, "err-linear-no-api-key", "LINEAR_API_KEY is not set")
53	            raise RuntimeError("LINEAR_API_KEY is not set")
54	        url = os.environ.get("LINEAR_API_URL", _DEFAULT_GRAPHQL_URL)
55	        resp = httpx.post(
56	            url,
57	            headers={"Authorization": key, "Content-Type": "application/json"},
58	            json={"query": query, "variables": variables or {}},
59	            timeout=30.0,
60	        )
61	        try:
62	            resp.raise_for_status()
63	        except httpx.HTTPStatusError as exc:
64	            span.set_attribute("http.response.status_code", resp.status_code)
65	            telemetry.mark_error(span, "err-linear-http", str(exc))
66	            raise
67	        body = resp.json()
68	        if "errors" in body:
69	            telemetry.mark_error(span, "err-linear-graphql", "Linear GraphQL errors")
70	            raise RuntimeError(f"Linear GraphQL errors: {body['errors']}")
71	        return body["data"]
72	
73	
74	def current_cycle_id() -> str:
75	    """Return the active cycle id for the configured team."""
76	    data = _post(
77	        """
78	        query CurrentCycle($name: String!) {
79	          teams(filter: { name: { eq: $name } }) {
80	            nodes {
81	              id
82	              activeCycle { id }
83	            }
84	          }
85	        }
86	        """,
87	        {"name": _TEAM_NAME},
88	        operation="current_cycle",
89	    )
90	    nodes = data["teams"]["nodes"]
91	    if not nodes:
92	        raise RuntimeError(f"Linear team {_TEAM_NAME!r} not found")
93	    cycle = nodes[0].get("activeCycle")
94	    if not cycle:
95	        raise RuntimeError(f"Linear team {_TEAM_NAME!r} has no active cycle")
96	    return cycle["id"]
97	
98	
99	def _plan(issues: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> ExecutionPlan:
100	    """Topological sort over ``issues`` respecting blocks/blocked-by.
101	
102	    Returns an ``ExecutionPlan`` with ``order`` (runnable, topo-sorted by
103	    ``(sortOrder, id)`` tiebreak) and ``deferred`` (issues skipped because
104	    an external unresolved blocker exists, or because a blocking issue in
105	    the drain was itself deferred — cascade).
106	
107	    Raises ``DependencyCycleError`` when the intra-set blocks graph contains
108	    a cycle (self-loops included).
109	    """
110	    P: set[str] = {i["id"] for i in issues}
111	    by_id: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] = {i["id"]: i for i in issues}
112	
113	    # Build de-duped intra-P edges: blocker_id → set of blocked_ids
114	    children: dict[str, set[str]] = {i["id"]: set() for i in issues}
115	    # Per-issue unique set of intra-P blocker ids (for cascade check + in-degree)
116	    intra_blockers: dict[str, set[str]] = {i["id"]: set() for i in issues}
117	    indegree: dict[str, int] = {i["id"]: 0 for i in issues}
118	
119	    for issue in issues:
120	        for blocker in issue.get("blockers", []):
121	            bid = blocker["id"]
122	            if bid in P:
123	                if bid not in intra_blockers[issue["id"]]:
124	                    intra_blockers[issue["id"]].add(bid)
125	                    indegree[issue["id"]] += 1
126	                    children[bid].add(issue["id"])
127	
128	    # Kahn's algorithm: ready = in-degree-0 issues, sorted by (sortOrder, id)
129	    ready: list[dict[str, Any]] = sorted(
130	        [i for i in issues if indegree[i["id"]] == 0],
131	        key=lambda i: (i["sortOrder"], i["id"]),
132	    )
133	
134	    order: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
135	    deferred: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
136	    deferred_ids: set[str] = set()
137	
138	    while ready:
139	        issue = ready.pop(0)
140	        iid = issue["id"]
141	
142	        # Cascade: any intra-P blocker already deferred → defer this too
143	        cascade_blocker = next(
144	            (by_id[bid] for bid in intra_blockers[iid] if bid in deferred_ids),
145	            None,
146	        )
147	        if cascade_blocker is not None:
148	            deferred.append({
149	                "issue": issue,
150	                "blocker_identifier": cascade_blocker["identifier"],
151	                "blocker_state_type": cascade_blocker["state"]["type"],
152	            })
153	            deferred_ids.add(iid)
154	        else:
155	            # External unresolved blocker → defer
156	            ext_blocker = next(
157	                (
158	                    b for b in issue.get("blockers", [])
159	                    if b["id"] not in P and b["state_type"] not in _RESOLVED_STATE_TYPES
160	                ),
161	                None,
162	            )
163	            if ext_blocker is not None:
164	                deferred.append({
165	                    "issue": issue,
166	                    "blocker_identifier": ext_blocker["identifier"],
167	                    "blocker_state_type": ext_blocker["state_type"],
168	                })
169	                deferred_ids.add(iid)
170	            else:
171	                order.append(issue)
172	
173	        # Decrement children regardless (deferred nodes still unblock their children
174	        # in the graph so cycle detection remains accurate)
175	        for child_id in children[iid]:
176	            indegree[child_id] -= 1
177	            if indegree[child_id] == 0:
178	                child = by_id[child_id]
179	                # Insert maintaining (sortOrder, id) order
180	                idx = 0
181	                key = (child["sortOrder"], child["id"])
182	                while idx < len(ready) and (ready[idx]["sortOrder"], ready[idx]["id"]) <= key:
183	                    idx += 1
184	                ready.insert(idx, child)
185	
186	    cycle_nodes = [i for i in issues if indegree[i["id"]] > 0]
187	    if cycle_nodes:
188	        raise DependencyCycleError([i["identifier"] for i in cycle_nodes])
189	
190	    return ExecutionPlan(order=order, deferred=deferred)
191	
192	
193	def _label_name(node: dict[str, Any]) -> str:
194	    """Render a label node in its prefix form.
195	
196	    A label in a Linear label *group* arrives as a leaf ``name`` with a
197	    ``parent`` group; downstream resolvers match on a ``<group>:`` prefix
198	    (``repo:``, ``model:``), so a grouped label renders as
199	    ``"<group>:<name>"``. An ungrouped label keeps its bare name, which
200	    already carries any literal prefix typed into the label name.
201	    """
202	    parent = node.get("parent")
203	    if parent:
204	        return f"{parent['name']}:{node['name']}"
205	    return node["name"]
206	
207	
208	def pending_issues(cycle_id: str) -> ExecutionPlan:
209	    """Return an ``ExecutionPlan`` for every Todo/Backlog issue in the cycle.
210	
211	    No pagination: personal cycles fit comfortably in one page. If a cycle
212	    ever exceeds 100 pending issues, that's a planning problem, not a tool
213	    problem (see ``PRODUCT_RULES`` Rule A5 — focus is the multiplier).
214	
215	    Post-processing flattens two wire-shape fields:
216	    - ``labels { nodes { name parent { name } } }`` → ``labels: list[str]``
217	      (grouped labels rendered as ``"<group>:<name>"``)
218	    - ``inverseRelations`` filtered to ``type == "blocks"``
219	      → ``blockers: list[{id, identifier, state_type}]``; raw key removed.
220	    """
221	    data = _post(
222	        """
223	        query CyclePending($cycleId: ID!, $stateTypes: [String!]!) {
224	          issues(
225	            filter: {
226	              cycle: { id: { eq: $cycleId } }
227	              state: { type: { in: $stateTypes } }
228	            }
229	            first: 100
230	          ) {
231	            nodes {
232	              id
233	              identifier
234	              title
235	              description
236	              sortOrder
237	              state { type name }
238	              labels { nodes { name parent { name } } }
239	              inverseRelations { nodes { type issue { id identifier state { type } } } }
240	            }
241	          }
242	        }
243	        """,
244	        {"cycleId": cycle_id, "stateTypes": _PENDING_STATE_TYPES},
245	        operation="pending_issues",
246	    )
247	    issues = data["issues"]["nodes"]
248	    for issue in issues:
249	        issue["labels"] = [_label_name(node) for node in issue["labels"]["nodes"]]
250	        issue["blockers"] = [
251	            {
252	                "id": node["issue"]["id"],
253	                "identifier": node["issue"]["identifier"],
254	                "state_type": node["issue"]["state"]["type"],
255	            }
256	            for node in issue["inverseRelations"]["nodes"]
257	            if node["type"] == "blocks"
258	        ]
259	        del issue["inverseRelations"]
260	    return _plan(issues)
261	
262	
263	def get_issue(issue_id: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
264	    """Re-fetch an issue's current state by id."""
265	    data = _post(
266	        """
267	        query Issue($id: String!) {
268	          issue(id: $id) {
269	            id
270	            identifier
271	            title
272	            state { type name }
273	          }
274	        }
275	        """,
276	        {"id": issue_id},
277	        operation="get_issue",
278	    )
279	    issue = data["issue"]
280	    if issue is None:
281	        raise RuntimeError(f"Linear issue {issue_id!r} not found")
282	    return issue
283	
284	
285	def set_state(issue_id: str, state_name: str) -> None:
286	    """Transition an issue to the named workflow state.
287	
288	    Resolves the state ID by name against the configured team and then issues
289	    an ``issueUpdate`` mutation. No caching — at cycle scale (≤ ~15 issues)
290	    the extra round-trip is irrelevant and the simpler code is easier to test.
291	    """
292	    state_id = _resolve_state_id(state_name)
293	    data = _post(
294	        """
295	        mutation IssueSetState($id: String!, $input: IssueUpdateInput!) {
296	          issueUpdate(id: $id, input: $input) {

298	          }
299	        }
300	        """,
301	        {"id": issue_id, "input": {"stateId": state_id}},
302	        operation="set_state",
303	    )

307	        )
308

310	def add_comment(issue_id: str, body: str) -> None:
311	    """Post a comment on a Linear issue."""
312	    data = _post(
313	        """
314	        mutation CommentCreate($input: CommentCreateInput!) {
315	          commentCreate(input: $input) {
316	            success
317	          }
318	        }
319	        """,

321	        operation="add_comment",

324	        raise RuntimeError(f"Linear commentCreate failed for issue {issue_id!r}")
325	
326	
327	def _resolve_state_id(state_name: str) -> str:
328	    data = _post(
329	        """
330	        query WorkflowState($team: String!, $name: String!) {
331	          workflowStates(
332	            filter: {

334	              name: { eq: $name }
335	            }
336	            first: 1
337	          ) {
338	            nodes { id }

340	        }

343	        operation="resolve_state",
344	    )

346	    if not nodes:

1	"""Thin wrapper around ``git worktree``.
2	
3	Each issue gets ``.worktrees/<issue-identifier>/`` branched off ``main``,
4	used once, then removed on Done — or preserved on halt so a later re-run
5	can resume against the committed work (see ``docs/design-decisions.md``
6	§14).
7	
8	``git worktree`` stderr is captured and surfaced in the raised
9	``RuntimeError`` on failure. The orchestrator's pre-spawn try/except
10	threads the message into the runlog's ``halt_reason`` so the operator
11	sees git's actual diagnostic (dirty tree, branch already exists,
12	missing ``main``) rather than just a non-zero exit code.
13	"""
14	from __future__ import annotations
15	
16	import os
17	import shutil
18	import subprocess
19	from dataclasses import dataclass
20	from pathlib import Path
21	from typing import Iterable
22	
23	from . import telemetry
24	
25	BASE_BRANCH = "main"
26	WORKTREE_DIR = ".worktrees"
27	# Records the branch a worktree was forked from, so a later run that reuses a
28	# preserved worktree (a resumed halt) recovers the true base rather than
29	# recomputing it from the in-memory chain baton, which is empty across runs.
30	BASE_FILE = ".drain-base"
31	
32	
33	@dataclass(frozen=True)
34	class WorktreeHandle:
35	    """A prepared worktree, together with whether it was reused.
36	
37	    ``resumed`` is ``True`` when ``ensure`` found a pre-existing worktree
38	    registered at the expected path (typically left behind by an earlier
39	    halted run). Callers thread the flag into the spawn-time prompt so
40	    the agent knows it is continuing from prior committed work rather
41	    than starting fresh.
42	    """
43	
44	    path: Path
45	    resumed: bool
46	
47	
48	def add(repo: Path, identifier: str, base: str = BASE_BRANCH) -> Path:
49	    """Create a worktree branched off ``base`` for ``identifier``.
50	
51	    Returns the absolute path to the new worktree.
52	    """
53	    worktree_path = repo / WORKTREE_DIR / identifier
54	    with telemetry.tracer.start_as_current_span("drain.worktree.add") as span:
55	        span.set_attribute("worktree.identifier", identifier)
56	        span.set_attribute("worktree.repo", repo.name)
57	        span.set_attribute("worktree.path", str(worktree_path))
58	        span.set_attribute("worktree.base", base)
59	        _run_git(
60	            ["worktree", "add", "-b", identifier, str(worktree_path), base],
61	            cwd=repo,
62	        )
63	    (worktree_path / BASE_FILE).write_text(f"{base}\n")
64	    return worktree_path
65	
66	
67	def ensure(repo: Path, identifier: str, base: str = BASE_BRANCH) -> WorktreeHandle:
68	    """Reuse a preserved worktree if one is already registered, else add.
69	
70	    A worktree registered at ``repo/.worktrees/<identifier>`` is reused
71	    as-is — no mutating git command is run, so a dirty index, staged or
72	    untracked files, and the gitignored config symlinks all survive
73	    untouched. Any other state (no entry at that path) falls through to
74	    :func:`add`, whose ``RuntimeError`` on a leftover branch or orphan
75	    directory is what the orchestrator's existing pre-spawn handler
76	    turns into the clean ``Halt: … — setup failed: …`` line.
77	    """
78	    worktree_path = repo / WORKTREE_DIR / identifier
79	    with telemetry.tracer.start_as_current_span("drain.worktree.ensure") as span:
80	        span.set_attribute("worktree.identifier", identifier)
81	        span.set_attribute("worktree.repo", repo.name)
82	        span.set_attribute("worktree.path", str(worktree_path))
83	        if _is_registered_worktree(repo, worktree_path):
84	            span.set_attribute("worktree.resumed", True)
85	            return WorktreeHandle(path=worktree_path, resumed=True)
86	        span.set_attribute("worktree.resumed", False)
87	    return WorktreeHandle(path=add(repo, identifier, base), resumed=False)
88	
89	
90	def read_base(worktree_path: Path) -> str:
91	    """Return the branch a worktree was forked from, or ``main`` if unknown.
92	
93	    Reads the :data:`BASE_FILE` marker written by :func:`add`. A missing or
94	    empty marker (a worktree created before this marker existed, or one not
95	    created by :func:`add`) falls back to ``BASE_BRANCH`` — the same default
96	    an unchained issue would compute.
97	    """
98	    try:
99	        base = (worktree_path / BASE_FILE).read_text().strip()
100	    except OSError:
101	        return BASE_BRANCH
102	    return base or BASE_BRANCH
103	
104	
105	def link_project_config(
106	    repo: Path, worktree_path: Path, names: Iterable[str]
107	) -> list[Path]:
108	    """Symlink gitignored project-scoped config from ``repo`` into the worktree.
109	
110	    A git worktree checks out only tracked files, so gitignored project config
111	    (``.claude/`` settings/hooks/agents/skills, a root ``.mcp.json``) is absent.
112	    Linking the repo's real entries in gives a worker the same settings, hooks,
113	    agents, skills, and MCP config as an interactive session at the repo root —
114	    and because the link points at the live dir, a stateful hook reads and
115	    writes the repo's actual config exactly as a non-worktree run would.
116	
117	    For each name: skip it if absent in ``repo`` (a clean no-op for repos
118	    without that config) or if something already occupies that path in the
119	    worktree (a tracked entry git checked out, or a pre-existing link). The
120	    check uses ``os.path.lexists`` so a dangling link counts as present and is
121	    never clobbered.
122	
123	    Exception: if the worktree path is a real directory (not a symlink), it
124	    was likely created by a tool running in the worktree before the symlink
125	    could be planted (e.g. entire.io creating ``.entire/`` on first run). In
126	    that case, merge any files from the worktree directory into ``source``
127	    (without overwriting existing files), remove the directory, and replace it
128	    with a symlink so future writes land in the shared location.
129	
130	    Returns the links created.
131	    """
132	    created: list[Path] = []
133	    repo = repo.resolve()
134	    for name in names:
135	        source = repo / name
136	        if not source.exists():
137	            continue
138	        link = worktree_path / name
139	        if os.path.islink(link):
140	            continue
141	        if link.is_dir() and _is_gitignored(worktree_path, name):
142	            # Migrate: move files not already in source, then replace with symlink.
143	            for item in link.iterdir():
144	                dest = source / item.name
145	                if not dest.exists():
146	                    shutil.move(str(item), str(dest))
147	            shutil.rmtree(link)
148	        elif os.path.lexists(link):
149	            # A tracked or otherwise pre-existing entry we won't clobber.
150	            continue
151	        os.symlink(source, link)
152	        created.append(link)
153	    return created
154	
155	
156	def merge_entire_sessions(worktree_path: Path, repo: Path) -> None:
157	    """Merge session dirs from a worktree-local ``.entire/`` into the repo-root one.
158	
159	    Called just before removal so entire.io session data written by the
160	    worker isn't lost. No-op if the worktree's ``.entire`` is a symlink
161	    (already points at the repo root) or if the repo has no ``.entire/``.
162	    """
163	    worktree_entire = worktree_path / ".entire"
164	    if not worktree_entire.is_dir() or os.path.islink(worktree_entire):
165	        return
166	    repo_entire = repo.resolve() / ".entire"
167	    if not repo_entire.exists():
168	        return
169	    for item in worktree_entire.iterdir():
170	        dest = repo_entire / item.name
171	        if not dest.exists():
172	            shutil.move(str(item), str(dest))
173	
174	
175	def remove(repo: Path, worktree_path: Path) -> None:
176	    """Remove a worktree previously created by :func:`add`."""
177	    with telemetry.tracer.start_as_current_span("drain.worktree.remove") as span:
178	        span.set_attribute("worktree.repo", repo.name)
179	        span.set_attribute("worktree.path", str(worktree_path))
180	        _run_git(["worktree", "remove", "--force", str(worktree_path)], cwd=repo)
181	
182	
183	def _is_gitignored(repo: Path, name: str) -> bool:
184	    """Return ``True`` if ``name`` is gitignored in ``repo``."""
185	    result = subprocess.run(
186	        ["git", "check-ignore", "-q", name],
187	        cwd=repo,
188	        check=False,
189	        capture_output=True,
190	    )
191	    return result.returncode == 0
192	
193	
194	def _is_registered_worktree(repo: Path, worktree_path: Path) -> bool:
195	    """Return ``True`` if git lists a worktree at ``worktree_path``.
196	
197	    Parses ``git worktree list --porcelain -z`` for a ``worktree <path>``
198	    record matching ``worktree_path.resolve()``. ``-z`` makes each record
199	    NUL-separated and each field NUL-terminated, so paths with embedded
200	    spaces or newlines are unambiguous. The resolve step matches git's
201	    own canonicalisation (symlinks, ``..``) so a worktree under a
202	    symlinked repo path still matches the entry git printed. A non-zero
203	    git exit is treated as not-registered so ``ensure`` falls through to
204	    ``add``, whose error surfaces git's real diagnostic via the
205	    orchestrator's pre-spawn halt path.
206	    """
207	    target = worktree_path.resolve()
208	    result = subprocess.run(
209	        ["git", "worktree", "list", "--porcelain", "-z"],
210	        cwd=repo,
211	        check=False,
212	        capture_output=True,
213	        text=True,
214	    )
215	    if result.returncode != 0:
216	        return False
217	    for field in result.stdout.split("\0"):
218	        if not field.startswith("worktree "):
219	            continue
220	        listed = Path(field.removeprefix("worktree "))
221	        if listed.resolve() == target:
222	            return True
223	    return False
224	
225	
226	def _run_git(args: list[str], *, cwd: Path) -> None:
227	    result = subprocess.run(
228	        ["git", *args],
229	        cwd=cwd,
230	        check=False,
231	        capture_output=True,
232	        text=True,
233	    )
234	    if result.returncode != 0:
235	        raise RuntimeError(
236	            f"git {' '.join(args)} failed (exit {result.returncode}): "
237	            f"{result.stderr.strip() or result.stdout.strip() or '<no output>'}"
238	        )
239	
```

1	"""Prompt builder for spawned ``claude -p`` sessions.
2	
3	The prompt is the entire contract between the orchestrator and the spawned
4	agent — there's no system prompt, no multi-turn loop. The four-segment
5	ordering below is load-bearing: the agent reads top-down, so context
6	(title + body) comes before instructions (preamble + tail), and the tail
7	line is last so it stays in the trailing-tokens window the model attends
8	to most strongly.
9	"""
10	from __future__ import annotations
11	
12	from pathlib import Path
13	from typing import Any
14	
15	_VERIFY_FLOW_LABEL = "verify"
16	
17	
18	def is_verify_flow(issue: dict[str, Any]) -> bool:
19	    """Return True when the issue carries the ``verify`` label."""
20	    return _VERIFY_FLOW_LABEL in issue.get("labels", [])
21	
22	
23	def _shape_task_directive(identifier: str) -> str:
24	    """Shape-task preamble for verify-flow worker sessions.
25	
26	    Instructs the worker to check for an existing Task Shaper output
27	    block in the Linear issue body before invoking ``/shape:task``. The
28	    check-first approach handles both fresh sessions (block absent →
29	    invoke) and §14 resumes (block present → reuse).
30	    """
31	    return (
32	        f"Shaping step (run before implementing): fetch the Linear issue body "
33	        f"for {identifier} and check for a Task Shaper output block "
34	        "(delimited by `<!-- TASK-SHAPER-START -->` and `<!-- TASK-SHAPER-END -->`). "
35	        "If the block is present, use it as your implementation guide and skip "
36	        "re-running `/shape:task`. If the block is absent, run "
37	        f"`/shape:task {identifier}` first and use its output as your "
38	        "implementation guide.\n\n"
39	    )
40	
41	
42	_TAIL = (
43	    "before marking Done: review the working-tree changes for correctness "
44	    "and quality, fix Critical/Required findings, commit + push, then post a "
45	    "review-summary comment on the issue and transition to Done."
46	)
47	
48	_STACK_TAIL = (
49	    "before marking Done: review the changes, fix Critical/Required findings, "
50	    "commit them as reviewable slices on the issue branch, then run "
51	    "`/shape:pr-finishing` to submit the stacked PR(s) and post the "
52	    "review-summary comment, and only then transition to Done."
53	)
54	
55	
56	def _resume_directive(identifier: str, base: str) -> str:
57	    """Resume preamble for a worktree carrying prior committed work.
58	
59	    Inserted as the first line inside the preamble (after the ``---``
60	    separator, before "Execution instructions:") so the agent reads it
61	    ahead of the procedure but the tail still holds the last-line
62	    position the four-segment ordering reserves for it.
63	
64	    The ``git log`` range is anchored at ``base`` so a chained worktree
65	    (branched off the prior issue's branch, not ``main``) reads back only
66	    its own commits rather than the whole stack beneath it.
67	    """
68	    return (
69	        f"Resuming issue {identifier}: this worktree carries prior committed "
70	        "work from an earlier session that was halted. Run "
71	        f"`git log --oneline {base}..HEAD` and `git status` first to read what "
72	        "is already done, then continue from that point — do not restart "
73	        "from scratch.\n\n"
74	    )
75	
76	
77	def _normal_preamble(
78	    identifier: str, worktree: Path, base: str, resume_segment: str, shape_task_segment: str
79	) -> str:
80	    return (
81	        "---\n\n"
82	        f"{resume_segment}"
83	        f"{shape_task_segment}"
84	        "Execution instructions:\n"
85	        f"- Working directory: {worktree}\n"
86	        f"- Base branch: {base}\n"
87	        f"- Completion sequence for issue {identifier} (run in this order, "
88	        "before marking Done):\n"
89	        "  1. Review the working-tree changes for correctness and quality.\n"
90	        "  2. Fix any Critical or Required findings. Lower-severity findings "
91	        "are at your discretion.\n"
92	        "  3. Commit and push to main.\n"
93	        "  4. Post a short review-summary comment on the Linear issue via "
94	        "`mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_comment` (count of findings by severity, "
95	        "fixed vs deferred).\n"
96	        "  5. Transition issue to Done via `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_issue` "
97	        '(state: "Done").\n'
98	    )
99	
100	
101	def _stack_preamble(
102	    identifier: str, worktree: Path, base: str, resume_segment: str, shape_task_segment: str
103	) -> str:
104	    # When the worktree is chained off a prior issue's branch rather than
105	    # ``main``, the slices stack on that branch — tell pr-finishing so it
106	    # slices ``<base>..HEAD`` instead of its ``main..HEAD`` default and
107	    # opens the PR against the right base. Omitted for the ``main`` base so
108	    # the unchained prompt stays unchanged.
109	    base_clause = (
110	        ""
111	        if base == "main"
112	        else (
113	            f" These commits are stacked on `{base}`, not `main`, so pass "
114	            f"`{base}` to the skill as its base branch (it slices `{base}..HEAD`)."
115	        )
116	    )
117	    return (
118	        "---\n\n"
119	        f"{resume_segment}"
120	        f"{shape_task_segment}"
121	        "Execution instructions:\n"
122	        f"- Working directory: {worktree}\n"
123	        f"- Base branch: {base}\n"
124	        f"- Completion sequence for issue {identifier} (run in this order, "
125	        "before marking Done):\n"
126	        "  1. Review the working-tree changes for correctness and quality.\n"
127	        "  2. Fix any Critical or Required findings. Lower-severity findings "
128	        "are at your discretion.\n"
129	        "  3. Commit the changes to the issue branch as reviewable slices — "
130	        "one logical change per commit. Do not push by hand.\n"
131	        "  4. Run `/shape:pr-finishing`. It submits the slices as stacked "
132	        "PR(s) via Graphite, writes the submitted PR URLs into "
133	        "`.drain-handoff.json` (`pr_urls`), and posts the review-summary "
134	        "comment on the Linear issue. Do not run `gt`/`gh` by hand or write "
135	        f"`.drain-handoff.json` yourself — the skill owns both.{base_clause}\n"
136	        "  5. Confirm `.drain-handoff.json` now contains a non-empty `pr_urls` "
137	        "list. If the skill could not submit, leave the issue In Progress and "
138	        "comment the blocker — do not mark Done.\n"
139	        "  6. Transition issue to Done via `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_issue` "
140	        '(state: "Done").\n'
141	    )
142	
143	
144	def build(
145	    issue: dict[str, Any],
146	    worktree: Path,
147	    *,
148	    resumed: bool = False,
149	    stack: bool = False,
150	    base: str = "main",
151	) -> str:
152	    title = issue.get("title", "")
153	    description = issue.get("description") or ""
154	    identifier = issue.get("identifier", "")
155	
156	    resume_segment = _resume_directive(identifier, base) if resumed else ""
157	    shape_task_segment = _shape_task_directive(identifier) if is_verify_flow(issue) else ""
158	
159	    if stack:
160	        preamble = _stack_preamble(
161	            identifier, worktree, base, resume_segment, shape_task_segment
162	        )
163	        tail = _STACK_TAIL
164	    else:
165	        preamble = _normal_preamble(
166	            identifier, worktree, base, resume_segment, shape_task_segment
167	        )
168	        tail = _TAIL
169	
170	    return (
171	        f"# {title}\n\n"
172	        f"{description}\n\n"
173	        f"{preamble}\n"
174	        f"{tail}\n"
175	    )
176	
```

1	"""Handoff artefact a stack-mode worker leaves behind after submitting PRs.
2	
3	In stack mode the worker drives PR submission itself via the ``pr-finishing``
4	skill, which runs ``gt``/``gh`` inside the worktree, posts the Linear
5	review-summary comment, and records the submitted PRs in
6	``.drain-handoff.json`` as a ``pr_urls`` list. The orchestrator reads that
7	list back as its confirmation signal — a present, non-empty ``pr_urls`` means
8	the skill submitted at least one PR; its absence means submission never
9	completed and the per-repo chain must halt rather than march on.
10	
11	``read`` never raises: a missing or malformed file returns ``None`` so callers
12	can treat it as "not submitted yet."
13	"""
14	from __future__ import annotations
15	
16	import json
17	from dataclasses import dataclass
18	from pathlib import Path
19	
20	HANDOFF_FILE = ".drain-handoff.json"
21	
22	
23	@dataclass(frozen=True)
24	class PullRequest:
25	    title: str
26	    url: str
27	
28	
29	@dataclass(frozen=True)
30	class HandoffData:
31	    pr_urls: tuple[PullRequest, ...]
32	
33	
34	def write(worktree: Path, data: HandoffData) -> None:
35	    """Serialise ``data`` to ``<worktree>/.drain-handoff.json``."""
36	    path = worktree / HANDOFF_FILE
37	    path.write_text(
38	        json.dumps(
39	            {
40	                "pr_urls": [
41	                    {"title": pr.title, "url": pr.url} for pr in data.pr_urls
42	                ],
43	            },
44	            indent=2,
45	        )
46	    )
47	
48	
49	def read(worktree: Path) -> HandoffData | None:
50	    """Return the handoff data for ``worktree``, or ``None`` if absent or invalid.
51	
52	    A valid handoff has a ``pr_urls`` list with at least one entry, each entry
53	    a dict carrying string ``title`` and ``url``. An empty list is treated as
54	    invalid: the skill writes ``pr_urls`` only after a PR is actually created,
55	    so "present but empty" means no submission happened.
56	    """
57	    path = worktree / HANDOFF_FILE
58	    try:
59	        payload = json.loads(path.read_text())
60	    except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError):
61	        return None
62	    if not isinstance(payload, dict):
63	        return None
64	    raw = payload.get("pr_urls")
65	    if not isinstance(raw, list) or not raw:
66	        return None
67	    prs: list[PullRequest] = []
68	    for entry in raw:
69	        if not isinstance(entry, dict):
70	            return None
71	        title = entry.get("title")
72	        url = entry.get("url")
73	        if not isinstance(title, str) or not isinstance(url, str) or not url:
74	            return None
75	        prs.append(PullRequest(title=title, url=url))
76	    return HandoffData(pr_urls=tuple(prs))
77	
```

1	"""Per-issue target-repo resolution.
2	
3	The orchestrator used to be single-repo by construction: every worktree
4	landed under ``Path.cwd()``. Cycles in this workspace span multiple repos
5	by design — ``linear-workflow.md`` makes "Affected repos" part of the
6	six initiative-readiness fields, and the Ops slot deliberately holds
7	cross-repo maintenance issues — so each issue now carries a
8	``repo:<name>`` label and ``~/.drain-cycle/repos.yml`` maps the name to
9	an absolute path on disk.
10	
11	Two distinct error types so the two halt paths can branch cleanly:
12	
13	* ``RepoConfigError`` — startup-time problems (missing file, malformed
14	  YAML, empty, wrong top-level shape). The CLI catches it before any
15	  Linear traffic, prints to stderr, exits 1, and does **not** write a
16	  run-log entry — there is no cycle to log against yet.
17	* ``RepoResolutionError`` — per-issue problems (no ``repo:`` label,
18	  multiple ``repo:`` labels, name not in config, resolved path missing).
19	  The orchestrator catches it as a pre-spawn halt: write a run-log
20	  entry, print the matching ``Halt:`` line, exit 1. No revert is
21	  attempted because no Linear state was moved yet.
22	"""
23	from __future__ import annotations
24	
25	from dataclasses import dataclass
26	from pathlib import Path
27	from typing import Any
28	
29	import yaml
30	
31	_CONFIG_DISPLAY = "~/.drain-cycle/repos.yml"
32	_REPO_LABEL_PREFIX = "repo:"
33	
34	DEFAULT_WORKTREE_CONFIG_PATHS = (".claude", ".mcp.json")
35	"""Project-scoped config a worker should inherit inside its worktree.
36	
37	A git worktree checks out only tracked files, so gitignored project config
38	(`.claude/`, a root `.mcp.json`) is absent unless symlinked in. These two
39	defaults suit any repo; an operator whose repo uses other tooling (e.g. the
40	`entire.io` tool's `.entire/`) extends the set via ``worktree_config_paths`` in
41	``repos.yml``. See :func:`drain_cycle.worktree.link_project_config`."""
42	
43	_MISSING_CONFIG_HINT = (
44	    "create it mapping each repo: label to an absolute path, e.g.:\n\n"
45	    "  repos:\n"
46	    "    drain-cycle: /Users/you/src/drain-cycle\n"
47	    "    pde-skills:  /Users/you/src/pde-skills\n\n"
48	    "see docs/repos.example.yml for a template."
49	)
50	
51	
52	class RepoConfigError(RuntimeError):
53	    """``repos.yml`` is missing or malformed."""
54	
55	
56	class RepoResolutionError(RuntimeError):
57	    """An issue cannot be mapped to a target repo."""
58	
59	
60	def default_config_path() -> Path:
61	    """Resolved per call so tests can redirect via ``monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", ...)``."""
62	    return Path.home() / ".drain-cycle" / "repos.yml"
63	
64	
65	@dataclass(frozen=True)
66	class Repos:
67	    """Loaded ``repos.yml`` mapping (``name`` → absolute ``Path``)."""
68	
69	    mapping: dict[str, Path]
70	    worktree_config_paths: tuple[str, ...] = DEFAULT_WORKTREE_CONFIG_PATHS
71	    push_to_main_repos: tuple[str, ...] = ()
72	
73	    def resolve(self, issue: dict[str, Any]) -> Path:
74	        labels = [
75	            label
76	            for label in issue.get("labels", [])
77	            if label.startswith(_REPO_LABEL_PREFIX)
78	        ]
79	        if not labels:
80	            raise RepoResolutionError(f"no {_REPO_LABEL_PREFIX} label on issue")
81	        if len(labels) > 1:
82	            names = ", ".join(sorted(label.removeprefix(_REPO_LABEL_PREFIX) for label in labels))
83	            raise RepoResolutionError(f"multiple {_REPO_LABEL_PREFIX} labels: {names}")
84	        name = labels[0].removeprefix(_REPO_LABEL_PREFIX)
85	        try:
86	            path = self.mapping[name]
87	        except KeyError:
88	            raise RepoResolutionError(
89	                f'repo "{name}" not in {_CONFIG_DISPLAY}'
90	            ) from None
91	        if not path.exists():
92	            raise RepoResolutionError(f"resolved path {path} does not exist")
93	        return path
94	
95	
96	def _parse_worktree_config_paths(value: Any) -> tuple[str, ...]:
97	    """Validate the optional ``worktree_config_paths`` key.
98	
99	    ``None`` (key absent) yields the default. A present value must be a
100	    non-empty list of relative-path strings — entries are symlinked into a
101	    worktree, so an absolute path or one containing ``..`` could point outside
102	    the repo and is rejected. Every failure raises ``RepoConfigError`` naming
103	    the offending entry.
104	    """
105	    if value is None:
106	        return DEFAULT_WORKTREE_CONFIG_PATHS
107	    if not isinstance(value, list) or not value:
108	        raise RepoConfigError(
109	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} 'worktree_config_paths' must be a non-empty list"
110	        )
111	    entries: list[str] = []
112	    for entry in value:
113	        if not isinstance(entry, str) or not entry.strip():
114	            raise RepoConfigError(
115	                f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} 'worktree_config_paths' entry {entry!r}: "
116	                "must be a non-empty string"
117	            )
118	        if Path(entry).is_absolute():
119	            raise RepoConfigError(
120	                f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} 'worktree_config_paths' entry {entry!r}: "
121	                "must be a relative path, not absolute"
122	            )
123	        if ".." in Path(entry).parts:
124	            raise RepoConfigError(
125	                f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} 'worktree_config_paths' entry {entry!r}: "
126	                "must not contain '..'"
127	            )
128	        entries.append(entry)
129	    return tuple(entries)
130	
131	
132	def _parse_push_to_main_repos(value: Any) -> tuple[str, ...]:
133	    """Validate the optional ``push_to_main_repos`` key.
134	
135	    ``None`` (key absent) yields the default (empty tuple). A present value
136	    must be a list of repo names matching entries in the ``repos:`` block.
137	    Every failure raises ``RepoConfigError`` naming the offending entry.
138	    """
139	    if value is None:
140	        return ()
141	    if not isinstance(value, list):
142	        raise RepoConfigError(
143	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} 'push_to_main_repos' must be a list"
144	        )
145	    entries: list[str] = []
146	    for entry in value:
147	        if not isinstance(entry, str) or not entry.strip():
148	            raise RepoConfigError(
149	                f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} 'push_to_main_repos' entry {entry!r}: "
150	                "must be a non-empty string"
151	            )
152	        entries.append(entry)
153	    return tuple(entries)
154	
155	
156	def load(path: Path | None = None) -> Repos:
157	    """Read ``repos.yml`` and return a validated ``Repos``.
158	
159	    Tilde-prefixed paths inside the file are expanded against ``$HOME``.
160	    Every failure mode raises ``RepoConfigError`` with a message that
161	    names the on-disk problem so the operator can fix it without
162	    re-reading source.
163	    """
164	    if path is None:
165	        path = default_config_path()
166	    if not path.exists():
167	        raise RepoConfigError(
168	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} not found at {path}\n{_MISSING_CONFIG_HINT}"
169	        )
170	    try:
171	        data = yaml.safe_load(path.read_text())
172	    except yaml.YAMLError as exc:
173	        raise RepoConfigError(
174	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} is not valid YAML: {exc}"
175	        ) from None
176	    if data is None:
177	        raise RepoConfigError(f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} is empty")
178	    if not isinstance(data, dict) or "repos" not in data:
179	        raise RepoConfigError(
180	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} must have a top-level 'repos:' key"
181	        )
182	    block = data["repos"]
183	    if not isinstance(block, dict) or not block:
184	        raise RepoConfigError(
185	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} 'repos:' must be a non-empty mapping"
186	        )
187	    mapping: dict[str, Path] = {}
188	    for name, value in block.items():
189	        if not isinstance(value, str):
190	            raise RepoConfigError(
191	                f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} entry {name!r}: path must be a string"
192	            )
193	        mapping[str(name)] = Path(value).expanduser()
194	    worktree_config_paths = _parse_worktree_config_paths(
195	        data.get("worktree_config_paths")
196	    )
197	    push_to_main_repos = _parse_push_to_main_repos(
198	        data.get("push_to_main_repos")
199	    )
200	    for name in push_to_main_repos:
201	        if name not in mapping:
202	            raise RepoConfigError(
203	                f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} 'push_to_main_repos' entry {name!r}: "
204	                "no such repo in the 'repos:' block"
205	            )
206	    return Repos(
207	        mapping=mapping,
208	        worktree_config_paths=worktree_config_paths,
209	        push_to_main_repos=push_to_main_repos,
210	    )
211	
```

1	"""Stop-hook guard for spawned drain workers.
2	
3	A ``claude -p`` session that loads a "report findings / await input" skill
4	can end its turn before the completion sequence (commit + handoff + Linear
5	comment + Done) runs. The session exits cleanly with the implementation
6	green but uncommitted, and the orchestrator only sees a generic not-Done
7	halt with no signal that the work was finished.
8	
9	This module is the Stop hook the worker session runs at end-of-turn. It
10	fires only when the orchestrator has planted ``.drain-guard.json`` in the
11	worktree root, so interactive sessions and non-drain runs are a silent
12	no-op. When the marker is present and the session is trying to stop with
13	work still uncaptured (dirty tree or, in stack mode, no
14	``.drain-handoff.json``), it returns ``decision: block`` to push the
15	agent through the completion sequence. After ``max_blocks`` re-injections
16	it gives up, writes ``.drain-guard-tripped`` with the observed state, and
17	lets the session exit — the orchestrator reads that marker and tags the
18	halt as ``worker_stopped_incomplete`` rather than the generic not-Done
19	line.
20	"""
21	from __future__ import annotations
22	
23	import json
24	import subprocess
25	import sys
26	from dataclasses import dataclass
27	from pathlib import Path
28	from typing import Literal, TextIO
29	
30	from .handoff import HANDOFF_FILE, read as _read_handoff
31	from .worktree import BASE_FILE
32	
33	MARKER_FILE = ".drain-guard.json"
34	TRIPPED_FILE = ".drain-guard-tripped"
35	DEFAULT_MAX_BLOCKS = 3
36	TRIPPED_HALT_REASON = "worker_stopped_incomplete"
37	
38	
39	@dataclass(frozen=True)
40	class GuardConfig:
41	    """State written by the orchestrator and updated by the hook.
42	
43	    ``mode`` is ``"stack"`` (handoff-driven assembly) or ``"push"`` (direct
44	    push to main). ``count`` is the number of times this hook has already
45	    returned ``block`` for the session — incremented in place. ``max_blocks``
46	    is the cap before the guard gives up.
47	    """
48	
49	    mode: str
50	    count: int
51	    max_blocks: int
52	
53	
54	def write_marker(
55	    worktree: Path,
56	    *,
57	    mode: Literal["stack", "push"],
58	    max_blocks: int = DEFAULT_MAX_BLOCKS,
59	) -> None:
60	    """Plant the per-session marker the hook keys on.
61	
62	    Called by the orchestrator immediately before spawning a worker. Replaces
63	    any prior marker so a resumed worktree starts fresh from ``count=0``.
64	    Also clears any stale tripped-marker from a previous attempt so the
65	    orchestrator's post-session read isn't a false positive.
66	    """
67	    (worktree / MARKER_FILE).write_text(
68	        json.dumps({"mode": mode, "count": 0, "max_blocks": max_blocks})
69	    )
70	    tripped = worktree / TRIPPED_FILE
71	    if tripped.exists():
72	        tripped.unlink()
73	
74	
75	def read_marker(worktree: Path) -> GuardConfig | None:
76	    """Return the guard state for ``worktree``, or ``None`` if absent/invalid."""
77	    path = worktree / MARKER_FILE
78	    try:
79	        payload = json.loads(path.read_text())
80	    except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError):
81	        return None
82	    if not isinstance(payload, dict):
83	        return None
84	    mode = payload.get("mode")
85	    count = payload.get("count")
86	    max_blocks = payload.get("max_blocks")
87	    if mode not in ("stack", "push"):
88	        return None
89	    if not isinstance(count, int) or not isinstance(max_blocks, int):
90	        return None
91	    return GuardConfig(mode=mode, count=count, max_blocks=max_blocks)
92	
93	
94	def _write_marker_count(worktree: Path, config: GuardConfig, *, count: int) -> None:
95	    (worktree / MARKER_FILE).write_text(
96	        json.dumps({"mode": config.mode, "count": count, "max_blocks": config.max_blocks})
97	    )
98	
99	
100	def read_tripped(worktree: Path) -> str | None:
101	    """Return the tripped reason for ``worktree``, or ``None`` if not tripped."""
102	    path = worktree / TRIPPED_FILE
103	    try:
104	        return path.read_text().strip() or None
105	    except OSError:
106	        return None
107	
108	
109	# drain-cycle's own per-session artefacts live at the worktree root and are
110	# expected to be untracked. ``_git_dirty`` filters them out so the guard
111	# doesn't see itself (or the handoff file written *after* the commit) as
112	# uncaptured work.
113	_OWN_ARTEFACTS = frozenset({MARKER_FILE, TRIPPED_FILE, HANDOFF_FILE, BASE_FILE})
114	
115	
116	def _git_dirty(worktree: Path) -> bool:
117	    """Return True if the worktree has uncommitted changes (excluding our own artefacts).
118	
119	    ``git status --porcelain`` lines are ``XY <path>`` (two status chars,
120	    a space, then the path). Untracked entries for our own artefacts —
121	    ``.drain-guard.json``, ``.drain-guard-tripped``, ``.drain-handoff.json``
122	    — are skipped: the handoff is written after the commit by design, and
123	    the guard's own markers are not the agent's work.
124	    """
125	    result = subprocess.run(
126	        ["git", "status", "--porcelain"],
127	        cwd=str(worktree),
128	        check=False,
129	        capture_output=True,
130	        text=True,
131	    )
132	    if result.returncode != 0:
133	        return False
134	    for line in result.stdout.splitlines():
135	        if len(line) < 4:
136	            continue
137	        path = line[3:].strip()
138	        if path in _OWN_ARTEFACTS:
139	            continue
140	        return True
141	    return False
142	
143	
144	def _has_handoff(worktree: Path) -> bool:
145	    return _read_handoff(worktree) is not None
146	
147	
148	@dataclass(frozen=True)
149	class Decision:
150	    """The hook's response to one Stop event.
151	
152	    ``block_reason`` is non-None when the agent should be re-prompted (the
153	    hook emits ``{"decision": "block", "reason": ...}``). ``tripped_reason``
154	    is non-None when the guard has given up and written ``TRIPPED_FILE`` —
155	    the hook then exits silently and the orchestrator surfaces the tripped
156	    state in the halt line.
157	    """
158	
159	    block_reason: str | None
160	    tripped_reason: str | None
161	
162	
163	_BLOCK_PROMPT_STACK = (
164	    "drain-cycle stop-guard: the issue is not finished — the worktree has "
165	    "uncommitted changes or no submitted PRs in .drain-handoff.json yet. "
166	    "Complete the remaining steps now: commit any pending changes to the "
167	    "issue branch as reviewable slices (do not push by hand), run "
168	    "`/shape:pr-finishing` to submit the stacked PR(s) — it writes the "
169	    "pr_urls into .drain-handoff.json and posts the review-summary comment "
170	    "— then transition the issue to Done. If you are genuinely blocked, "
171	    "leave the issue In Progress and post a comment naming the blocker — "
172	    "do not stop silently."
173	)
174	
175	_BLOCK_PROMPT_PUSH = (
176	    "drain-cycle stop-guard: the issue is not finished — the worktree has "
177	    "uncommitted changes. Complete the remaining steps now: commit and push "
178	    "to main, post a review-summary comment on the Linear issue, then "
179	    "transition the issue to Done. If you are genuinely blocked, leave the "
180	    "issue In Progress and post a comment naming the blocker — do not stop "
181	    "silently."
182	)
183	
184	
185	def evaluate(worktree: Path, *, stop_hook_active: bool) -> Decision:
186	    """Decide whether to block the Stop event, give up, or pass through.
187	
188	    The decision tree, in order:
189	
190	    * No marker → not a drain worker session. Pass through.
191	    * Stack mode + clean tree + valid handoff → completion sequence ran.
192	      Pass through.
193	    * Push mode + clean tree → completion sequence ran. Pass through.
194	    * Otherwise: incomplete. If ``count < max_blocks`` (and we're not
195	      already deep in a block-loop per Claude's ``stop_hook_active`` flag),
196	      increment and emit a block. If at the cap, write the tripped file
197	      and pass through so the session can exit cleanly.
198	
199	    ``stop_hook_active`` mirrors Claude's payload — when True the hook is
200	    being re-invoked from within a previous block, so the cap applies even
201	    if the count somehow lagged.
202	    """
203	    config = read_marker(worktree)
204	    if config is None:
205	        return Decision(block_reason=None, tripped_reason=None)
206	
207	    dirty = _git_dirty(worktree)
208	    handoff_ok = _has_handoff(worktree) if config.mode == "stack" else True
209	    complete = (not dirty) and handoff_ok
210	    if complete:
211	        return Decision(block_reason=None, tripped_reason=None)
212	
213	    state_summary = _describe_incomplete(config.mode, dirty=dirty, handoff_ok=handoff_ok)
214	
215	    if config.count >= config.max_blocks or (stop_hook_active and config.count > 0):
216	        reason = f"{state_summary} after {config.count} re-injection(s)"
217	        (worktree / TRIPPED_FILE).write_text(reason)
218	        return Decision(block_reason=None, tripped_reason=reason)
219	
220	    _write_marker_count(worktree, config, count=config.count + 1)
221	    prompt = _BLOCK_PROMPT_STACK if config.mode == "stack" else _BLOCK_PROMPT_PUSH
222	    return Decision(block_reason=prompt, tripped_reason=None)
223	
224	
225	def _describe_incomplete(mode: str, *, dirty: bool, handoff_ok: bool) -> str:
226	    parts = []
227	    if dirty:
228	        parts.append("uncommitted changes in worktree")
229	    if mode == "stack" and not handoff_ok:
230	        parts.append("no valid .drain-handoff.json")
231	    return ", ".join(parts) or "incomplete state"
232	
233	
234	def run(stdin: TextIO, stdout: TextIO, *, cwd: Path | None = None) -> int:
235	    """Read the Stop hook payload from ``stdin`` and emit a decision on ``stdout``.
236	
237	    Exit code 0 always; the decision is carried in the JSON body. Errors
238	    parsing the payload fall through to a silent pass — a broken hook must
239	    not block a legitimate stop.
240	    """
241	    try:
242	        payload = json.load(stdin)
243	    except (json.JSONDecodeError, ValueError):
244	        payload = {}
245	    stop_hook_active = bool(payload.get("stop_hook_active"))
246	    worktree = cwd if cwd is not None else Path(payload.get("cwd") or ".").resolve()
247	
248	    decision = evaluate(worktree, stop_hook_active=stop_hook_active)
249	    if decision.block_reason is not None:
250	        json.dump({"decision": "block", "reason": decision.block_reason}, stdout)
251	    return 0
252	
253	
254	def main() -> None:
255	    sys.exit(run(sys.stdin, sys.stdout))
256	
```

1	"""Per-cycle run-log artefact.
2	
3	Each ``drain-cycle`` invocation produces a single JSON file at
4	``~/.drain-cycle/runs/<cycle-id>-<run-timestamp>.json`` capturing one
5	entry per attempted issue. The filename embeds the run-start timestamp
6	(UTC, ``%Y%m%dT%H%M%S%fZ``) so re-running ``drain-cycle`` against the
7	same cycle writes a new file instead of clobbering the prior one.
8	Downstream consumers glob the directory and group by ``cycle_id``
9	(carried inside each file).
10	
11	* KR1 (completion %) is computed from ``entries[].final_linear_state``
12	  merged across every file sharing the same ``cycle_id``.
13	* Cycle-drain self-grading reads every file across cycles.
14	* The kill condition (KR1 < 50%) merges per-cycle then thresholds.
15	
16	Schema:
17	
18	::
19	
20	    {
21	      "cycle_id":               "<linear-cycle-uuid>",
22	      "cycle_duration_seconds": <float>,
23	      "cycle_cost_usd":         <float>,
24	      "cycle_tokens_cumulative": <int>,
25	      "cycle_halt_reason":      null | "<cycle-budget halt line>",
26	      "entries":                [
27	        {
28	          "issue_identifier":   "ABA-NNN",
29	          "started_at":         "<iso-8601 UTC>",
30	          "finished_at":        "<iso-8601 UTC>",
31	          "exit_code":          <int>,
32	          "final_linear_state": "Done" | "Todo" | ...,
33	          "worktree_path":      "<absolute path>",
34	          "halt_reason":        null | "<orchestrator stderr halt line>",
35	          "duration_seconds":   <float>,
36	          "model":              null | "<resolved model id>",
37	          "usage":              null | {
38	            "input_tokens":               <int>,
39	            "output_tokens":              <int>,
40	            "cache_creation_input_tokens": <int>,
41	            "cache_read_input_tokens":    <int>,
42	            "cumulative":                 <int>,
43	            "peak_context":               <int>,
44	          },
45	          "cost_usd":           null | <float>,
46	          "num_turns":          null | <int>,
47	          "session_id":         null | "<uuid>",
48	          "is_error":           null | <bool>,
49	          "flow":               null | "verify",
50	          "outcome_verdict":    null | {"result": "pass"|"fail", "findings": [...], "invoked_at": "<iso-8601>"},
51	          "prep_verdict":       null | {"result": "<str>", "route": "human-review"|"auto-merge", "reasoning": "<str>"},
52	          "responder_runs":     [],
53	          "shape_task_invoked": null | <bool>,
54	        },
55	        ...
56	      ],
57	    }
58	
59	``halt_reason`` is ``null`` on Done entries unless worktree teardown
60	failed after the session completed — in that case the Done entry carries
61	the teardown error string and the drain continues. On the orchestrator's
62	halt entry it is the exact string also written to stderr — both surfaces
63	are produced by the same ``_halt_message`` helper in ``orchestrator.py``
64	so the on-disk and terminal values cannot drift. Non-last entries are
65	``null`` by construction: the orchestrator returns on first halt, so
66	anything before the halt is a Done entry.
67	
68	The worker-usage fields (``model`` through ``is_error``) are populated
69	from the spawned session's stream-json events — see ``worker.py``.
70	Entries written before any session runs (pre-spawn resolution and
71	setup-failure halts) carry ``null`` for ``model`` / ``usage`` /
72	``cost_usd`` / ``num_turns`` / ``session_id`` / ``is_error``; every entry
73	keeps the same key set. ``usage.cumulative`` is the billed-token total
74	across turns; ``usage.peak_context`` is the largest single-turn context.
75	These fields are additive — ``grade.py`` reads only ``cycle_id`` and
76	``entries[].final_linear_state`` / ``exit_code``, so existing run logs
77	without them grade unchanged.
78	
79	``cycle_cost_usd`` and ``cycle_tokens_cumulative`` are per-invocation
80	totals over ``entries`` (entries with ``null`` cost or usage contribute
81	zero), recomputed on every persist alongside ``cycle_duration_seconds``.
82	
83	``cycle_halt_reason`` is ``null`` unless the orchestrator stopped the run
84	because a *cycle-wide* cap (tokens / cost / wall-clock) was breached — the
85	death-by-aggregate case where every issue stayed under its own per-issue
86	caps but their sum crossed the cycle budget. The breaching issue's own
87	entry is recorded normally (it ran to Done); this top-level field is the
88	record of why no further issue was attempted, carrying the same ``Halt:``
89	string printed to stderr. A per-issue breach, by contrast, lands in the
90	halting entry's ``halt_reason`` like any other halt.
91	
92	``cycle_duration_seconds`` is computed automatically on every persist as
93	``max(finished_at) - min(started_at)`` across ``entries`` (``0.0`` when
94	empty). This is the KR2 grading proxy — "how long was the agent doing
95	execution for me unattended" stands in for "operator hands-off time" —
96	and replaces the originally specified ``time_spent`` block, which
97	required manual self-report at cycle close and was never going to be
98	filled in.
99	
100	Persistence is incremental: ``append_entry`` re-serialises the whole dict
101	on every call, so a mid-run crash still leaves a well-formed file with
102	every issue attempted so far. Cycle scale (≤ 15 issues) makes the cost
103	irrelevant.
104	"""
105	from __future__ import annotations
106	
107	import json
108	from dataclasses import dataclass, field
109	from datetime import datetime, timezone
110	from pathlib import Path
111	from typing import Any
112	
113	
114	def runs_dir() -> Path:
115	    """Return the directory where per-cycle run logs are written.
116	
117	    Resolved on every call (not module-import time) so tests can redirect
118	    via ``monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", ...)`` after the module is imported.
119	    """
120	    return Path.home() / ".drain-cycle" / "runs"
121	
122	
123	@dataclass
124	class RunLog:
125	    """Per-cycle run-log file. Construct once at orchestrator start.
126	
127	    Writing the initial ``{cycle_id, cycle_duration_seconds: 0.0,
128	    entries: []}`` shell in ``__post_init__`` means that even a zero-issue
129	    cycle (or a crash before the first ``append_entry``) leaves the
130	    artefact on disk, which is what kill-condition tooling needs to
131	    distinguish "drained nothing" from "never ran".
132	
133	    The filename embeds a UTC run-start timestamp with microsecond
134	    resolution, so two ``RunLog`` instances on the same ``cycle_id``
135	    (re-running ``drain-cycle`` after a halt) write to two separate
136	    files instead of one clobbering the other.
137	    """
138	
139	    cycle_id: str
140	    path: Path = field(init=False)
141	    entries: list[dict[str, Any]] = field(default_factory=list, init=False)
142	    cycle_halt_reason: str | None = field(default=None, init=False)
143	
144	    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
145	        directory = runs_dir()
146	        directory.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
147	        run_started_at = datetime.now(tz=timezone.utc)
148	        timestamp = run_started_at.strftime("%Y%m%dT%H%M%S%fZ")
149	        self.path = directory / f"{self.cycle_id}-{timestamp}.json"
150	        self._persist()
151	
152	    def append_entry(
153	        self,
154	        *,
155	        issue_identifier: str,
156	        started_at: str,
157	        finished_at: str,
158	        exit_code: int,
159	        final_linear_state: str,
160	        worktree_path: str,
161	        halt_reason: str | None = None,
162	        duration_seconds: float | None = None,
163	        model: str | None = None,
164	        usage: dict[str, int] | None = None,
165	        cost_usd: float | None = None,
166	        num_turns: int | None = None,
167	        session_id: str | None = None,
168	        is_error: bool | None = None,
169	        flow: str | None = None,
170	        outcome_verdict: dict[str, Any] | None = None,
171	        prep_verdict: dict[str, Any] | None = None,
172	        responder_runs: list[dict[str, Any]] | None = None,
173	        shape_task_invoked: bool | None = None,
174	    ) -> None:
175	        if duration_seconds is None:
176	            duration_seconds = (
177	                datetime.fromisoformat(finished_at)
178	                - datetime.fromisoformat(started_at)
179	            ).total_seconds()
180	        self.entries.append(
181	            {
182	                "issue_identifier": issue_identifier,
183	                "started_at": started_at,
184	                "finished_at": finished_at,
185	                "exit_code": exit_code,
186	                "final_linear_state": final_linear_state,
187	                "worktree_path": worktree_path,
188	                "halt_reason": halt_reason,
189	                "duration_seconds": duration_seconds,
190	                "model": model,
191	                "usage": usage,
192	                "cost_usd": cost_usd,
193	                "num_turns": num_turns,
194	                "session_id": session_id,
195	                "is_error": is_error,
196	                "flow": flow,
197	                "outcome_verdict": outcome_verdict,
198	                "prep_verdict": prep_verdict,
199	                "responder_runs": responder_runs if responder_runs is not None else [],
200	                "shape_task_invoked": shape_task_invoked,
201	            }
202	        )
203	        self._persist()
204	
205	    def debug_path(self, issue_identifier: str) -> Path:
206	        """Path for one issue's opt-in ``--debug-file`` capture, beside the log.
207	
208	        Named ``<run-log-stem>-<issue-identifier>.debug.log`` so each issue's
209	        startup diagnostics sit next to the run log they belong to and the
210	        run-start timestamp keeps re-runs from clobbering a prior capture.
211	        Only written when debug capture is enabled — see ``orchestrator.run``
212	        and ``docs/design-decisions.md`` §10.
213	        """
214	        return self.path.with_name(f"{self.path.stem}-{issue_identifier}.debug.log")
215	
216	    def set_cycle_halt(self, reason: str) -> None:
217	        """Record why a cycle-wide cap stopped the run, and persist.
218	
219	        Called by the orchestrator after the breaching issue's own entry is
220	        already written, so the file carries both the issue's normal entry
221	        and the cycle-level reason no further issue was attempted.
222	        """
223	        self.cycle_halt_reason = reason
224	        self._persist()
225	
226	    def cycle_duration_seconds(self) -> float:
227	        if not self.entries:
228	            return 0.0
229	        starts = [datetime.fromisoformat(e["started_at"]) for e in self.entries]
230	        finishes = [datetime.fromisoformat(e["finished_at"]) for e in self.entries]
231	        return (max(finishes) - min(starts)).total_seconds()
232	
233	    def cycle_cost_usd(self) -> float:
234	        return sum(
235	            e["cost_usd"] for e in self.entries if e.get("cost_usd") is not None
236	        )
237	
238	    def cycle_tokens_cumulative(self) -> int:
239	        return sum(
240	            e["usage"]["cumulative"]
241	            for e in self.entries
242	            if e.get("usage") is not None
243	        )
244	
245	    def _persist(self) -> None:
246	        payload = {
247	            "cycle_id": self.cycle_id,
248	            "cycle_duration_seconds": self.cycle_duration_seconds(),
249	            "cycle_cost_usd": self.cycle_cost_usd(),
250	            "cycle_tokens_cumulative": self.cycle_tokens_cumulative(),
251	            "cycle_halt_reason": self.cycle_halt_reason,
252	            "entries": self.entries,
253	        }
254	        self.path.write_text(json.dumps(payload, indent=2) + "\n")
255	
```
