```
1	"""Per-issue and per-cycle resource guardrails.
2	
3	A spawned ``claude -p`` worker can run away — burning tokens, wall-clock,
4	and quota with no ceiling but the outer per-issue timeout. This module
5	defines the thresholds that bound that spend and the ``Breach`` value that
6	names which one was crossed.
7	
8	**Two layers, belt and suspenders.**
9	
10	* *Native belt* — ``per_issue_cost_usd`` is handed to ``claude`` as
11	  ``--max-budget-usd`` so the session self-terminates on cost without the
12	  orchestrator having to watch it.
13	* *Orchestrator suspenders* — ``per_issue_tokens`` and ``per_issue_seconds``
14	  are enforced by the worker against the live event stream: when either is
15	  crossed the worker SIGKILLs the session's process group (see
16	  ``worker.py``). The ``cycle_*`` caps are enforced by the orchestrator
17	  between issues via ``check_cycle`` — death-by-aggregate, where every issue
18	  is individually under its cap but their sum drains the quota, is exactly
19	  what the per-issue caps cannot catch.
20	
21	Token count is the primary guardrail (a subscription user pays in tokens,
22	not dollars); cost rides alongside it.
23	
24	**On/off per guardrail.** Any threshold may be ``None`` — that guardrail is
25	off. The defaults below are all live; an operator turns one off by setting
26	its key to ``null`` in ``~/.drain-cycle/limits.yml``.
27	
28	**Load precedence.** Baked-in defaults, overlaid by ``limits.yml`` if it
29	exists: a key absent from the file keeps its default, an explicit ``null``
30	turns the guardrail off, a positive number overrides it. A present-but-
31	malformed file raises ``LimitsConfigError`` rather than silently falling
32	back to defaults — a typo that quietly disabled a cap the operator believed
33	was active is worse than a loud startup halt. The defaults are deliberately
34	generous starting points; recalibrate against real run-log spend.
35	"""
36	from __future__ import annotations
37	
38	from dataclasses import dataclass, fields
39	from pathlib import Path
40	from typing import Any
41	
42	import yaml
43	
44	_CONFIG_DISPLAY = "~/.drain-cycle/limits.yml"
45	
46	
47	class LimitsConfigError(RuntimeError):
48	    """``limits.yml`` exists but is malformed."""
49	
50	
51	@dataclass(frozen=True)
52	class Breach:
53	    """A crossed guardrail, recorded into the halting run-log entry.
54	
55	    ``scope`` is ``"per-issue"`` or ``"cycle"``; ``metric`` is ``"token"``,
56	    ``"time"``, or ``"cost"``. ``limit`` is the cap that was set and
57	    ``observed`` the value at the moment of the breach. ``describe`` renders
58	    the operator-facing line — the worker uses it for a per-issue kill and
59	    the orchestrator for a cycle stop, so the wording cannot drift between
60	    the two surfaces.
61	    """
62	
63	    scope: str
64	    metric: str
65	    limit: float
66	    observed: float
67	
68	    def describe(self) -> str:
69	        return f"{self.scope} {self.metric} cap exceeded: {self._detail()}"
70	
71	    def _detail(self) -> str:
72	        if self.metric == "cost":
73	            return f"${self.observed:,.2f} ≥ ${self.limit:,.2f}"
74	        if self.metric == "time":
75	            return f"{self.observed:,.0f}s ≥ {self.limit:,.0f}s"
76	        return f"{self.observed:,.0f} ≥ {self.limit:,.0f} tokens"
77	
78	
79	@dataclass(frozen=True)
80	class Limits:
81	    """Resource caps. ``None`` on any field turns that guardrail off.
82	
83	    Defaults: per-issue 8M tokens · 20 min · $15; cycle 30M tokens ·
84	    90 min · $60.
85	
86	    ``max_resume_attempts`` is a *policy* cap, not a runtime guardrail:
87	    it bounds how many times the orchestrator will spawn a worker for a
88	    halted issue across re-runs before refusing further attempts
89	    (default 3). ``None`` removes the cap so a perma-stuck issue keeps
90	    being retried on every re-run. No ``Breach`` is raised when it
91	    fires — the cap is checked pre-spawn against the run-log history.
92	    """
93	
94	    per_issue_tokens: int | None = 8_000_000
95	    per_issue_seconds: float | None = 20 * 60
96	    per_issue_cost_usd: float | None = 15.0
97	    cycle_tokens: int | None = 30_000_000
98	    cycle_seconds: float | None = 90 * 60
99	    cycle_cost_usd: float | None = 60.0
100	    max_resume_attempts: int | None = 3
101	
102	
103	def default_config_path() -> Path:
104	    """Resolved per call so tests can redirect via ``monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", ...)``."""
105	    return Path.home() / ".drain-cycle" / "limits.yml"
106	
107	
108	def load(path: Path | None = None) -> Limits:
109	    """Read ``limits.yml`` over the baked-in defaults.
110	
111	    A missing or empty file yields the defaults unchanged — ``limits.yml``
112	    is optional. A present file maps limit names to positive numbers (or
113	    ``null`` to disable that guardrail); unknown keys, non-numeric values,
114	    booleans, and non-positive numbers each raise ``LimitsConfigError`` with
115	    a message naming the on-disk problem.
116	    """
117	    defaults = Limits()
118	    if path is None:
119	        path = default_config_path()
120	    if not path.exists():
121	        return defaults
122	    try:
123	        data = yaml.safe_load(path.read_text())
124	    except yaml.YAMLError as exc:
125	        raise LimitsConfigError(f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} is not valid YAML: {exc}") from None
126	    if data is None:
127	        return defaults
128	    if not isinstance(data, dict):
129	        raise LimitsConfigError(
130	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} must be a mapping of limit names to numbers"
131	        )
132	
133	    valid = [f.name for f in fields(Limits)]
134	    unknown = set(data) - set(valid)
135	    if unknown:
136	        raise LimitsConfigError(
137	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} has unknown keys: {', '.join(sorted(unknown))}; "
138	            f"valid keys: {', '.join(valid)}"
139	        )
140	
141	    values: dict[str, Any] = {}
142	    for name in valid:
143	        if name not in data:
144	            values[name] = getattr(defaults, name)
145	            continue
146	        values[name] = _coerce(name, data[name])
147	    return Limits(**values)
148	
149	
150	def _coerce(name: str, raw: Any) -> float | None:
151	    """Validate one ``limits.yml`` value: ``None`` (off) or a positive number."""
152	    if raw is None:
153	        return None
154	    # ``bool`` is a subclass of ``int``; ``per_issue_tokens: true`` would
155	    # otherwise slip through as ``1`` and silently arm an absurd cap.
156	    if isinstance(raw, bool) or not isinstance(raw, (int, float)) or raw <= 0:
157	        raise LimitsConfigError(
158	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} entry {name!r}: must be a positive number "
159	            f"or null (got {raw!r})"
160	        )
161	    return raw
162	
163	
164	def check_cycle(
165	    limits: Limits, *, tokens: int, cost_usd: float, seconds: float
166	) -> Breach | None:
167	    """Return the first cycle-wide cap the running totals breach, else ``None``.
168	
169	    Tokens are checked first (the primary guardrail), then cost, then
170	    wall-clock. A guardrail set to ``None`` is skipped.
171	    """
172	    if limits.cycle_tokens is not None and tokens >= limits.cycle_tokens:
173	        return Breach("cycle", "token", limits.cycle_tokens, tokens)
174	    if limits.cycle_cost_usd is not None and cost_usd >= limits.cycle_cost_usd:
175	        return Breach("cycle", "cost", limits.cycle_cost_usd, cost_usd)
176	    if limits.cycle_seconds is not None and seconds >= limits.cycle_seconds:
177	        return Breach("cycle", "time", limits.cycle_seconds, seconds)
178	    return None
179	
```

> AGENT

I now have a complete picture of the codebase. Let me give you the architecture from the API-interaction angle, then stress-test the design.

## What the program is, in one breath

`drain-cycle` is a CLI that drains a Linear cycle: it fetches the cycle's pending issues, topologically sorts them by their blocks-graph, and for each one spawns an isolated `claude -p` worker in a git worktree, watches its token/time/cost spend, then reads Linear back to decide Done vs. halt. Everything is a **synchronous, single-threaded loop** (one helper thread per worker for stream parsing). No async, no daemon, no queue.

## The dependency graph (who calls whom)

```
cli.main()                         ← the only real entry point
  ├─ _load_secrets()               (dotenv)
  ├─ telemetry.setup()
  ├─ repos.load()      → Repos     ─┐ eager config validation,
  ├─ limits.load()     → Limits    ─┘ before any Linear traffic
  └─ orchestrator.run(repos, limits, watch, no_stack)
        │
        ├─ linear.current_cycle_id()          → str
        ├─ linear.pending_issues(cycle_id)    → ExecutionPlan (topo sort lives here)
        │
        └─ for each issue:  _drain_one_issue(...)
              ├─ repos.resolve(issue)          → Path      (repo: label → disk)
              ├─ model.resolve(issue)          → str       (model: label → model id)
              ├─ flow.resolve(issue)           → "verify"|None
              ├─ prompt.build(issue, ...)      → str       (the entire agent contract)
              ├─ worktree.ensure/add/link_project_config
              ├─ linear.set_state(id, "In Progress")
              ├─ stop_guard.write_marker(worktree, mode)
              ├─ worker.run_issue(...)         → WorkerResult   ← spawns claude, parses stream
              ├─ linear.get_issue(id)          → re-read state
              ├─ handoff.read(worktree)        → HandoffData|None  (stack-mode PR proof)
              ├─ worktree.merge_entire_sessions / remove
              ├─ runlog.append_entry(...)      (incremental persist every call)
              └─ grade_draft.write_draft_from_entry(...)
```

Crosscutting leaf modules everyone imports but which import almost nothing: `telemetry`, `console`, `progress`, `limits`.

The dependency direction is clean — it's a **layered DAG with no cycles**. `cli → orchestrator → {linear, worker, worktree, prompt, repos, model, flow, handoff, stop_guard, runlog}`. The leaves don't reach back up. That's the single biggest structural win here.

## How you'd drive it from an interactive Python session

The module API is genuinely usable in a REPL — that's a good sign of decoupling. From `python` with the venv active:

```python
from drain_cycle import linear, repos, limits, model, flow, prompt, worktree, worker

# 1. Config (validates eagerly, raises RepoConfigError/LimitsConfigError)
loaded_repos  = repos.load()      # reads ~/.drain-cycle/repos.yml
loaded_limits = limits.load()     # reads ~/.drain-cycle/limits.yml

# 2. Talk to Linear directly (needs LINEAR_API_KEY in env)
cycle_id = linear.current_cycle_id()
plan = linear.pending_issues(cycle_id)     # ExecutionPlan(order=[...], deferred=[...])
issue = plan.order[0]

# 3. Pure resolvers — no side effects, trivially testable in isolation
repo_path = loaded_repos.resolve(issue)    # Path
m         = model.resolve(issue)           # "claude-sonnet-4-6"
f         = flow.resolve(issue)            # None or "verify"
text      = prompt.build(issue, repo_path / ".worktrees" / issue["identifier"],
                         stack=True, base="main")   # the agent prompt, as a string

# 4. Side-effecting steps, each callable standalone
handle = worktree.ensure(repo_path, issue["identifier"], "main")
result = worker.run_issue(
    claude_cmd=["claude", "-p", "--dangerously-skip-permissions"],
    model=m, prompt=text, cwd=handle.path,
    token_limit=loaded_limits.per_issue_tokens,
    time_limit_seconds=loaded_limits.per_issue_seconds,
    cost_limit_usd=loaded_limits.per_issue_cost_usd,
)                                          # WorkerResult dataclass

# 5. The whole loop
from drain_cycle import orchestrator
exit_code = orchestrator.run(loaded_repos, loaded_limits)
```

Two things to note about *how it's meant to be invoked*:

- **The "API" is split into pure resolvers and effectful actions.** `model.resolve`, `flow.resolve`, `repos.resolve`, `prompt.build`, `linear._plan` are all pure functions of an issue dict — you can call them with a hand-built `{"labels": [...], "title": ...}` and assert the output. This is why the test suite is so large and granular (40+ test files mostly hitting one module each).
- **The issue dict is the universal currency.** Every resolver takes `dict[str, Any]` shaped like Linear's flattened wire format (`labels: list[str]`, `state: {type, name}`, `blockers: [...]`). This is the implicit contract gluing the whole system together.

---

## Stress test: separation of concerns & cohesion

Here's where it gets interesting. The design is **mostly very coherent**, with a few real seams worth naming.

### What's strong

1. **High cohesion in the leaf modules.** `linear.py` is *only* GraphQL. `worktree.py` is *only* `git worktree`. `handoff.py` is *only* the `.drain-handoff.json` artefact. `limits.py` is *only* thresholds + the `Breach` value. Each has one reason to change. Textbook.

2. **The worker/orchestrator split is the cleanest boundary in the codebase.** `worker.py`'s docstring states it explicitly: *"The orchestrator owns the cycle loop and the Linear lifecycle; this module owns one session."* `worker.run_issue` knows nothing about Linear, worktrees, run-logs, or issues — it takes a prompt string and a cwd, returns a `WorkerResult`. You could lift it into a different product unchanged.

3. **Error-type-as-control-flow done well.** `RepoConfigError` (startup, no cycle to log against) vs `RepoResolutionError` (per-issue, write a halt entry) cleanly map to the two halt paths. Same with `Breach.scope` distinguishing per-issue kill (worker) from cycle stop (orchestrator), sharing one `describe()` so the wording can't drift between surfaces.

4. **Single source of truth for crosscutting strings.** `_halt_message` feeds both stderr and the run-log `halt_reason`; `Breach.describe()` feeds both the worker kill and the cycle stop. This is deliberate anti-drift design and it's done consistently.

### Where the design strains

**1. `orchestrator.py` is a god module (907 lines, 38KB) — this is the real cohesion weakness.** It carries at least four distinct responsibilities that don't share a reason to change:

- the cycle loop + per-issue lifecycle (its core job)
- the **entire tmux/FIFO watch-pane subsystem** (`_open_watch_pane`, `_open_fifo_stream`, `_watch_formatter_stage`, `_cleanup_fifo`, `_close_watch_pane` — ~120 lines of pty/FIFO plumbing)
- the resume-attempt accounting (`_resume_attempts` globbing run-log files — that's arguably runlog's job)
- halt-entry assembly, repeated nearly verbatim across **five branches** (repo-resolution, resume-cap, setup-failure, per-issue breach, stack-no-PRs, not-Done)

The watch-pane code is the clearest extraction candidate: it's a self-contained concern (`watch.py`) with a narrow interface — give it an argv and a cwd, get back `(stream, kill_fn, pane_id)` or `None`. Right now it's interleaved with lifecycle logic in `_drain_one_issue`, which is why that function is ~480 lines.

**2. The five `log.append_entry(...)` calls are a DRY/coupling smell.** Each halt branch hand-assembles an entry with the same ~12 fields, differing only in `final_linear_state`, `halt_reason`, and `exit_code`. `_worker_log_fields` was extracted to fight this for the usage fields — but the *envelope* is still copy-pasted six times. A `record_halt(...)` helper or a small `HaltContext` would collapse this. As written, adding one run-log field means editing six call sites correctly (the docstring even has to promise "every entry keeps the same key set" because nothing enforces it).

**3. `prompt.py` encodes the agent's completion *protocol* as prose — and that protocol is duplicated in `stop_guard.py`.** The stack-mode completion sequence ("commit slices → `/shape:pr-finishing` → confirm `pr_urls` → Done") lives as numbered English in `prompt._stack_preamble`, *and* as a block-reason string in `stop_guard._BLOCK_PROMPT_STACK`, *and* as the actual check in `stop_guard.evaluate` (`dirty` + `handoff_ok`). Three representations of one contract. They're consistent today, but there's no shared definition forcing them to stay so — change the completion sequence and you must remember all three. This is the weakest cohesion point conceptually, even though each file individually is clean.

**4. The "issue dict" is an unsealed contract.** Everything depends on the shape `linear.pending_issues` produces (`labels` flattened to `list[str]`, `state.type`/`state.name`, `blockers`), but it's an untyped `dict[str, Any]` threaded through eight modules. A `@dataclass Issue` would turn a dozen `issue.get("labels", [])` guards into a typed field and make the resolvers' contracts explicit. Right now the contract is enforced only by convention and tests.

**5. Minor leak: `worker.build_argv` is called from two places** — inside `worker.run_issue` (spawn path) and from `orchestrator._drain_one_issue` (watch path, to feed the tmux pane). That's a deliberate sharing, and the docstring justifies it, but it means the orchestrator reaches into the worker's argv-construction. It's the price of the watch-mode design where the *pane* owns the process instead of the worker. Acceptable, but it's the one spot where the otherwise-clean worker boundary is porous.

### Net assessment

The **module-level separation is genuinely good** — clean DAG, single-responsibility leaves, pure-vs-effectful split, REPL-drivable. The coherence problem is concentrated almost entirely in **one place: `orchestrator.py` is doing too much**, and the consequence is the repeated halt-entry assembly and the embedded watch subsystem. If you extracted `watch.py` and a `record_halt` helper, the orchestrator would drop from ~900 to maybe ~500 lines and each remaining function would have one job.

The subtler risk is the **completion-protocol triplication** across `prompt`, `stop_guard`, and the orchestrator's handoff gate — that's not a file-boundary problem, it's a missing shared abstraction for "what does Done mean in stack mode."

Want me to sketch the `watch.py` extraction or a `record_halt`/`HaltContext` refactor concretely? Either is a low-risk, high-readability win that the test suite (which already targets these branches individually) would catch regressions on.

```
1	"""Watch mode: tmux pane lifecycle and the claude-in-pane FIFO wiring.
2	
3	In watch mode the pane *is* the claude session: the orchestrator runs
4	``claude ... | tee <fifo> | <formatter>`` in a split pane and reads the same
5	stream-json bytes off the FIFO. The formatter renders the pane copy
6	human-readable; ``tee`` stays upstream so the FIFO branch is byte-for-byte
7	unaffected. These tests mock tmux; where the external (pane) path is
8	meant to succeed, a fake-pane writer thread opens the FIFO and streams a
9	``result`` event (standing in for the pane's claude), so the orchestrator
10	takes the FIFO path rather than spawning a subprocess.
11	
12	Tests cover:
13	- watch=True drives claude through the pane (``| tee <fifo>``); no watch log
14	  file is left behind
15	- non-tmux path: no tmux subprocess spawned, drain falls back and completes
16	- tmux failure swallowed (drain falls back to a normal spawn)
17	- pane opened before each session, prior pane killed before the next issue,
18	  final pane left open — using the ID split-window returned
19	- FIFO startup timeout: pane torn down, drain falls back to a normal spawn
20	"""
21	from __future__ import annotations
22	
23	import json
24	import shlex
25	import subprocess
26	import threading
27	from pathlib import Path
28	from typing import Any
29	
30	import pytest
31	
32	from drain_cycle import linear, orchestrator, repos
33	
34	
35	def _issue(
36	    identifier: str,
37	    sort_order: float,
38	    *,
39	    repo_name: str = "test-repo",
40	) -> dict:
41	    return {
42	        "id": f"id-{identifier}",
43	        "identifier": identifier,
44	        "title": f"Title for {identifier}",
45	        "description": f"Body for {identifier}",
46	        "sortOrder": sort_order,
47	        "state": {"type": "unstarted", "name": "Todo"},
48	        "labels": [f"repo:{repo_name}"],
49	    }
50	
51	
52	def _init_repo(repo: Path) -> None:
53	    subprocess.run(["git", "init", "-b", "main"], cwd=repo, check=True, capture_output=True)
54	    subprocess.run(["git", "config", "user.email", "test@example.com"], cwd=repo, check=True)
55	    subprocess.run(["git", "config", "user.name", "Test"], cwd=repo, check=True)
56	    (repo / "README.md").write_text("seed\n")
57	    subprocess.run(["git", "add", "."], cwd=repo, check=True, capture_output=True)
58	    subprocess.run(["git", "commit", "-m", "seed"], cwd=repo, check=True, capture_output=True)
59	
60	
61	def _write_done_script(tmp_path: Path, done_marker: Path) -> Path:

63	    script.write_text(
64	        "#!/bin/sh\n"
65	        f'printf "%s\\n" "$(basename "$PWD")" >> "{done_marker}"\n'
66	    )
67	    script.chmod(0o755)

71	def _completed(marker: Path) -> set[str]:
72	    if not marker.exists():
73	        return set()
74	    return {line for line in marker.read_text().splitlines() if line}
75	
76	
77	def _setup_linear_stubs(monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch, raw_issues: list[dict], done_marker: Path) -> None:
78	    issues_by_id = {i["id"]: i for i in raw_issues}
79	
80	    def fake_pending_issues(cycle_id: str):
81	        completed = _completed(done_marker)
82	        return linear._plan([i for i in raw_issues if i["identifier"] not in completed])
83	
84	    def fake_get_issue(issue_id: str) -> dict:
85	        issue = issues_by_id[issue_id]
86	        if issue["identifier"] in _completed(done_marker):
87	            return {**issue, "state": {"type": "completed", "name": "Done"}}
88	        return issue
89	
90	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "current_cycle_id", lambda: "stub-cycle-id")
91	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "pending_issues", fake_pending_issues)
92	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "get_issue", fake_get_issue)
93	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "set_state", lambda issue_id, state_name: None)
94	
95	
96	def _fifo_from_pipeline(pipeline: str) -> str:
97	    """Pull the FIFO path out of a ``... | tee <fifo>; exec ...`` pane command.
98	
99	    The shell separates ``tee <fifo>`` from the trailing ``exec`` on the ``;``;
100	    ``shlex.split`` doesn't treat ``;`` as a separator, so the fifo token keeps
101	    a trailing ``;`` when no space precedes it — strip it back off."""
102	    tokens = shlex.split(pipeline)
103	    return tokens[tokens.index("tee") + 1].rstrip(";")
104	
105	
106	def _make_fake_tmux(
107	    tmux_calls: list[list[str]],
108	    pane_ids: list[int],
109	    *,
110	    done_marker: Path | None,
111	) -> Any:
112	    """A tmux stand-in that captures calls and, when ``done_marker`` is given,
113	    simulates the pane's claude by writing a ``result`` event into the FIFO and
114	    marking the issue done — so the orchestrator takes the external FIFO path.
115	
116	    With ``done_marker=None`` the pane is "opened" but never writes, so the
117	    FIFO read times out and the orchestrator falls back to a normal spawn.
118	    """
119	    real_run = subprocess.run
120	
121	    def fake_tmux(args: Any, **kwargs: Any) -> Any:
122	        if not (isinstance(args, list) and args and args[0] == "tmux"):
123	            return real_run(args, **kwargs)
124	        tmux_calls.append(list(args))
125	        if len(args) > 1 and args[1] == "split-window":
126	            pane_ids[0] += 1
127	            pane = f"%{pane_ids[0]}"
128	            if done_marker is not None:
129	                pipeline = args[-1]
130	                fifo = _fifo_from_pipeline(pipeline)
131	                cwd = args[args.index("-c") + 1]
132	                identifier = Path(cwd).name
133	                _spawn_fake_pane(fifo, identifier, done_marker)
134	            return type("R", (), {"stdout": f"{pane}\n", "returncode": 0})()
135	        return type("R", (), {"stdout": "", "returncode": 0})()
136	
137	    return fake_tmux
138	
139	
140	def _spawn_fake_pane(fifo: str, identifier: str, done_marker: Path) -> None:
141	    """Open the FIFO and stream a single ``result`` event, then mark the issue
142	    done before closing (so the marker is set before the reader sees EOF)."""
143	
144	    def _write() -> None:
145	        f = open(fifo, "w")  # blocks until the orchestrator opens the read end
146	        f.write(
147	            json.dumps(
148	                {
149	                    "type": "result",
150	                    "total_cost_usd": 0.0,
151	                    "num_turns": 1,
152	                    "session_id": "s",
153	                    "is_error": False,
154	                }
155	            )
156	            + "\n"
157	        )
158	        f.flush()
159	        with open(done_marker, "a") as d:
160	            d.write(identifier + "\n")
161	        f.close()
162	
163	    threading.Thread(target=_write, daemon=True).start()
164	
165	
```

---

# Spec 1 — Extract `watch.py` (tmux/FIFO watch subsystem)

## Problem

`orchestrator.py` carries ~120 lines of tmux-pane + FIFO plumbing that has nothing to do with the cycle loop or Linear lifecycle. It's a self-contained concern — "run this argv in a visible pane and hand me a readable stream of its output" — but it's interleaved into `_drain_one_issue`, which is why that function is ~480 lines. The watch code is also the one place the otherwise-clean worker boundary leaks: the orchestrator calls `worker.build_argv` directly to feed the pane.

## What moves

Out of `orchestrator.py` into a new `drain_cycle/watch.py`:

| Current symbol | Becomes |
|---|---|
| `_WATCH_FIFO_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` | `watch.FIFO_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (public) |
| `_watch_formatter_stage()` | `watch._formatter_stage()` (private) |
| `_open_watch_pane(argv, cwd)` | folded into `watch.open_session` / `watch._open_pane` |
| `_open_fifo_stream(fifo, timeout)` | `watch._open_fifo_stream` (private) |
| `_cleanup_fifo(fifo)` | `watch._cleanup_fifo` (private) |
| `_close_watch_pane(pane_id)` | `watch.close_pane(pane_id)` (public) |

Stays in `orchestrator.py`: the `watch and in_tmux` decision, the `current_pane_id` cross-issue baton, and the fallback-to-subprocess control flow (these are loop-lifecycle concerns, not pane mechanics).

## New module API

```python
# drain_cycle/watch.py

FIFO_TIMEOUT_SECONDS: float = 10.0

@dataclass
class WatchSession:
    """A live tmux pane running the session, plus an open stream of its
    stream-json. The pane *is* the claude process; the orchestrator reads
    the FIFO branch while the operator watches the formatted pane copy."""
    pane_id: str
    stream: TextIO
    fifo_path: Path
    def kill(self) -> None: ...      # close the pane (was kill_fn)
    def cleanup(self) -> None: ...   # remove the FIFO + temp dir

def open_session(
    argv: list[str], cwd: Path, *, timeout: float = FIFO_TIMEOUT_SECONDS
) -> WatchSession | None:
    """Open a pane running ``argv | tee <fifo> | <formatter>`` and return a
    session once the FIFO produces its first bytes. Returns None on *any*
    failure — no tmux, non-zero exit, FIFO timeout — having already torn down
    whatever it partially created. The caller falls back to a normal spawn."""

def close_pane(pane_id: str) -> None:
    """Kill a pane by id; swallows all errors. Used for the cross-issue
    prior-pane teardown, where the pane_id outlives its WatchSession."""
```

The key design move: **`open_session` absorbs the partial-teardown logic** that's currently spread across `_drain_one_issue` (the "pane accepted but FIFO timed out → close pane + cleanup fifo + reset to None" branch at orchestrator.py:630-636). The caller gets a clean binary: a usable `WatchSession` or `None`.

## Caller shape after extraction

The watch block in `_drain_one_issue` (orchestrator.py:612-639) collapses to:

```python
session: watch.WatchSession | None = None
external_stream = kill_fn = None
if watch and in_tmux:
    argv = worker.build_argv(_CLAUDE_CMD, model=worker_model, prompt=agent_prompt,
                             cost_limit_usd=limits.per_issue_cost_usd, debug_file=debug_file)
    session = watch.open_session(argv, worktree_path)
    if session is not None:
        external_stream, kill_fn = session.stream, session.kill

# ... worker.run_issue(..., external_stream=external_stream, kill_fn=kill_fn)
finally:
    progress.clear()
    if session is not None:
        session.cleanup()
```

And the return value: `pane_id = session.pane_id if session else None` — unchanged contract to the caller, which keeps owning `current_pane_id` and the `close_pane` calls in `_run`.

The `worker.build_argv` call stays in the orchestrator (it's the bridge between "what to run" and "where to run it"). That leak is inherent to watch mode and is left as-is; this spec doesn't try to close it.

## Test impact

- **`tests/test_orchestrator_watch.py`** — these drive through `orchestrator.run` and mock `subprocess.run` at the `tmux` level, so they pass unchanged **except** `test_fifo_timeout_falls_back_to_subprocess`, which patches `orchestrator._WATCH_FIFO_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (line 336). That constant moves, so the patch target becomes `watch.FIFO_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`. One-line change. The `"drain_cycle.watch_format" in pipeline` assertions (lines 206-207) still hold — the formatter stage is unchanged, just relocated.
- **New `tests/test_watch.py`** (optional but cheap, and the payoff of the extraction): unit-test `open_session` and `close_pane` directly now that they're a public, narrow surface — the success path, the no-tmux path, the FIFO-timeout teardown path. Currently each is only reachable through a full orchestrator drain.
- `tests/test_watch_format.py` is untouched (it tests `watch_format.py`, a different module).

## Acceptance

1. `watch.py` exists with the API above; the six symbols are gone from `orchestrator.py`.
2. `orchestrator._drain_one_issue` no longer contains FIFO/pane teardown branching — only the `open_session(...) -> session | None` decision.
3. Full suite green with the one patch-target change.
4. `_drain_one_issue` line count drops by ~30; `orchestrator.py` by ~120.

## Risk

Low. Pure move + interface tightening, no behavior change. The partial-teardown consolidation is the only logic that actually relocates, and the FIFO-timeout test exercises exactly that path.

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/watch.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Watch-mode tmux pane + FIFO plumbing for a spawned ``claude`` session.\n\nIn watch mode the pane *is* the ``claude`` session: the orchestrator runs\n``claude ... | tee <fifo> | <formatter>`` in a tmux split-pane and reads the\nsame stream-json bytes off the FIFO that the formatter renders human-readable\nin the pane. ``tee`` sits upstream of the formatter so the FIFO branch is\nbyte-for-byte identical to what the worker's own subprocess pipe would carry.\n\nThis module owns the mechanics — open a pane, wire the FIFO, hand back a\nreadable stream and a way to kill the pane, tear everything down on failure.\nThe orchestrator owns the *policy*: whether to watch at all, the cross-issue\npane baton, and the fall-back-to-subprocess decision when ``open_session``\nreturns ``None``.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport fcntl\nimport os\nimport select\nimport shlex\nimport shutil\nimport subprocess\nimport sys\nimport tempfile\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import TextIO\n\nFIFO_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 10.0\n\"\"\"How long :func:`open_session` waits for the pane's ``claude | tee`` to\nproduce its first bytes before giving up and returning ``None`` (the caller\nthen falls back to a normal subprocess spawn). The pane's ``claude`` emits its\ninit event within seconds; this bound only guards a pane that never started\n(tmux accepted the split-window but the command died), so the drain never\nwedges on a dead FIFO.\"\"\"\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass WatchSession:\n    \"\"\"A live pane running the session plus an open stream of its stream-json.\n\n    The pane owns the ``claude`` process; :attr:`stream` is the FIFO branch the\n    orchestrator reads while the operator watches the formatted pane copy.\n    :meth:`kill` stops the pane (used on a guardrail breach); :meth:`cleanup`\n    removes the FIFO and its temp dir (used unconditionally once the session is\n    done with). The pane itself is left for scrollback unless :meth:`kill` is\n    called — the orchestrator kills it via :func:`close_pane` when the next\n    issue opens its own pane.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    pane_id: str\n    stream: TextIO\n    fifo_path: Path\n\n    def kill(self) -> None:\n        close_pane(self.pane_id)\n\n    def cleanup(self) -> None:\n        _cleanup_fifo(self.fifo_path)\n\n\ndef open_session(\n    argv: list[str], cwd: Path, *, timeout: float = FIFO_TIMEOUT_SECONDS\n) -> WatchSession | None:\n    \"\"\"Open a watch pane running ``argv`` and return a readable session.\n\n    Runs ``argv | tee <fifo> | <formatter>`` in a tmux split-pane (in ``cwd``,\n    the issue's worktree) and waits up to ``timeout`` for the FIFO to produce\n    its first bytes. Returns a :class:`WatchSession` on success, or ``None`` on\n    *any* failure — no tmux, non-zero split-window exit, a pane that never\n    wrote — having already torn down whatever it partially created. The caller\n    treats ``None`` as \"fall back to a normal subprocess spawn.\"\n    \"\"\"\n    opened = _open_pane(argv, cwd)\n    if opened is None:\n        return None\n    pane_id, fifo_path = opened\n    stream = _open_fifo_stream(fifo_path, timeout)\n    if stream is None:\n        # Pane accepted but produced no output in time — tear it down so the\n        # caller doesn't double-spawn, then signal fall-back.\n        close_pane(pane_id)\n        _cleanup_fifo(fifo_path)\n        return None\n    return WatchSession(pane_id=pane_id, stream=stream, fifo_path=fifo_path)\n\n\ndef close_pane(pane_id: str) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Kill a tmux pane by ID; swallows all errors.\n\n    Public because the pane_id outlives its :class:`WatchSession`: the\n    orchestrator kills the prior issue's pane when the next issue opens one.\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        subprocess.run(\n            [\"tmux\", \"kill-pane\", \"-t\", pane_id],\n            check=False,\n            capture_output=True,\n        )\n    except Exception:\n        pass\n\n\ndef _formatter_stage() -> str:\n    \"\"\"The pane-visible pipe stage that renders stream-json human-readable.\n\n    ``<python> -u -m drain_cycle.watch_format`` reads the operator's copy and\n    writes formatted activity; ``-u`` keeps it unbuffered so the pane updates\n    live. ``sys.executable`` is the interpreter drain-cycle itself runs under,\n    so for the editable uv-tool install it is the venv that has ``drain_cycle``\n    importable.\n\n    The whole thing is a brace group with ``|| cat`` so the FIFO branch stays\n    truly independent of the formatter. A bare ``tee <fifo> | formatter`` is\n    *not* independent: if the formatter can't launch or dies, ``tee`` takes\n    SIGPIPE on its stdout and exits, closing the FIFO write end and truncating\n    drain-cycle's parse. The brace-group subshell holds the pipe's read end\n    open across the formatter→``cat`` transition, so ``tee`` never sees a\n    readerless pipe — worst case the pane falls back to raw JSON via ``cat``.\n    \"\"\"\n    py = shlex.quote(sys.executable)\n    return f\"{{ {py} -u -m drain_cycle.watch_format || cat; }}\"\n\n\ndef _open_pane(argv: list[str], cwd: Path) -> tuple[str, Path] | None:\n    \"\"\"Open a tmux split-pane running ``argv`` piped through ``tee`` into a FIFO.\n\n    The pane *is* the ``claude`` session: ``argv | tee <fifo> | <formatter>``\n    splits the stream — the FIFO branch carries byte-for-byte stream-json to\n    drain-cycle's reader, while the pane copy flows through the formatter (see\n    :func:`_formatter_stage`) so the operator sees human-readable activity\n    rather than raw JSON. ``exec ${SHELL}`` after the pipeline keeps the pane\n    alive for scrollback once ``claude`` exits (``tee`` still closes the FIFO\n    write end, so the reader reaches EOF).\n\n    ``split-window -P -F \"#{pane_id}\"`` prints the new pane's ID directly so we\n    capture the session pane, not the operator's active pane; ``-c`` runs it in\n    the issue's worktree. Returns ``(pane_id, fifo_path)``, or ``None`` if the\n    FIFO or pane could not be created — every failure (tmux not on PATH,\n    non-zero exit, any OS error) is swallowed so a broken tmux environment\n    falls back to a normal spawn rather than crashing the drain.\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        fifo_path = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix=\"drain-watch-\")) / \"stream.fifo\"\n        os.mkfifo(fifo_path)\n    except OSError:\n        return None\n    pipeline = (\n        \" \".join(shlex.quote(a) for a in argv)\n        + f\" | tee {shlex.quote(str(fifo_path))}\"\n        + f\" | {_formatter_stage()}\"\n        + \"; exec ${SHELL:-/bin/sh}\"\n    )\n    try:\n        result = subprocess.run(\n            [\n                \"tmux\", \"split-window\", \"-d\",\n                \"-P\", \"-F\", \"#{pane_id}\",\n                \"-c\", str(cwd),\n                pipeline,\n            ],\n            check=True,\n            capture_output=True,\n            text=True,\n        )\n        pane_id = result.stdout.strip()\n    except Exception:\n        _cleanup_fifo(fifo_path)\n        return None\n    if not pane_id:\n        _cleanup_fifo(fifo_path)\n        return None\n    return pane_id, fifo_path\n\n\ndef _open_fifo_stream(fifo_path: Path, timeout: float) -> TextIO | None:\n    \"\"\"Open ``fifo_path`` for reading, waiting up to ``timeout`` for a writer.\n\n    The FIFO is opened non-blocking so a pane that never started can't wedge\n    the drain; ``select`` then waits for the first bytes (``tee`` writing the\n    pane's first stream-json line). Once readable, ``O_NONBLOCK`` is cleared so\n    the reader thread's line iteration blocks normally until EOF. Returns a\n    line-buffered text stream, or ``None`` if no writer/data appeared in time\n    (the caller then tears down the pane and falls back to a normal spawn).\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        fd = os.open(fifo_path, os.O_RDONLY | os.O_NONBLOCK)\n    except OSError:\n        return None\n    readable, _, _ = select.select([fd], [], [], timeout)\n    if not readable:\n        os.close(fd)\n        return None\n    flags = fcntl.fcntl(fd, fcntl.F_GETFL)\n    fcntl.fcntl(fd, fcntl.F_SETFL, flags & ~os.O_NONBLOCK)\n    try:\n        return os.fdopen(fd, \"r\", buffering=1)\n    except OSError:\n        os.close(fd)\n        return None\n\n\ndef _cleanup_fifo(fifo_path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Remove the FIFO and its temp dir; swallows all errors.\"\"\"\n    shutil.rmtree(fifo_path.parent, ignore_errors=True)\n"
}
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/tests/test_watch.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Unit tests for the watch-mode pane/FIFO subsystem.\n\nThese exercise ``watch.open_session`` and ``watch.close_pane`` directly — the\nnarrow public surface the orchestrator drives. The success path uses a fake\ntmux that simulates the pane's ``claude`` by opening the FIFO and writing a\nline, so the FIFO read returns a live stream; the failure paths cover tmux\nabsent, a non-zero split-window, and a pane that never writes (FIFO timeout).\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport shlex\nimport subprocess\nimport threading\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Any\n\nfrom drain_cycle import watch\n\n\ndef _fifo_from_pipeline(pipeline: str) -> str:\n    tokens = shlex.split(pipeline)\n    return tokens[tokens.index(\"tee\") + 1].rstrip(\";\")\n\n\ndef _spawn_fake_pane(fifo: str) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Open the FIFO and write one line, standing in for the pane's claude.\"\"\"\n\n    def _write() -> None:\n        with open(fifo, \"w\") as f:  # blocks until the reader opens its end\n            f.write('{\"type\": \"result\"}\\n')\n            f.flush()\n\n    threading.Thread(target=_write, daemon=True).start()\n\n\ndef _fake_tmux(calls: list[list[str]], *, write_fifo: bool, pane: str = \"%1\") -> Any:\n    real_run = subprocess.run\n\n    def run(args: Any, **kwargs: Any) -> Any:\n        if not (isinstance(args, list) and args and args[0] == \"tmux\"):\n            return real_run(args, **kwargs)\n        calls.append(list(args))\n        if len(args) > 1 and args[1] == \"split-window\":\n            if write_fifo:\n                _spawn_fake_pane(_fifo_from_pipeline(args[-1]))\n            return type(\"R\", (), {\"stdout\": f\"{pane}\\n\", \"returncode\": 0})()\n        return type(\"R\", (), {\"stdout\": \"\", \"returncode\": 0})()\n\n    return run\n\n\ndef test_open_session_returns_live_stream(monkeypatch, tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    calls: list[list[str]] = []\n    monkeypatch.setattr(subprocess, \"run\", _fake_tmux(calls, write_fifo=True))\n\n    session = watch.open_session([\"claude\", \"-p\"], tmp_path)\n\n    assert session is not None\n    assert session.pane_id == \"%1\"\n    assert session.stream.readline() == '{\"type\": \"result\"}\\n'\n    session.stream.close()\n    session.cleanup()\n    # The pane command carried the formatter stage downstream of tee.\n    pipeline = calls[0][-1]\n    assert \"drain_cycle.watch_format\" in pipeline\n    assert pipeline.index(\"| tee \") < pipeline.index(\"drain_cycle.watch_format\")\n\n\ndef test_open_session_none_when_tmux_missing(monkeypatch, tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    def boom(args: Any, **kwargs: Any) -> Any:\n        if isinstance(args, list) and args and args[0] == \"tmux\":\n            raise FileNotFoundError(\"tmux\")\n        raise AssertionError(\"unexpected non-tmux call\")\n\n    monkeypatch.setattr(subprocess, \"run\", boom)\n    assert watch.open_session([\"claude\"], tmp_path) is None\n\n\ndef test_open_session_none_on_fifo_timeout(monkeypatch, tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Pane opens but never writes — the FIFO read times out and the pane is\n    torn down, so the caller falls back to a normal spawn.\"\"\"\n    calls: list[list[str]] = []\n    monkeypatch.setattr(subprocess, \"run\", _fake_tmux(calls, write_fifo=False))\n\n    session = watch.open_session([\"claude\"], tmp_path, timeout=0.2)\n\n    assert session is None\n    # split-window opened it, then kill-pane tore it down.\n    assert any(c[1] == \"split-window\" for c in calls)\n    assert any(c[1] == \"kill-pane\" for c in calls)\n\n\ndef test_close_pane_swallows_errors(monkeypatch) -> None:\n    def boom(args: Any, **kwargs: Any) -> Any:\n        raise FileNotFoundError(\"tmux\")\n\n    monkeypatch.setattr(subprocess, \"run\", boom)\n    watch.close_pane(\"%9\")  # must not raise\n"
}
```

```
63	_WATCH_FIFO_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 10.0
64	"""How long ``_open_fifo_stream`` waits for the pane's ``claude | tee`` to
65	produce its first bytes before giving up and falling back to a normal
66	subprocess spawn. The pane's ``claude`` emits its init event within seconds;
67	this bound only guards a pane that never started (tmux accepted the
68	split-window but the command died), so the drain never wedges on a dead FIFO."""
69	
70	
71	def _watch_formatter_stage() -> str:
72	    """The pane-visible pipe stage that renders stream-json human-readable.
73	
74	    ``<python> -u -m drain_cycle.watch_format`` reads the operator's copy and
75	    writes formatted activity; ``-u`` keeps it unbuffered so the pane updates
76	    live. ``sys.executable`` is the interpreter drain-cycle itself runs under,
77	    so for the editable uv-tool install it is the venv that has ``drain_cycle``
78	    importable.
79	
80	    The whole thing is a brace group with ``|| cat`` so the FIFO branch stays
81	    truly independent of the formatter. A bare ``tee <fifo> | formatter`` is
82	    *not* independent: if the formatter can't launch or dies, ``tee`` takes
83	    SIGPIPE on its stdout and exits, closing the FIFO write end and truncating
84	    drain-cycle's parse. The brace-group subshell holds the pipe's read end
85	    open across the formatter→``cat`` transition, so ``tee`` never sees a
86	    readerless pipe — worst case the pane falls back to raw JSON via ``cat``.
87	    """
88	    py = shlex.quote(sys.executable)
89	    return f"{{ {py} -u -m drain_cycle.watch_format || cat; }}"
90	
91	
92	def _open_watch_pane(argv: list[str], cwd: Path) -> tuple[str, Path] | None:
93	    """Open a tmux split-pane running ``argv`` piped through ``tee`` into a FIFO.
94	
95	    The pane *is* the ``claude`` session: ``argv | tee <fifo> | <formatter>``
96	    splits the stream — the FIFO branch carries byte-for-byte stream-json to
97	    drain-cycle's reader, while the pane copy flows through the formatter (see
98	    ``_watch_formatter_stage``) so the operator sees human-readable activity
99	    rather than raw JSON. ``exec ${SHELL}`` after the pipeline keeps the pane
100	    alive for scrollback once ``claude`` exits (``tee`` still closes the FIFO
101	    write end, so the reader reaches EOF).
102	
103	    ``split-window -P -F "#{pane_id}"`` prints the new pane's ID directly so we
104	    capture the session pane, not the operator's active pane; ``-c`` runs it in
105	    the issue's worktree. Returns ``(pane_id, fifo_path)``, or ``None`` if the
106	    FIFO or pane could not be created — every failure (tmux not on PATH,
107	    non-zero exit, any OS error) is swallowed so a broken tmux environment
108	    falls back to a normal spawn rather than crashing the drain.
109	    """
110	    try:
111	        fifo_path = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="drain-watch-")) / "stream.fifo"
112	        os.mkfifo(fifo_path)
113	    except OSError:
114	        return None
115	    pipeline = (
116	        " ".join(shlex.quote(a) for a in argv)
117	        + f" | tee {shlex.quote(str(fifo_path))}"
118	        + f" | {_watch_formatter_stage()}"
119	        + "; exec ${SHELL:-/bin/sh}"
120	    )
121	    try:
122	        result = subprocess.run(
123	            [
124	                "tmux", "split-window", "-d",
125	                "-P", "-F", "#{pane_id}",
126	                "-c", str(cwd),
127	                pipeline,
128	            ],
129	            check=True,
130	            capture_output=True,
131	            text=True,
132	        )
133	        pane_id = result.stdout.strip()
134	    except Exception:
135	        _cleanup_fifo(fifo_path)
136	        return None
137	    if not pane_id:
138	        _cleanup_fifo(fifo_path)
139	        return None
140	    return pane_id, fifo_path
141	
142	
143	def _open_fifo_stream(fifo_path: Path, timeout: float) -> TextIO | None:
144	    """Open ``fifo_path`` for reading, waiting up to ``timeout`` for a writer.
145	
146	    The FIFO is opened non-blocking so a pane that never started can't wedge
147	    the drain; ``select`` then waits for the first bytes (``tee`` writing the
148	    pane's first stream-json line). Once readable, ``O_NONBLOCK`` is cleared so
149	    the reader thread's line iteration blocks normally until EOF. Returns a
150	    line-buffered text stream, or ``None`` if no writer/data appeared in time
151	    (the caller then tears down the pane and falls back to a normal spawn).
152	    """
153	    try:
154	        fd = os.open(fifo_path, os.O_RDONLY | os.O_NONBLOCK)
155	    except OSError:
156	        return None
157	    readable, _, _ = select.select([fd], [], [], timeout)
158	    if not readable:
159	        os.close(fd)
160	        return None
161	    flags = fcntl.fcntl(fd, fcntl.F_GETFL)
162	    fcntl.fcntl(fd, fcntl.F_SETFL, flags & ~os.O_NONBLOCK)
163	    try:
164	        return os.fdopen(fd, "r", buffering=1)
165	    except OSError:
166	        os.close(fd)
167	        return None
168	
169	
170	def _cleanup_fifo(fifo_path: Path) -> None:
171	    """Remove the FIFO and its temp dir; swallows all errors."""
172	    shutil.rmtree(fifo_path.parent, ignore_errors=True)
173	
174	
175	def _close_watch_pane(pane_id: str) -> None:
176	    """Kill a tmux pane by ID; swallows all errors."""
177	    try:
178	        subprocess.run(
179	            ["tmux", "kill-pane", "-t", pane_id],
180	            check=False,
181	            capture_output=True,
182	        )
183	    except Exception:
184	        pass
185	
186

189

```

```
324	def test_fifo_timeout_falls_back_to_subprocess(
325	    tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch
326	) -> None:
327	    """If the pane opens but never produces output, the FIFO read times out;
328	    the pane is torn down and the drain falls back to a normal spawn."""
329	    repo = tmp_path / "repo"
330	    repo.mkdir()
331	    _init_repo(repo)
332	    monkeypatch.chdir(repo)
333	    monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", str(tmp_path))
334	    monkeypatch.setenv("TMUX", "/tmp/tmux-stub,1234,0")
335	    # Keep the startup window short so the timeout path is quick.
336	    monkeypatch.setattr(orchestrator, "_WATCH_FIFO_TIMEOUT_SECONDS", 0.2)
337	
338	    done_marker = tmp_path / "done.txt"
339	    raw_issues = [_issue("ABA-TO", 1.0)]
340	    _setup_linear_stubs(monkeypatch, raw_issues, done_marker)
341	    fake_claude = _write_done_script(tmp_path, done_marker)
342	    monkeypatch.setattr(orchestrator, "_CLAUDE_CMD", [str(fake_claude)])
343	
344	    tmux_calls: list[list[str]] = []
345	    # done_marker=None → the fake pane never writes, so the FIFO read times out.
346	    monkeypatch.setattr(
347	        subprocess, "run", _make_fake_tmux(tmux_calls, [0], done_marker=None)
348	    )
349	
350	    exit_code = orchestrator.run(repos.Repos(mapping={"test-repo": repo}), watch=True, no_stack=True)
351	    # Fallback spawn ran fake-claude, which marked the issue done.
352	    assert exit_code == 0
353	    assert "ABA-TO" in _completed(done_marker)
354	
355	    split_calls = [c for c in tmux_calls if len(c) > 1 and c[1] == "split-window"]
```

> AGENT

Spec 1 is built and committed in two clean slices. Summary:

## Done — `watch.py` extraction

**Slice 1** (`5a3189d`) — new `drain_cycle/watch.py` (205 lines) with the public API:
- `WatchSession` dataclass (`pane_id`, `stream`, `.kill()`, `.cleanup()`)
- `open_session(argv, cwd, *, timeout)` → `WatchSession | None`, absorbing the partial-teardown logic
- `close_pane(pane_id)` for the cross-issue prior-pane kill
- `FIFO_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` constant
- Plus `tests/test_watch.py` (4 unit tests) covering the success path, no-tmux, FIFO-timeout teardown, and error-swallowing — paths previously only reachable through a full drain.

**Slice 2** (`e6abd70`) — rewired the orchestrator: the watch block in `_drain_one_issue` collapsed from ~35 lines of pane/FIFO branching to a clean `open_session(...) → session | None` decision; dropped six imports (`fcntl`, `select`, `shlex`, `shutil`, `tempfile`, `sys`, `subprocess`) now unused there; one test patch-target updated (`watch.FIFO_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`).

**Result:** `orchestrator.py` dropped from **907 → 766 lines** (−141). Full suite **327 passed** (was 323 + 4 new).

Two notes:
- I had to import the module as `watch_pane` inside the orchestrator — the existing `watch: bool` parameter shadows the module name. Minor, but worth knowing.
- My first slice-2 commit accidentally swept your untracked `docs/delivery-plans/` and `docs/ideas/` WIP into it via `git add -A`. I un-bundled them with a soft reset; they're back to untracked and the refactor commit is now code-only. Nothing was pushed.

Ready for **spec 2** (the `record_halt` / `HaltContext` helper for the six duplicated `append_entry` halt branches) whenever you want — shall I spec it?

```
627	            # worktree. A Done issue in stack mode must have left a non-empty
628	            # ``pr_urls`` — that is the orchestrator's proof submission ran.
629	            # If it's missing, the worker marked Done without opening a PR:
630	            # revert + halt, preserve the worktree for inspection, and do NOT
631	            # extend the baton — the next same-repo issue must not stack onto a
632	            # branch that was never pushed. Push-mode issues have no stack to
633	            # extend and no handoff, so they bypass the gate entirely.
634	            submitted = handoff.read(worktree_path) if stack else None
635	            if stack and submitted is None:
636	                original_state_name = issue["state"]["name"]
637	                effective_state, revert_error = _revert_to_pre_halt_state(
638	                    issue["id"],
639	                    target_state_name=original_state_name,
640	                    pre_revert_state_name=post_spawn_state,
641	                )
642	                halt_reason = (
643	                    f"{_halt_message(identifier, effective_state, worktree_path)}"
644	                    " — marked Done but .drain-handoff.json has no submitted "
645	                    "pr_urls; stack chain halted"
646	                )
647	                if revert_error is not None:
648	                    halt_reason += (
649	                        f" — revert to {original_state_name!r} failed: {revert_error}"
650	                    )
651	                log.append_entry(
652	                    issue_identifier=identifier,
653	                    started_at=started_at,
654	                    finished_at=finished_at,
655	                    exit_code=result.exit_code,
656	                    final_linear_state=effective_state,
657	                    worktree_path=str(worktree_path),
658	                    halt_reason=halt_reason,
659	                    flow=flow_name,
660	                    outcome_verdict=outcome_verdict,
661	                    prep_verdict=prep_verdict,
662	                    responder_runs=responder_runs,
663	                    shape_task_invoked=is_verify,
664	                    **_worker_log_fields(result),
665	                )
666	                issue_span.set_attribute("issue.final_linear_state", effective_state)
667	                telemetry.mark_error(issue_span, "err-stack-no-prs", halt_reason)
668	                console.halt(halt_reason)
669	                return 1, pane_id
670	
671	            if submitted is not None:
672	                # Hand the baton to the next same-repo issue and record the
673	                # submitted PRs as the orchestrator's confirmation line.
674	                baton[target_repo.name] = identifier
675	                issue_span.set_attribute("issue.submitted_pr_count", len(submitted.pr_urls))
676	                pr_list = ", ".join(pr.url for pr in submitted.pr_urls)
677	                console.worker_event(identifier, f"submitted {pr_list}")
678	
679	            issue_span.set_attribute("issue.final_linear_state", post_spawn_state)
680	
681	            remove_error: str | None = None
682	            try:
683	                worktree.merge_entire_sessions(worktree_path, target_repo)
684	                worktree.remove(target_repo, worktree_path)
685	            except RuntimeError as exc:
686	                remove_error = str(exc)
687	                issue_span.set_attribute("worktree.remove_error", remove_error)
688	                console.orch(f"{identifier}: worktree teardown failed: {exc}")
689	            # Append unconditionally for every attempted issue.
690	            log.append_entry(
691	                issue_identifier=identifier,
692	                started_at=started_at,
693	                finished_at=finished_at,
694	                exit_code=result.exit_code,
695	                final_linear_state=post_spawn_state,
696	                worktree_path=str(worktree_path),
697	                halt_reason=remove_error,
698	                flow=flow_name,
699	                outcome_verdict=outcome_verdict,
700	                prep_verdict=prep_verdict,
701	                responder_runs=responder_runs,
702	                shape_task_invoked=is_verify,
703	                **_worker_log_fields(result),
704	            )
705	            if outcome_verdict is not None:
706	                issue_span.set_attribute("issue.outcome_verdict", outcome_verdict["result"])
707	            if prep_verdict is not None:
708	                issue_span.set_attribute("issue.prep_verdict", prep_verdict["result"])
709	            if flow_name is not None:
710	                issue_span.set_attribute("issue.responder_run_count", len(responder_runs))
711	            try:
712	                draft_path = grade_draft.write_draft_from_entry(
713	                    identifier, log.entries[-1]
714	                )
715	                console.worker_event(identifier, f"grade draft → {draft_path}")
716	            except Exception as exc:
717	                console.orch(
718	                    f"{identifier}: grade-draft write failed (non-fatal): {exc}"
719	                )
720	            if remove_error is None:
721	                console.worker_event(identifier, "done; worktree removed")
722	            return None, pane_id
723	
724	        # Not-Done halt: revert to the pre-halt state so a re-run picks
725	        # this issue back up instead of silently skipping it.
726	        original_state_name = issue["state"]["name"]
727	        effective_state, revert_error = _revert_to_pre_halt_state(
728	            issue["id"],
729	            target_state_name=original_state_name,
730	            pre_revert_state_name=post_spawn_state,
731	        )
732	        halt_reason = _halt_message(identifier, effective_state, worktree_path)
733	        tripped = stop_guard.read_tripped(worktree_path)
734	        if tripped is not None:
735	            halt_reason += f" — {stop_guard.TRIPPED_HALT_REASON}: {tripped}"
736	        if revert_error is not None:
737	            halt_reason += (
738	                f" — revert to {original_state_name!r} failed: {revert_error}"
739	            )
740	        # halt_reason carries the same string also printed to stderr below
741	        # so the on-disk and terminal surfaces cannot drift.
742	        log.append_entry(
743	            issue_identifier=identifier,
744	            started_at=started_at,
745	            finished_at=finished_at,
746	            exit_code=result.exit_code,
747	            final_linear_state=effective_state,
748	            worktree_path=str(worktree_path),
749	            halt_reason=halt_reason,
750	            flow=flow_name,
751	            outcome_verdict=outcome_verdict,
752	            prep_verdict=prep_verdict,
753	            responder_runs=responder_runs,
754	            shape_task_invoked=is_verify,
755	            **_worker_log_fields(result),
756	        )
757	        issue_span.set_attribute("issue.final_linear_state", effective_state)
758	        if outcome_verdict is not None:
759	            issue_span.set_attribute("issue.outcome_verdict", outcome_verdict["result"])
760	        if prep_verdict is not None:
761	            issue_span.set_attribute("issue.prep_verdict", prep_verdict["result"])
762	        if flow_name is not None:
763	            issue_span.set_attribute("issue.responder_run_count", len(responder_runs))
764	        telemetry.mark_error(issue_span, "err-issue-not-done", halt_reason)
765	        console.halt(halt_reason)
766	        return 1, pane_id
767
```

Now I have the full picture. Here's the spec.

---

## Spec 2 — `record_halt` / `HaltContext`: collapse the duplicated halt-entry assembly

### Problem

`_drain_one_issue` has **six terminal halt branches** that each hand-assemble the same four-call epilogue: `log.append_entry(...)` → set `issue.final_linear_state` span attr → `telemetry.mark_error(issue_span, slug, halt_reason)` → `console.halt(halt_reason)` → `return <code>, <pane>`. The `append_entry` keyword block alone is 10–14 lines repeated verbatim. The branches:

| # | slug | line | family | extras over base |
|---|------|------|--------|------------------|
| 1 | `err-repo-resolution` | 337 | pre-spawn | — |
| 2 | `err-resume-cap` | 380 | pre-spawn | `issue.resumed=True` |
| 3 | `err-setup-failed` | 435 | pre-spawn | — |
| 4 | `err-per-issue-breach` | 573 | post-spawn | worker fields + verdict attrs |
| 5 | `err-stack-no-prs` | 635 | post-spawn | worker fields |
| 6 | `err-issue-not-done` | 724 | post-spawn | worker fields + verdict attrs |

Two families differ structurally:
- **pre-spawn** (1–3): `exit_code=-1`, no `_worker_log_fields`, no verdicts/responder_runs, `worktree_path` is a planned/placeholder string, returns `1, None`.
- **post-spawn** (4–6): real `result.exit_code` + `**_worker_log_fields(result)`, verdicts + `responder_runs`, returns `1, pane_id`, and emit the three-line verdict span-attr block (`issue.outcome_verdict` / `issue.prep_verdict` / `issue.responder_run_count`) — which **also** appears in the Done success append (690).

### Goal

One helper that owns the append_entry + span-attr + mark_error + console.halt epilogue, parameterised by what genuinely varies (slug, halt_reason, state, worktree_path, and the post-spawn payload). Each branch shrinks to: compute its `halt_reason`, call the helper, `return`.

### Design

A frozen dataclass carrying the per-issue constants assembled once near the top of `_drain_one_issue`, plus a `record_halt` method (or free function taking the context). Recommended shape — a context object, since `log`, `issue_span`, `identifier`, `started_at`, `flow_name`, `is_verify` are all stable for the whole issue:

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class HaltContext:
    log: runlog.RunLog
    issue_span: object              # telemetry span handle
    identifier: str
    started_at: str
    flow_name: str | None
    shape_task_invoked: bool

    def record(
        self,
        *,
        slug: str,
        halt_reason: str,
        final_linear_state: str,
        worktree_path: str,
        exit_code: int,
        worker: worker_mod.WorkerResult | None = None,
        outcome_verdict: dict | None = None,
        prep_verdict: dict | None = None,
        responder_runs: list | None = None,
        extra_span_attrs: dict[str, object] | None = None,
    ) -> None:
        ...
```

`record` internally:
1. Builds the `append_entry` kwargs from the base fields, conditionally splicing `**_worker_log_fields(worker)` and the verdict/responder fields when `worker is not None`.
2. Always sets `issue.final_linear_state`.
3. When `worker is not None`, emits the shared verdict span-attr block (the same three `if … is not None` lines now duplicated 4×) — extract that into a small `_set_verdict_span_attrs(span, outcome_verdict, prep_verdict, flow_name, responder_runs)` helper and **reuse it in the Done success path too** (690-710), so the verdict-attr logic has one home.
4. Applies `extra_span_attrs` (covers `issue.resumed` for branch 2, `issue.exit_code` for post-spawn branches).
5. `telemetry.mark_error(self.issue_span, slug, halt_reason)` then `console.halt(halt_reason)`.

The caller keeps the `return 1, None` / `return 1, pane_id` — the helper does **not** return the exit tuple (keeping control flow visible at the call site; the helper is side-effect-only). Each branch becomes ~6 lines: build `halt_reason`, `ctx.record(...)`, `return`.

### Non-goals / scope discipline

- Do **not** fold the halt_reason *construction* into the helper — each reason string is genuinely branch-specific (the `_halt_message(...)` base plus a tail). Forcing it into the helper would just move a big `if slug == ...` ladder inside. Leave reason-building at the call site.
- Do **not** touch the `_revert_to_pre_halt_state` calls — they stay at the call site (they produce `effective_state` + `revert_error` that feed the reason).
- Do **not** change behaviour, slugs, recorded fields, or the stderr/run-log strings. This is a pure structural refactor; the existing tests must stay green unchanged.
- Do **not** absorb the Done success append into `record` — it's not a halt (no `mark_error`/`console.halt`). Only its verdict-span-attr block is shared, via `_set_verdict_span_attrs`.

### Slices (incremental-implementation discipline)

**Slice 1 — extract `_set_verdict_span_attrs`.** Pure helper; replace the four duplicated 3-line blocks (branches 4, 6, Done append, and verify branch 5 has only `final_linear_state` so it's untouched). Run suite → commit-candidate.

**Slice 2 — introduce `HaltContext` + `record`, route the three pre-spawn branches** (1–3) through it. Smallest family first; proves the API on the no-worker path. Run suite.

**Slice 3 — route the three post-spawn branches** (4–6) through `record`, exercising the `worker`/verdict/`extra_span_attrs` path. Run suite.

Baseline to confirm first: `rtk proxy uv run pytest` should report **327 passed**. Same count must hold after each slice (no test churn — behaviour is unchanged). Expected net: ~80–100 lines out of `orchestrator.py`.

### Verification gate before any commit

**Do not commit any slice until both of these run clean over the changes:**

1. **`/code-review`** — dispatched to a sub-agent (via the Agent tool, `code-reviewer` type) over the working-tree diff. Address every Critical/Required finding before proceeding.
2. **`/simplify`** — run by a sub-agent after the review fixes land, to strip any incidental over-abstraction the refactor introduced (e.g. the `extra_span_attrs` escape hatch may turn out unnecessary if only two callers use it — collapse it if so).

Only once both passes are clean do the slices get committed (one commit per slice, conventional-commit prefix `refactor:`, no `Co-Authored-By`, subject ≤70 chars). Push is a separate explicit decision — not part of this spec.

```
315	    issue: dict,
316	    *,
317	    index: int,
318	    total: int,
319	    repos: Repos,
320	    limits: Limits,
321	    log: runlog.RunLog,

326	    no_stack: bool = False,

328	) -> tuple[int | None, str | None]:
329	    """Drain a single issue end to end inside a ``drain.issue`` span.
330	
331	    Returns ``(halt_code, pane_id)`` where ``halt_code`` is ``None`` when the
332	    issue reached Done (the caller then runs the cycle-wide circuit breaker and
333	    proceeds to the next issue), or an exit code when the run must halt.
334	    ``pane_id`` is the tmux pane ID opened for this issue (or ``None``).
335	    Each halt path is also marked on the span with a static ``exception.slug``.
336	    """
337	    identifier = issue["identifier"]
338	    with telemetry.tracer.start_as_current_span("drain.issue") as issue_span:
339	        issue_span.set_attribute("issue.identifier", identifier)
340	        issue_span.set_attribute("issue.title", issue["title"])
341	        issue_span.set_attribute("issue.index", index + 1)
342	        issue_span.set_attribute("issue.total", total)
343	        console.worker_event(identifier, f"picked: {issue['title']}")
344	
345	        flow_name = flow.resolve(issue)
346	        if flow_name is not None:
347	            issue_span.set_attribute("issue.flow", flow_name)
348	            issue_span.set_attribute("issue.responder_run_count", 0)
349	
350	        started_at = _now_iso()
351	        is_verify = prompt.is_verify_flow(issue)
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/orchestrator.py",
  "old_string": "    if flow_name is not None:\n        issue_span.set_attribute(\"issue.responder_run_count\", len(responder_runs))\n\n\n",
  "new_string": "    if flow_name is not None:\n        issue_span.set_attribute(\"issue.responder_run_count\", len(responder_runs))\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass _HaltContext:\n    \"\"\"Per-issue constants for recording a terminal halt.\n\n    Every halt branch builds its own ``halt_reason`` and any branch-specific\n    span attributes, then funnels through :meth:`record` for the universal\n    epilogue: the run-log entry, the ``final_linear_state`` span attribute, the\n    error mark, and the operator-facing halt line. Pass ``worker_result`` on the\n    post-spawn paths to splice the recorded usage and verdict fields into the\n    entry; the pre-spawn paths leave it ``None``.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    log: runlog.RunLog\n    issue_span: Span\n    identifier: str\n    started_at: str\n    flow_name: str | None\n    shape_task_invoked: bool\n\n    def record(\n        self,\n        *,\n        slug: str,\n        halt_reason: str,\n        final_linear_state: str,\n        worktree_path: str,\n        finished_at: str,\n        exit_code: int,\n        worker_result: worker.WorkerResult | None = None,\n        outcome_verdict: dict | None = None,\n        prep_verdict: dict | None = None,\n        responder_runs: list | None = None,\n    ) -> None:\n        entry_fields: dict[str, object] = {}\n        if worker_result is not None:\n            entry_fields = {\n                \"outcome_verdict\": outcome_verdict,\n                \"prep_verdict\": prep_verdict,\n                \"responder_runs\": responder_runs,\n                **_worker_log_fields(worker_result),\n            }\n        self.log.append_entry(\n            issue_identifier=self.identifier,\n            started_at=self.started_at,\n            finished_at=finished_at,\n            exit_code=exit_code,\n            final_linear_state=final_linear_state,\n            worktree_path=worktree_path,\n            halt_reason=halt_reason,\n            flow=self.flow_name,\n            shape_task_invoked=self.shape_task_invoked,\n            **entry_fields,\n        )\n        self.issue_span.set_attribute(\"issue.final_linear_state\", final_linear_state)\n        telemetry.mark_error(self.issue_span, slug, halt_reason)\n        console.halt(halt_reason)\n\n\n"
}
```

```
## Review Summary

**Verdict:** APPROVE

**Overview:** A pure structural refactor that collapses six duplicated terminal halt epilogues into a `_HaltContext.record(...)` method plus a `_set_verdict_span_attrs(...)` helper. I checked all six branches against the pre-refactor file (`/tmp/orch_old.py`) field-by-field and attribute-by-attribute; behaviour is preserved exactly, including the deliberate asymmetries called out in the constraints.

### Critical Issues
None.

### Required Issues
None. Both hard constraints hold:

**Constraint 1 — no behavioural change (verified per branch):**
- `err-repo-resolution` (orchestrator.py:431) — pre-spawn, `exit_code=-1`, no worker/verdict fields, no verdict span attrs. Matches old.
- `err-resume-cap` (orchestrator.py:469) — `issue.resumed=True` set at orchestrator.py:468 *before* `record`, preserving the attr. Pre-spawn field set. Matches old.
- `err-setup-failed` (orchestrator.py:519) — pre-spawn, `exit_code=-1`. Matches old.
- `err-per-issue-breach` (orchestrator.py:667) — `issue.exit_code` set at orchestrator.py:659 before `record`, verdict attrs set via `_set_verdict_span_attrs` at orchestrator.py:660, post-spawn entry fields passed through. Matches old (old set exit_code at line 607, then the same verdict block).
- `err-stack-no-prs` (orchestrator.py:713) — post-spawn entry fields (worker + verdict) present, but `_set_verdict_span_attrs` deliberately NOT called. This is the intended asymmetry and it matches old exactly (old lines 651-668 had no verdict span attrs). `issue.exit_code` is already set at orchestrator.py:684 on the shared path before the `is_done` block — preserved.
- `err-issue-not-done` (orchestrator.py:806) — verdict attrs via helper, post-spawn entry fields. Matches old.

**Constraint 2 — append_entry field set per branch unchanged:** `record` emits the base field set always; the `worker_result is not None` guard splices `outcome_verdict`/`prep_verdict`/`responder_runs` + `**_worker_log_fields(result)` only on post-spawn branches. Pre-spawn branches pass `worker_result=None` and thus carry none of those fields — identical to old. Verified the Done success append (orchestrator.py:758, unchanged from old) still inlines its own append + helper call and is not routed through `record`, which is correct since it's not a halt.

Span-attribute set ordering differs (verdict attrs now set before `final_linear_state` inside `record`, vs. after in old), but span attributes are an unordered map, so this is not observable. No drift.

### Suggestions

- **orchestrator.py:198-234 — the verdict-attrs split is the right call; keep it, but tighten the seam.** Your prompt asks whether `record` should own the verdict span-attr emission. It should *not* be folded in wholesale: `err-stack-no-prs` writes the verdict *log fields* but intentionally omits the verdict *span attrs*, while every other post-spawn branch writes both. If `record` owned span-attr emission keyed off `worker_result is not None`, you could no longer express that asymmetry without a second flag — which would be strictly more complexity than the current explicit `_set_verdict_span_attrs(...)` call at each site that wants it. The apparent redundancy (passing the same three values to both `_set_verdict_span_attrs` and `record`) is real but shallow: the two consumers want different *projections* of those values (the helper writes `["result"]` to the span; `record` stuffs the raw dicts into the log entry). They're not the same operation on the same data. Current split is correct.

  If you want to reduce the visual redundancy without losing the asymmetry, consider a small `_Verdict` value object (`outcome`, `prep`, `responder_runs`) passed once to `record`, plus a boolean `emit_verdict_span_attrs` that `record` honours — collapsing six call-site arguments into one object and one flag. That removes the double-pass at the cost of one new type. Marginal; only worth it if these branches keep multiplying.

- **orchestrator.py:179-196 — the +52-line growth is the abstraction's weakest point.** The dataclass holds six fields purely to avoid threading them through `record`, and `record` itself has eleven parameters. The net effect is that a reader must now hold two new top-level definitions in their head to follow any single halt branch, where before each branch was self-contained. The dedup is real (the four-line epilogue × six sites), but the win is partly eaten by the keyword-argument boilerplate at each call site, which is nearly as long as the inlined epilogue it replaces. This is a judgement call and I'd lean APPROVE because the *correctness* argument — "the recorded shape can't drift across six sites" — is the genuine payoff and is well stated in the docstrings. Just flagging that line count is honest here: this trades raw brevity for a single funnel, and that's a defensible trade for terminal-state bookkeeping that must stay identical.

- **orchestrator.py:167,210 — bare `list` annotations.** `responder_runs: list` (helper) and `responder_runs: list | None` (record) lose element typing. Pre-existing style elsewhere may already be loose, but if you're touching these signatures, `list[dict]` (or whatever the element type is) would document intent. Cosmetic.

### What's Done Well
- The two docstrings (orchestrator.py:169-170, 181-189) explicitly name the *invariant* being protected ("so the recorded shape can't drift") and the pre-spawn/post-spawn `worker_result` contract, rather than narrating history — exactly what AGENTS.md asks of comments. No ticket/commit/fix-history references anywhere in the diff.
- The intentional `err-stack-no-prs` asymmetry was preserved correctly, which is the single easiest thing to get wrong in a dedup like this. The author clearly traced each branch rather than mechanically applying one template.
- `frozen=True` on the dataclass is the right choice — the per-issue constants genuinely shouldn't mutate after construction.

### Verification Story
- Tests reviewed: No tests are in this diff (`tests/test_worktree.py` is modified in the working tree but is unrelated to this orchestrator refactor and out of scope). For a behaviour-preserving refactor of six terminal halt paths, I'd want confirmation the existing orchestrator/halt tests still pass — recommend running the suite before merge. I could not find evidence in the diff that the halt epilogue (run-log fields, span attrs, slug, halt string) is covered by an existing test; if it is not, that gap predates this change but makes the "no behavioural drift" claim rest entirely on manual inspection.
- Build verified: No. Recommend `python -m compileall drain_cycle/orchestrator.py` and the test suite given the new `dataclass` import and the 11-parameter signature.
- Security checked: Yes — no new input boundaries, no secrets, no injection surface; this is internal control-flow plumbing only.

One follow-up I'd recommend (not a blocker): run the existing test suite and confirm green, since the entire safety case for this refactor is "behaviour is byte-identical." Files relevant to this review: `/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/orchestrator.py`.
```

```
## Report

**Sanity check:** `rtk proxy uv run pytest -q` → 327 passed; `import drain_cycle.orchestrator` clean.

### What I changed (all in `/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/orchestrator.py`)

Applied candidate **2** (bundle) and **3** (tighten `list`), which together also subsumed candidate **4**:

- **`orchestrator.py:14`** — added `field` to the `dataclasses` import.
- **`orchestrator.py:161-171`** — new frozen `_WorkerOutcome` dataclass bundling the four post-spawn values that always travel together: `result`, `outcome_verdict`, `prep_verdict`, `responder_runs: list[dict]` (candidate 3 — the bare `list` now lives here, correctly typed to match the `list[dict]` at the init site).
- **`orchestrator.py:174-190`** — `_set_verdict_span_attrs` now takes `(issue_span, outcome, *, flow_name)` instead of four unpacked verdict kwargs.
- **`orchestrator.py:198-217`** — `record` went from **11 params to 8**: the `worker_result`/`outcome_verdict`/`prep_verdict`/`responder_runs` quartet collapsed into one optional `outcome: _WorkerOutcome | None`. The `if outcome is not None:` gate keeps the exact same log-field-splice semantics the old `worker_result is not None` gate had.
- **`orchestrator.py:~644`** — build `outcome` once, right after `finished_at`, from the trio locals.
- **Four call sites** (breach, stack-no-prs, Done append, not-done) — each two-call epilogue shrank from ~9 lines of repeated kwargs to single `outcome=`/`outcome` args.

Net effect: removed roughly the ~52 lines the refactor added back, while *strengthening* the drift-safety invariant — the recorded shape is now a single immutable object built once, so individual branches physically cannot pass a divergent subset of the trio.

### Invariants preserved
- **err-stack-no-prs asymmetry intact:** that branch still calls `record(..., outcome=outcome)` (writes log fields) and deliberately does **not** call `_set_verdict_span_attrs` (omits span attrs). Bundling left the two calls independent per-branch; only the breach / Done / not-done branches make both calls.
- **No changes** to slugs, run-log field names/values, span attribute keys, or halt strings — only the plumbing carrying them.

### Left alone deliberately
- **Candidate 1 (merge/inline the two helpers):** kept both. They write to two different sinks with a real asymmetry — `record` writes raw verdict dicts into the run-log entry; `_set_verdict_span_attrs` writes the `["result"]` projection to the span and is skipped entirely by err-stack-no-prs. Merging them would have to re-encode that skip as a flag, re-coupling exactly the two outputs the review confirmed must stay separable. No net simplification.

result: Simplified orchestrator.py halt epilogue — bundled the post-spawn verdict trio + worker_result into a frozen `_WorkerOutcome`, cutting `record` from 11 to 8 params and collapsing the repeated call-site kwargs; bare `list` tightened to `list[dict]`; err-stack-no-prs asymmetry and recorded-shape invariant preserved; 327 tests pass.
```
