1. **I grounded it in the real code first** — I grepped every `append_entry`/halt site and read the actual branches *before* writing a word, so the spec referenced concrete line numbers, the real two-family split (pre-spawn vs post-spawn), and the genuine edge case (`err-stack-no-prs` omitting verdict attrs). That's what made it feel precise rather than generic.
2. **The `incremental-implementation` skill was already loaded** from the watch.py work — so "thin verifiable slices, test between each, scope discipline, simplicity-first" was in my working context and bled into how I structured the slices and the non-goals.
3. **Your explicit add-on** — the code-review-then-simplify gate — became a first-class section because you told me to put it there.

So the quality came from *reading the code before spec'ing* + the incremental-implementation framing, not from `delivery-shape`.

That's a useful signal for tuning it. If `delivery-shape` isn't producing specs at this level, the likely gaps are: (a) it may not force a **ground-in-the-actual-code pass** before writing — that's the single biggest lever; (b) it may not demand an explicit **Non-goals / scope-discipline** section; (c) it may not bake in a **slice plan with a green-test baseline + invariant ("behaviour unchanged, same test count")**; and (d) it may not leave a slot for a **verification gate** the user can specify.

1	---
2	name: delivery-shape
3	description: Decomposes a committed initiative (goal + key results) into an ordered, verifiable delivery hierarchy — deliverables → nodes → tasks — that a human reads top-down and a deterministic reader walks bottom-up into a tracker manifest. Use after an initiative is shaped and before any node is picked up to build. Trigger phrases: "turn this initiative into a delivery plan", "decompose this initiative", "break the initiative into deliverables", "plan the delivery for this", "shape the delivery", "what are the deliverables for this initiative".
4	---
5	
6	# Delivery shape
7	
8	## Purpose
9	
10	Take a committed initiative — its goal and key results — and emit the full delivery hierarchy that sits between "we have an initiative" and "we know what to build, in what order, and how we'll know each piece is done."
11	
12	The hierarchy has three layers: deliverables (each serving one KR), nodes (polymorphic units of work — a story, a spike, an ADR, an experiment, …), and tasks (the checklist inside a node). A human reads it top-down from the outcome; a deterministic reader walks it bottom-up into a tracker manifest. Every deliverable traces to a KR, every node names its completion form, and every node carries a uniform five-section body. The skill selects and delegates to the disciplines it points at; it does not re-author them inline.
13	
14	## When to use
15	
16	- A committed initiative exists (goal + 3–5 KRs) and you need the deliverable → node → task hierarchy before work starts.
17	- You are about to write a flat task list from an initiative and want the outcome trace (deliverable ↔ KR) and per-node completion framing baked in instead.
18	
19	## Do not use when
20	
21	- No committed initiative yet — the input is a vague idea or a problem with no goal/KRs. Run initiative-shape first; delivery-shape consumes its output.
22	- A single node is ready to build — you have one issue and need its task breakdown. Use the at-pickup breakdown phase of your build agent or hand the node to your build skill.
23	- Single-issue, bug, or KTLO work — create the issue directly in the ops slot. A bug fix does not need a deliverable hierarchy.
24	
25	## Inputs
26	
27	A committed initiative as text: the goal sentence and 3–5 key results (the six-field shape initiative-shape produces is ideal; goal + KRs as plain text is the minimum). The KRs are the spine every deliverable traces to. If the input has no KRs, stop — there is nothing for deliverables to serve.
28	
29	## Outputs
30	
31	A delivery-plan file-set under `examples/delivery-plans/<initiative-slug>/` (or the path the caller names), laid out per `docs/delivery-shape-contract.md`: a root `README.md` (goal + KRs + tree + hand-count manifest), one `D<n>-<slug>/` directory per deliverable with a `_deliverable.md`, and one `N<nn>-<slug>.md` per node. The file-set walks cleanly under `bin/walk-delivery-plan` and passes `bin/check-plan-framing`.
32	
33	## Workflow
34	
35	### 1. Restate the problem
36	
37	Write one sentence: "This initiative wants [outcome] for [who]." Anchor to the stated outcome, not to "what would make a plausible plan."
38	
39	### 2. Gate: confirm the input is a committed initiative
40	
41	Read the goal and KRs. delivery-shape consumes the committed initiative initiative-shape produces — it does not re-run that skill's goal/KR-shaping gates. If there is no goal sentence, or fewer KRs than the initiative was shaped with, the input is an idea, not an initiative — stop and route to initiative-shape. Do not invent KRs to proceed.
42	
43	### 3. Gate: map deliverables to KRs
44	
45	Group the work into deliverables, each a milestone-class chunk serving **exactly one KR**. Tag every `_deliverable.md` with `serves_kr:`. Two checks must hold: every deliverable serves one KR, and every KR is served by at least one deliverable. A KR with no deliverable is an unplanned outcome; a deliverable serving no KR is output without a bet.
46	
47	### 4. Gate: design-doc-worthy deliverables get a design doc before build
48	
49	For each deliverable, test the triggers: does it break into **more than ~5 nodes**, contain a **one-way-door** decision, touch **shared infrastructure**, or carry **meaningful user / cost / compliance** impact? If any holds, the deliverable's **first** node is a `design-doc` node (`type: design-doc`); the build nodes under it are **blocked by** that node and their task breakdown waits until the design doc is accepted. If none holds, proceed straight to nodes. A smaller architecturally-significant decision that is not design-doc-worthy takes an `adr` node instead. Delegate the design-doc discipline to the design-doc skill; do not re-author its structure inline.
50	
51	### 5. Decompose each deliverable into nodes; select the type
52	
53	A node is polymorphic — not always a story. For each unit of work, select the node `type` from the vocabulary in `docs/delivery-shape-contract.md` (`story`, `spike`, `adr`, `experiment`, `ktlo`, …), which fixes its completion form. Tag every node with `type`, `serves_kr`, and `maps_to`. Do not copy a spike protocol or ADR template into the node — link to the source instead.
54	
55	### 6. Gate: five-section body on every node, with type-appropriate Completion
56	
57	Emit all five body sections — **What / Why / Completion / Assumptions / Key Risks** — on every node, regardless of type. The Cohn story form ("As <role>, I want…, so that…") is the first sentence of `## What` on story nodes. Completion content is type-dependent (story → `Done when:` list; spike → decision + stop condition; experiment → hypothesis + success metric; adr → decision record; design-doc → what the accepted doc covers; ktlo → none). Every Assumptions item carries a `(verified)` or `(to-verify)` tag; `(to-verify)` items block pickup. Every Key Risk carries a mitigation step or a falsifier. Enforced mechanically by `bin/check-plan-framing`.
58	
59	### 7. Break each node into tasks
60	
61	Work through six sub-steps in order. Every task ends up on a single `- [ ]` line in the format shown in the template.
62	
63	**7a. Walking skeleton first, foundational work folded in.** The first task is the walking skeleton: the thinnest slice that runs the node's path end-to-end, marked with a leading `` `skeleton` `` tag. Before writing it, ask: "what toolchain or setup must exist for this skeleton to run?" Fold that answer into the skeleton task's description (a parenthetical is enough); never emit it as a separate setup task *before* the skeleton — that defers the integration discovery the skeleton exists to surface on day one.
64	
65	**7b. Feature slices — each extends one direction and is independently deployable.** After the skeleton, add one task per feature slice. Each extends the skeleton in exactly one direction (one capability, one layer, one error path), is independently testable, and leaves the system deployable on its own. A half-wired slice that needs the next task to compile is a fragment, not a slice. Name each as one observable outcome.
66	
67	**7c. Gate: acceptance criterion on every task, written before ordering.** Write a `Done when:` clause on every task (skeleton included) before sequencing. It must be a verifiable condition, not a description of the work: "Done when: the endpoint returns 200 for a valid request" is a criterion; "Done when: the handler is wired up" is a description. A task whose criterion can't be written in one sentence is not yet well-scoped.
68	
69	**7d. Gate: size check — one sentence, one verifiable outcome.** Review every task against two tests: the description fits in one sentence, and the task has exactly one `Done when:` clause. A description needing more than one sentence holds multiple concerns; more than one independent `Done when:` means multiple tasks. Split any that fail. This is verification granularity, not effort estimation.
70	
71	**7e. Dependency ordering — sequence tasks; flag external blocks inline.** Sequence so each task builds only on completed prior tasks: skeleton first, then feature slices ordered by dependency, not difficulty. For any task depending on a cross-team deliverable, external credential, third-party environment, or shared-infrastructure change outside this node, flag it inline with `⚠ blocks on: <dependency>`.
72	
73	**7f. Model routing — apply the 5-axis rubric and append a `Model:` annotation.** Score the five axes from `references/task-sizing.md` (RC, SC, HS, SR, OR) Low/Med/High and derive the tier and review flag using the reversibility-gated routing rule. Append the result inline; the annotation must begin with `Model:`. When SR = High, also append `companions code-review-and-quality`; when HS = High, also append `companions source-driven-development`. A task with no `Model:` annotation is not complete.
74	
75	### 8. Emit the file-set
76	
77	Write the directory layout from the template: the root `README.md` carrying goal + KRs verbatim, the rendered tree, and the hand-count manifest (milestones / issues / sub-issues); one `D<n>/` per deliverable; one `N<nn>.md` per node. Numeric prefixes give deterministic order; pad node numbers past nine.
78	
79	### 9. Gate: verify the emitted plan
80	
81	Run both gates. `bin/walk-delivery-plan <plan>` must exit 0 — the plan walks deterministically and the derived manifest equals the README oracle (also enforces `serves_kr`, `type`, `maps_to` presence). `bin/check-plan-framing <plan>` must exit 0 — every node carries the five sections, every Assumptions item is tagged, the plan has at least one `` `skeleton` `` task, no node places a setup task before its skeleton, and every task carries a `Done when:` clause and a `Model:` annotation. If either fails, fix the file-set — do not relax the gate.
82	
83	## Artefact template
84	
85	The emitted file-set (paths relative to the plan root):
86	
87	```
88	<initiative-slug>/
89	├── README.md                  goal + KRs verbatim · tree · hand-count manifest table
90	├── D1-<slug>/
91	│   ├── _deliverable.md        layer: deliverable · serves_kr · maps_to: <milestone-class>
92	│   └── N01-<slug>.md          a node (see below)
93	└── …
94	```
95	
96	Every node — regardless of type — uses the same five-section body. Completion content varies by `type`; the heading is required on all of them.
97	
98	```markdown
99	---
100	layer: node
101	id: N01
102	type: <story | spike | adr | experiment | ktlo | design-doc>
103	title: <one line>
104	parent: D1
105	serves_kr: KR<n>
106	maps_to: <issue-class>
107	completion:
108	  form: <completion-criterion form — see vocabulary in contract>
109	---
110	
111	# N01 — <title>
112	
113	## What
114	
115	<For `story`: the grounded story form (Cohn) — "As <role>, I want <capability>,
116	so that <benefit>" — as the first sentence, then clarifying context.>
117	<For other types: what this node investigates, decides, or maintains.>
118	
119	## Why
120	
121	<Per-node rationale beyond `serves_kr`: the bet this node makes, the rejected
122	alternative, what it unblocks. Do not re-state the KR.>
123	
124	## Completion
125	
126	<`story` → `- **Done when:** <verifiable state>` list (≥1 item).>
127	<`spike` → `**Decision:** <question>` + `**Stop condition:** <when to stop>`.>
128	<`adr` → `**Decision:** <accepted>` + context/consequences + ADR reference.>
129	<`design-doc` → prose naming what the accepted design doc covers.>
130	<`experiment` → `**Hypothesis:**` + `**Success metric:**` + falsification condition.>
131	<`ktlo` → `None — roadmap A5 carve-out.`>
132	
133	## Assumptions
134	
135	<`- <assumption> *(verified)*` or `- <assumption> *(to-verify)*` items.
136	`(to-verify)` items block pickup. `*(none)*` is valid for ktlo and simple nodes.>
137	
138	## Key Risks
139	
140	<`- **Risk:** <…>` items, each carrying a `*Mitigation:*` step OR a `*Falsifier:*`
141	(an observable outcome that would confirm it is not actually a risk).
142	`*(none)*` is valid when no risks are identified.>
143	
144	## Tasks
145	- [ ] `skeleton` — <thinnest end-to-end slice; foundational work folded in> · Done when: <verifiable condition> · Model: Balanced · risk reversible · review standard · axes RC·SC·HS·SR·OR = H·M·L·L·L
146	- [ ] <one observable outcome> · Done when: <verifiable condition> · Model: Fast · risk reversible · review standard · axes RC·SC·HS·SR·OR = L·L·L·L·L
147	```
148	
149	The root `README.md` ends with the hand-count manifest the walk-script reproduces:
150	
151	```markdown
152	| Tracker artefact | Source layer | Count |
153	|------------------|--------------|------:|
154	| Milestones | deliverables (`D*`) | **<n>** |
155	| Issues | nodes (`N*`) | **<n>** |
156	| Sub-issues | tasks (`- [ ]` lines) | **<n>** |
157	```
158	
159	## Red flags
160	
161	- A node is missing any of the five body sections, or Completion is absent on a story node.
162	- A task's `Done when:` describes the work performed rather than a verifiable outcome, or is absent.
163	- A task description needs more than one sentence, or carries more than one independent `Done when:` — both require a split.
164	- A feature slice leaves the system non-deployable, or depends on a later task to pass its own criterion — a fragment, not a slice.
165	- A cross-team or external dependency is not flagged inline on the task that blocks on it.
166	- Any task line is missing its `Model:` annotation.
167	- An Assumptions item lacks a `(verified)`/`(to-verify)` tag, or a Key Risk carries neither mitigation nor falsifier.
168	- A deliverable has no `serves_kr`, or serves more than one KR.
169	- A node is missing `type` or `maps_to`.
170	- A node re-states a spike protocol, ADR structure, design-doc structure, or test discipline inline instead of delegating.
171	- A deliverable that meets a design-doc trigger has no `design-doc` node before its build nodes — or a reversible, single-surface deliverable was given one it doesn't need.
172	- A node's fine-grained build tasks were expanded at emission time rather than deferred to pickup.
173	- A setup or scaffolding task precedes the walking-skeleton task in a node.
174	- The plan was declared done without running `bin/walk-delivery-plan` and `bin/check-plan-framing`.
175	- delivery-shape was run on a vague idea with no goal or KRs (should have routed to initiative-shape).
176	
177	## Exit criteria
178	
179	The skill is complete when:
180	
181	1. A file-set exists with a root `README.md`, one `D<n>/` per deliverable, and one `N<nn>.md` per node.
182	2. Every deliverable carries `serves_kr`, and every KR is served by at least one deliverable.
183	3. Every node carries `type`, `serves_kr`, and `maps_to`.
184	4. Every deliverable meeting a trigger has a `design-doc` node as its first node, with build nodes blocked by it; deliverables meeting no trigger have none.
185	5. Every node carries the five body sections, with Completion populated per its `type`, every Assumptions item tagged, and every Key Risk carrying a mitigation or falsifier.
186	6. Each node's first task is the walking skeleton (no preceding setup task, foundational work folded in); remaining tasks are independently testable, deployable slices; every task carries a verifiable `Done when:` and a one-sentence/one-criterion scope; tasks are sequenced by dependency with external blocks flagged inline; every task carries a `Model:` annotation.
187	7. `bin/walk-delivery-plan <plan>` exits 0.
188	8. `bin/check-plan-framing <plan>` exits 0.
189	
190	## Related
191	
192	- initiative-shape: produces the committed initiative (goal + KRs) this skill consumes.
193	- design-doc: the delegate for a design-doc-worthy deliverable, before its build nodes' task breakdown.
194	- `docs/delivery-shape-contract.md` — the plan-artefact contract: layers, cross-reference convention, per-node tags, node-type vocabulary, delegation timing.
195	- `bin/walk-delivery-plan`, `bin/check-plan-framing` — the deterministic reader and framing gate.
196	- `references/task-sizing.md` — the 5-axis model-routing rubric.
197

> AGENT

Reviewed both `skills/delivery-shape/SKILL.md` and `docs/delivery-shape-contract.md`. Here's the honest read.

## The big reframe first

Most of what you liked about that spec **isn't delivery-shape's job** — and that's by design, not a gap. delivery-shape stops at the *node* (a five-section body + a task list), and it deliberately **defers fine task breakdown to pickup** (contract line: "A node's fine-grained build tasks were expanded at emission time rather than deferred to pickup" is a red flag; "Do not use when: a single node is ready to build").

What I wrote was effectively **one `capability` node's body plus its at-pickup task breakdown** — the grounded edge cases, the slice-by-slice plan, the green-baseline. Those are pickup-time / build-skill artefacts (that's where `incremental-implementation` lives). So the lever I floated last turn — "ground it in the actual code first" — correctly does **not** apply here. You were right to flag it.

So the question narrows to: *what's genuinely missing at the WHAT level that would have made my node-equivalent better?* Two real things, both implementation-agnostic:

## Gap 1 — there's no home for **Non-goals / Out of scope**

The five-section body (What / Why / Completion / Assumptions / Key Risks) has nowhere to say *what this node deliberately excludes*. In my spec, the "do NOT touch X / do NOT fold Y into the helper" boundaries were the single thing that kept the work atomic — and they're pure WHAT (a scope boundary, not an implementation detail). Today that content either gets smuggled into `What` or lost, then rediscovered (and violated) at pickup.

**Proposal:** add an optional sixth section `## Non-goals` (or fold a required `Out of scope:` clause into `Completion`). A node that names its exclusions is the cheapest scope-creep guard there is. `check-plan-framing` could leave it optional (not every node needs one) but lint it when present.

## Gap 2 — no node `type` for **behaviour-preserving change** (refactor / tech-debt)

The vocabulary is story / spike / adr / experiment / ktlo, plus the appendix (migration / deprecation / slo / compliance) and `capability`. **None fits a refactor** — the exact work I just did. Its completion form isn't a Cohn "As a maintainer I want…" (awkward), it's not ktlo, and it's not a migration. Its real completion shape is: *"invariant X holds, nothing observable changed, proven by the existing suite at the same count."*

**Proposal:** add a `refactor` (or `change`) type, slotted into the appendix per the "named, not fabricated" rule (ground it when behaviour-preserving restructuring is planned). Completion form:

> `**Invariant:** <what must stay true>` + `**Verified by:** <existing coverage, unchanged>` + `**Out of scope:** <what is deliberately not changed>`

That single type would have carried 80% of my spec's value at the WHAT level — the invariant *is* the spec for a refactor.

## Not a gap — your verification gate is already there

The "code-review then simplify" gate I ran isn't missing — it maps onto the existing `companions` machinery: `SR = High → companions code-review-and-quality`, `HS = High → companions source-driven-development` (step 7f). It fires per-task at pickup rather than as a named node-level gate, which is arguably the right altitude. If anything, the tuning is *surfacing* that this is how a reviewer-gate gets expressed, so an author doesn't hand-roll one.

---

Net: two edits worth making — **`## Non-goals`** and a **`refactor` node type** — both WHAT-level, both respecting that delivery-shape doesn't (and shouldn't) know the implementation. Want me to draft them against `SKILL.md` + `docs/delivery-shape-contract.md` (+ a `check-plan-framing` tweak for the new section)? I'd do it in that repo as proper slices, not here.

Created: **[ABA-392 — Tune delivery-shape: add Non-goals section + refactor node type](https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-392/tune-delivery-shape-add-non-goals-section-refactor-node-type)** (team ABA / Personal, Backlog, Medium).

What's in it, shaped with `/delivery-shape`:

- **Context** — the full reframe we discussed: this is WHAT-level, pickup-time slicing is deliberately out of scope, the two real gaps, and the one **non-gap** (the review→simplify gate is already the `companions` mechanism — flagged so it doesn't get "fixed" twice).
- **Goal + 2 KRs** — one KR per gap.
- **2 deliverables** (each serves exactly one KR) → **2 story nodes**, each with the full five-section body (What / Why / Completion / Assumptions / Key Risks), `(to-verify)` tags on assumptions, and mitigations/falsifiers on risks.
- **8 tasks** — skeleton-first, each with a verifiable `Done when:` and a `Model:` annotation; the `check-plan-framing` lint task carries `companions code-review-and-quality` (SR=High).
- **Hand-count manifest** (2 / 2 / 8) and the verify-gate reminder.

Two notes:
- I left **project and cycle unset** so you can slot it where it belongs — none of the active projects fit (the Shaper authoring projects are closed); say the word and I'll attach it.
- The shape itself surfaced a design question for whoever picks it up: N02's `Out of scope:` (in a refactor's Completion) overlaps the new `## Non-goals` section from N01. I baked the intended discriminator into the ticket (Completion's `Out of scope` = what the *invariant* protects; `## Non-goals` = excluded *work*) as a task, but it's worth a second look at build time.

Separately, recall the drain-cycle halt refactor from earlier is committed locally (`80f400b`) and **not pushed** — flagging since it's unrelated to this ticket and still pending your call.

> AGENT

Good — that's a strong signal. Comparing what I generated against what you kept, you cut:

- **The entire task layer** — all 8 `- [ ]` lines with `Done when:` + `Model:` + axes annotations, and the **walking skeletons**.
- **The KR→deliverable→node scaffolding** — `## Key results`, the `D1/D2 · serves_kr · maps_to` labels, the `N01/N02` node IDs — flattened to plain `## 1.` / `## 2.`
- **The manifest table** and the **verify-before-done** footer.

What you kept is exactly: **Context + Goal + per-item {What / Why / Completion / Assumptions / Key Risks}**. So the five-section body is the gold; the initiative-decomposition machinery (tasks, skeletons, manifest, KR tagging) is overhead when the work is small.

That *is* the tuning you're now asking for: delivery-shape has one output shape, sized for multi-deliverable initiatives, and over-produces on everything smaller. The fix is a **scale gate** — triage the input, drop into a lite tier for single/small work, keep the full hierarchy for genuine initiatives. The walking-skeleton point folds in here too: even in full mode it's meaningless for doc/spec/config nodes (nothing to run end-to-end).

I'll add this as a third item to ABA-392, written in the trimmed style you just demonstrated (no tasks, no skeleton, no annotations):

An explicit exclusion list is pure WHAT and the cheapest scope-creep guard there is.\n* **Gap 2 — no node type for behaviour-preserving change.** Vocabulary is story / spike / adr / experiment / ktlo + appendix (migration / deprecation / slo / compliance) + capability. A **refactor** fits none: its completion form isn't a Cohn story, isn't ktlo, isn't a migration — it's *\"invariant X holds, nothing observable changed, proven by the existing suite at the same count.\"* Authors currently shoehorn it into `story`.\n* **Gap 3 — one-size-fits-all decomposition.** The skill hard-requires a committed initiative (goal + 3–5 KRs) and always emits the full KR→deliverable→node→task hierarchy with walking skeletons and a manifest. For a single task or a 2–3 change tuning job that machinery is pure overhead — the trim applied to *this very ticket* (dropping the task layer, walking skeletons, KR/deliverable/node tagging, and the manifest; keeping only Context + Goal + per-item What/Why/Completion/Assumptions/Key Risks) is the worked example.\n\n---\n\n## Goal\n\nFor plan authors using delivery-shape, make the skill fit the work: express two recurring node shapes natively — **explicit scope boundaries** and **behaviour-preserving change** — and **scale its output to the input**, from a single task up to a full project/design breakdown, instead of forcing initiative ceremony on small work.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Add an optional `## Non-goals` node body section\n\n**What.** As a plan author, I want a node to declare what it deliberately excludes, so that the exclusion survives into pickup instead of being rediscovered and violated. A new optional `## Non-goals` section sits in the node body (bullets of out-of-scope items); absent by default, linted when present.\n\n**Why.** Scope boundaries are the cheapest guard against pickup-time scope creep, and they are pure WHAT. The bet: optional-but-linted beats mandatory (not every node needs one) and beats free-text-in-What (gets lost). Rejected: a mandatory seventh gate — too heavy for simple nodes.\n\n**Completion.**\n\n* **Done when:** the node body template in `SKILL.md` and `docs/delivery-shape-contract.md` documents `## Non-goals` as an optional section with its content rule.\n* **Done when:** `bin/check-plan-framing` passes a node with well-formed Non-goals bullets and fails one whose `## Non-goals` heading is present but empty.\n* **Done when:** SKILL red flags and exit criteria reference the section.\n\n**Assumptions.**\n\n* `bin/check-plan-framing` parses node bodies section-by-section and can add a conditional check without a schema rewrite *(to-verify)*.\n* An optional section does not break `bin/walk-delivery-plan`'s manifest oracle (it counts D/N/tasks, not body sections) *(to-verify)*.\n\n**Key Risks.**\n\n* **Risk:** authors treat optional as \"always add one,\" diluting signal. *Mitigation:* SKILL guidance frames it as \"only when a tempting-but-excluded scope exists.\"\n* **Risk:** lint over-fires on legitimately terse Non-goals. *Falsifier:* a one-bullet Non-goals node passes the gate.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Add a `refactor` (behaviour-preserving change) node type\n\n**What.** As a plan author, I want a `refactor` node type whose completion is expressed as an invariant rather than a Cohn story, so that behaviour-preserving restructuring stops being shoehorned into `story` or `ktlo`. Completion content: `**Invariant:** <what stays true>` + `**Verified by:** <existing coverage, unchanged>` + `**Out of scope:** <what is deliberately not touched>`.\n\n**Why.** Refactors and tech-debt paydown are first-class delivery work whose \"done\" is *the absence of observable change* — a shape none of the current types capture. The invariant *is* the spec for a refactor. Rejected: reusing `story` (the Cohn form is dishonest here) and `ktlo` (carve-out, no completion framing).\n\n**Completion.**\n\n* **Done when:** `docs/delivery-shape-contract.md` node-type vocabulary has a `refactor` row with its completion-criterion form, `## Completion` content, and `delegates_to` target.\n* **Done when:** `SKILL.md` step 5 vocabulary mention and the node-template `type:` enum include `refactor`.\n* **Done when:** the contract records the \"named, not fabricated\" grounding note — `refactor` is grounded \"when behaviour-preserving restructuring is planned.\"\n\n**Assumptions.**\n\n* The contract's appendix-types pattern (migration/deprecation/slo/compliance) is the right home for a \"ground it when applicable\" type *(to-verify)*.\n* A `refactor` completion form needs no new `bin/check-plan-framing` rule beyond the existing \"Completion heading present\" check *(to-verify)*.\n\n**Key Risks.**\n\n* **Risk:** `refactor` overlaps `capability` and authors mis-select. *Mitigation:* contract row states the discriminator — `refactor` preserves behaviour (invariant), `capability` adds it.\n* **Risk:** \"Out of scope\" in Completion duplicates the `## Non-goals` section (item 1). *Mitigation:* contract notes the split — Completion's `Out of scope` names what the invariant protects; `## Non-goals` names excluded *work*.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Scale the skill from single task to full breakdown\n\n**What.** As an author shaping a single task or a handful of changes, I want delivery-shape to triage input scale up front and emit a **lite tier** — Context + Goal + per-item five-section body (What / Why / Completion / Assumptions / Key Risks), with no KR/deliverable/node tagging, no task breakdown, no walking skeleton, no manifest — so small work isn't buried in initiative ceremony. The full KR→deliverable→node→task hierarchy stays for genuine initiatives.\n\n**Why.** The skill has one output shape sized for multi-deliverable initiatives, so it over-produces on small work — this ticket is the evidence (the task layer, skeletons, KR/deliverable tagging, and manifest were all trimmed as noise). A scale gate keeps one skill serving both ends instead of forcing a separate task-shaper. Rejected: a second skill — duplicates the five-section discipline and splits the trigger surface.\n\n**Completion.**\n\n* **Done when:** the skill has an explicit up-front scale gate routing small inputs (a single outcome / no KR-worthy results) to the lite tier and initiatives to the full hierarchy.\n* **Done when:** the lite tier's documented output is Context + Goal + per-item five-section body — no KR/deliverable/node tags, no task lists, no walking skeleton, no manifest.\n* **Done when:** the walking-skeleton mandate is conditional on executable software nodes, not doc/spec/config changes (the skeleton has nothing to run end-to-end there) — in both tiers.\n* **Done when:** `bin/check-plan-framing` and `bin/walk-delivery-plan` have a lite-tier-aware path so the gates don't demand tasks/skeleton/manifest on a lite artefact.\n\n**Assumptions.**\n\n* The five-section body is tier-independent — only the surrounding hierarchy and task layer differ by scale *(to-verify)*.\n* The gates can detect tier from artefact shape (presence of `D*`/`N*` vs flat items) without a separate mode flag *(to-verify)*.\n\n**Key Risks.**\n\n* **Risk:** the scale gate is ambiguous and authors pick the wrong tier. *Mitigation:* a concrete threshold in the skill — lite when there's a single outcome and ≤~3 changes with no KR worth tracking; full otherwise.\n* **Risk:** the two output shapes drift in their five-section discipline. *Falsifier:* both tiers reference one shared body-section definition rather than re-specifying it."
}
```

> AGENT

Updated **[ABA-392](https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-392/tune-delivery-shape-add-non-goals-section-refactor-node-type)** — added **item 3: scale the skill from single task to full breakdown**, plus a Gap 3 in context and a broadened Goal. Written in your trimmed style: Context + Goal + the five-section body per item, no task layer, no skeletons, no manifest.

The scale-down item captures both pieces of your feedback as one design:
- **Lite tier** for single/small work → output is exactly what you kept (Context + Goal + five-section bodies), gated up front by input scale.
- **Walking-skeleton becomes conditional** — only for executable software nodes, never for doc/spec/config — which is the real reason it read as overkill here.
- Both gates (`check-plan-framing`, `walk-delivery-plan`) get a lite-aware path so they don't demand tasks/skeleton/manifest on a small artefact.

One genuine design tension I left explicit in the ticket rather than resolving (it's a pickup-time call): the lite tier drops KRs entirely, so item 3's "Goal" framing has no KR spine to trace to — which is fine for a tuning job but means the lite tier is *not* a strict subset of the full hierarchy, it's a distinct shape. The shared invariant I baked in is "both tiers reference one five-section body definition" so they can't drift.

Worth noting for honesty: the ticket now describes a lite mode using itself as the example, but it's still stored as a flat doc, not validated by the gates — those don't exist yet. That's the work, not a claim it's done.

Still outstanding from earlier, unrelated: the drain-cycle halt refactor (`80f400b`) is committed locally and **not pushed** — your call whether to push.

Rejected: reusing `story` (the Cohn form is dishonest here) and `ktlo` (carve-out, no completion framing).\n\n**Completion.**\n\n* **Done when:** `docs/delivery-shape-contract.md` node-type vocabulary has a `refactor` row with its completion-criterion form, `## Completion` content, and `delegates_to` target.\n* **Done when:** `SKILL.md` step 5 vocabulary mention and the node-template `type:` enum include `refactor`.\n* **Done when:** the contract records the \"named, not fabricated\" grounding note — `refactor` is grounded \"when behaviour-preserving restructuring is planned.\"\n\n**Assumptions.**\n\n* The contract's appendix-types pattern (migration/deprecation/slo/compliance) is the right home for a \"ground it when applicable\" type *(to-verify)*.\n* A `refactor` completion form needs no new `bin/check-plan-framing` rule beyond the existing \"Completion heading present\" check *(to-verify)*.\n\n**Key Risks.**\n\n* **Risk:** `refactor` overlaps `capability` and authors mis-select. *Mitigation:* contract row states the discriminator — `refactor` preserves behaviour (invariant), `capability` adds it.\n* **Risk:** \"Out of scope\" in Completion duplicates the `## Non-goals` section (item 1). *Mitigation:* contract notes the split — Completion's `Out of scope` names what the invariant protects; `## Non-goals` names excluded *work*.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Scale the skill from single task to full breakdown\n\n**What.** As an author shaping a single task or a handful of changes, I want delivery-shape to triage input scale up front and emit a **lite tier** — Context + Goal + per-item five-section body (What / Why / Completion / Assumptions / Key Risks), with no KR/deliverable/node tagging, no task breakdown, no walking skeleton, no manifest — so small work isn't buried in initiative ceremony. The full KR→deliverable→node→task hierarchy stays for genuine initiatives.\n\n**Why.** The skill has one output shape sized for multi-deliverable initiatives, so it over-produces on small work — this ticket is the evidence (the task layer, skeletons, KR/deliverable tagging, and manifest were all trimmed as noise). A scale gate keeps one skill serving both ends instead of forcing a separate task-shaper. Rejected: a second skill — duplicates the five-section discipline and splits the trigger surface.\n\n**Completion.**\n\n* **Done when:** the skill has an explicit up-front scale gate routing small inputs (a single outcome / no KR-worthy results) to the lite tier and initiatives to the full hierarchy.\n* **Done when:** the lite tier's documented output is Context + Goal + per-item five-section body — no KR/deliverable/node tags, no task lists, no walking skeleton, no manifest.\n* **Done when:** the walking-skeleton mandate is conditional on executable software nodes, not doc/spec/config changes (the skeleton has nothing to run end-to-end there) — in both tiers.\n* **Done when:** `bin/check-plan-framing` and `bin/walk-delivery-plan` have a lite-tier-aware path so the gates don't demand tasks/skeleton/manifest on a lite artefact.\n\n**Assumptions.**\n\n* The five-section body is tier-independent — only the surrounding hierarchy and task layer differ by scale *(to-verify)*.\n* The gates can detect tier from artefact shape (presence of `D*`/`N*` vs flat items) without a separate mode flag *(to-verify)*.\n\n**Key Risks.**\n\n* **Risk:** the scale gate is ambiguous and authors pick the wrong tier. *Mitigation:* a concrete threshold in the skill — lite when there's a single outcome and ≤~3 changes with no KR worth tracking; full otherwise.\n* **Risk:** the two output shapes drift in their five-section discipline. *Falsifier:* both tiers reference one shared body-section definition rather than re-specifying it."
}

```
Perfect. Now I have the authoritative documentation. Here's the answer:

## What "goal" refers to in Anthropic's documentation

**Yes, "goal" is a documented Claude Code feature.** It's a slash command (`/goal`) that sets a completion condition and runs Claude in an autonomous loop until that condition is met.

### Official Definition

From the [Keep Claude working toward a goal](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/goal) page:

> The `/goal` command sets a completion condition and Claude keeps working toward it without you prompting each step. After each turn, a small fast model checks whether the condition holds. If not, Claude starts another turn instead of returning control to you. The goal clears automatically once the condition is met.

### How to Write a Well-Formed Goal

Anthropic specifies exact properties a goal should have. From the official docs:

> A condition that holds up across many turns usually has:
>
> * **One measurable end state**: a test result, a build exit code, a file count, an empty queue
> * **A stated check**: how Claude should prove it, such as "`npm test` exits 0" or "`git status` is clean"
> * **Constraints that matter**: anything that must not change on the way there, such as "no other test file is modified"

### Key Guidance

The evaluator judges your condition against what Claude surfaces in the conversation—it doesn't run commands or read files independently. So write the condition as something Claude's output can demonstrate:

> Write the condition as something Claude's own output can demonstrate. "All tests in `test/auth` pass" works because Claude runs the tests and the result lands in the transcript for the evaluator to read.

### Example Goals

From the docs, appropriate uses are:
- Migrating a module to a new API until every call site compiles and tests pass
- Implementing a design doc until all acceptance criteria hold
- Splitting a large file into focused modules until each is under a size budget
- Working through a labeled issue backlog until the queue is empty

### How Evaluation Works

Each turn finishes, the goal condition is sent to a small fast model (defaults to Haiku), which returns a yes-or-no decision. A "no" tells Claude to keep working and includes reasoning for the next turn.

**Source:** [Keep Claude working toward a goal](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/goal) — Official Claude Code Documentation

A **refactor** fits none: its completion form isn't a Cohn story, isn't ktlo, isn't a migration — it's *\"invariant X holds, nothing observable changed, proven by the existing suite at the same count.\"* Authors currently shoehorn it into `story`.\n* **Gap 3 — one-size-fits-all decomposition.** The skill hard-requires a committed initiative (goal + 3–5 KRs) and always emits the full KR→deliverable→node→task hierarchy with walking skeletons and a manifest. For a single task or a 2–3 change tuning job that machinery is pure overhead — the trim applied to *this very ticket* (dropping the task layer, walking skeletons, KR/deliverable/node tagging, and the manifest; keeping only Context + Goal + per-item What/Why/Completion/Assumptions/Key Risks) is the worked example.\n\n---\n\n## Goal\n\nWritten as a `/goal` completion condition — one measurable end state, a stated check Claude's own output can demonstrate in-transcript, and constraints that must hold on the way there.\n\n**Goal is met when all of the following hold, each demonstrated in the transcript:**\n\n* `bin/check-plan-framing` and `bin/walk-delivery-plan` both exit 0 on a **lite** artefact (Context + Goal + per-item five-section body; no tasks, no walking skeleton, no manifest) **and** on a **full** multi-KR hierarchy artefact — all four exit codes shown;\n* a node carrying a `## Non-goals` section and `type: refactor` (with an invariant-based `## Completion`) appears in at least one of those artefacts and passes both gates;\n* a node whose work is doc/spec/config (no executable path) passes `bin/check-plan-framing` with **no** walking-skeleton task present.\n\n**Constraints (must not change on the way there):**\n\n* both tiers reference one shared five-section body definition — it is not duplicated across lite and full;\n* every pre-existing example plan under `examples/delivery-plans/` still passes `bin/walk-delivery-plan` and `bin/check-plan-framing` unchanged.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Add an optional `## Non-goals` node body section\n\n**What.** As a plan author, I want a node to declare what it deliberately excludes, so that the exclusion survives into pickup instead of being rediscovered and violated. A new optional `## Non-goals` section sits in the node body (bullets of out-of-scope items); absent by default, linted when present.\n\n**Why.** Scope boundaries are the cheapest guard against pickup-time scope creep, and they are pure WHAT. The bet: optional-but-linted beats mandatory (not every node needs one) and beats free-text-in-What (gets lost). Rejected: a mandatory seventh gate — too heavy for simple nodes.\n\n**Completion.**\n\n* **Done when:** the node body template in `SKILL.md` and `docs/delivery-shape-contract.md` documents `## Non-goals` as an optional section with its content rule.\n* **Done when:** `bin/check-plan-framing` passes a node with well-formed Non-goals bullets and fails one whose `## Non-goals` heading is present but empty.\n* **Done when:** SKILL red flags and exit criteria reference the section.\n\n**Assumptions.**\n\n* `bin/check-plan-framing` parses node bodies section-by-section and can add a conditional check without a schema rewrite *(to-verify)*.\n* An optional section does not break `bin/walk-delivery-plan`'s manifest oracle (it counts D/N/tasks, not body sections) *(to-verify)*.\n\n**Key Risks.**\n\n* **Risk:** authors treat optional as \"always add one,\" diluting signal. *Mitigation:* SKILL guidance frames it as \"only when a tempting-but-excluded scope exists.\"\n* **Risk:** lint over-fires on legitimately terse Non-goals. *Falsifier:* a one-bullet Non-goals node passes the gate.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Add a `refactor` (behaviour-preserving change) node type\n\n**What.** As a plan author, I want a `refactor` node type whose completion is expressed as an invariant rather than a Cohn story, so that behaviour-preserving restructuring stops being shoehorned into `story` or `ktlo`. Completion content: `**Invariant:** <what stays true>` + `**Verified by:** <existing coverage, unchanged>` + `**Out of scope:** <what is deliberately not touched>`.\n\n**Why.** Refactors and tech-debt paydown are first-class delivery work whose \"done\" is *the absence of observable change* — a shape none of the current types capture. The invariant *is* the spec for a refactor. Rejected: reusing `story` (the Cohn form is dishonest here) and `ktlo` (carve-out, no completion framing).\n\n**Completion.**\n\n* **Done when:** `docs/delivery-shape-contract.md` node-type vocabulary has a `refactor` row with its completion-criterion form, `## Completion` content, and `delegates_to` target.\n* **Done when:** `SKILL.md` step 5 vocabulary mention and the node-template `type:` enum include `refactor`.\n* **Done when:** the contract records the \"named, not fabricated\" grounding note — `refactor` is grounded \"when behaviour-preserving restructuring is planned.\"\n\n**Assumptions.**\n\n* The contract's appendix-types pattern (migration/deprecation/slo/compliance) is the right home for a \"ground it when applicable\" type *(to-verify)*.\n* A `refactor` completion form needs no new `bin/check-plan-framing` rule beyond the existing \"Completion heading present\" check *(to-verify)*.\n\n**Key Risks.**\n\n* **Risk:** `refactor` overlaps `capability` and authors mis-select. *Mitigation:* contract row states the discriminator — `refactor` preserves behaviour (invariant), `capability` adds it.\n* **Risk:** \"Out of scope\" in Completion duplicates the `## Non-goals` section (item 1). *Mitigation:* contract notes the split — Completion's `Out of scope` names what the invariant protects; `## Non-goals` names excluded *work*.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Scale the skill from single task to full breakdown\n\n**What.** As an author shaping a single task or a handful of changes, I want delivery-shape to triage input scale up front and emit a **lite tier** — Context + Goal + per-item five-section body (What / Why / Completion / Assumptions / Key Risks), with no KR/deliverable/node tagging, no task breakdown, no walking skeleton, no manifest — so small work isn't buried in initiative ceremony. The full KR→deliverable→node→task hierarchy stays for genuine initiatives.\n\n**Why.** The skill has one output shape sized for multi-deliverable initiatives, so it over-produces on small work — this ticket is the evidence (the task layer, skeletons, KR/deliverable tagging, and manifest were all trimmed as noise). A scale gate keeps one skill serving both ends instead of forcing a separate task-shaper. Rejected: a second skill — duplicates the five-section discipline and splits the trigger surface.\n\n**Completion.**\n\n* **Done when:** the skill has an explicit up-front scale gate routing small inputs (a single outcome / no KR-worthy results) to the lite tier and initiatives to the full hierarchy.\n* **Done when:** the lite tier's documented output is Context + Goal + per-item five-section body — no KR/deliverable/node tags, no task lists, no walking skeleton, no manifest.\n* **Done when:** the walking-skeleton mandate is conditional on executable software nodes, not doc/spec/config changes (the skeleton has nothing to run end-to-end there) — in both tiers.\n* **Done when:** `bin/check-plan-framing` and `bin/walk-delivery-plan` have a lite-tier-aware path so the gates don't demand tasks/skeleton/manifest on a lite artefact.\n\n**Assumptions.**\n\n* The five-section body is tier-independent — only the surrounding hierarchy and task layer differ by scale *(to-verify)*.\n* The gates can detect tier from artefact shape (presence of `D*`/`N*` vs flat items) without a separate mode flag *(to-verify)*.\n\n**Key Risks.**\n\n* **Risk:** the scale gate is ambiguous and authors pick the wrong tier. *Mitigation:* a concrete threshold in the skill — lite when there's a single outcome and ≤~3 changes with no KR worth tracking; > AGENT

Updated. The Goal is now a proper `/goal` condition per the [docs](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/goal): one measurable end state (both gates exit 0 on a lite *and* a full artefact), a stated check Claude's output demonstrates (the `bin/` exit codes in-transcript), and explicit constraints that must hold (shared body definition; existing example plans still pass). It's now phrased so a fast-model evaluator could judge it from what a worker surfaces.

Also worth noting for whoever builds this: item 3's **lite tier emits a "Goal" field** — that Goal should itself follow the `/goal` shape (condition + check + constraints), not free prose. That's a small refinement to bake into item 3 when it's picked up; I left the items as-is rather than re-expanding them.

> AGENT

Resolved — and the answer is good news. Checking by **content** rather than the shifting refs: `origin/main`'s `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py` contains both `_HaltContext` (2 matches) and `_WorkerOutcome` (4 matches). **The halt refactor is already on the remote.**

What happened: the Entire checkpoint/auto-commit system was concurrently rebasing local `main` onto `origin/main` and syncing while I was probing — that's why the ref snapshots kept shifting and why my initial `git push` got a stale-tracking-ref rejection. By the time it settled, `HEAD = main = origin/main = 1c4107b`, and that commit already includes the refactor. So no push from me was needed; nothing is left dangling.

Final state:
- **ABA-392** — updated with the `/goal`-shaped Goal (measurable end state + in-transcript check + constraints), all three tuning items in your trimmed style.
- **drain-cycle halt refactor** — shipped to `origin/main`; working tree clean except your two pre-existing untracked docs (`docs/delivery-plans/`, `docs/ideas/`), left as-is.

Nothing pending on my end. One caveat worth flagging for the future: because a background process rebases/pushes `main` here, "commit locally and decide about push later" doesn't hold in this repo the way I assumed — the checkpoint system may carry commits to the remote on its own. Good to know for how we sequence work next time.
